# Agent Note: Greeneek v0.3.9 — Master Release Plan (merged from all open plans)

Status: proposed

- **Author:** Kimi (WorkBuddy AI)
- **Date:** 2026-11-01
- **Target release:** `desktop-v0.3.9`
- **Plan type:** implementation blueprint for an executing AI model, with human owner approval gates
- **Supersedes:** [the v0.3.8 CTC fix and release plan](./2026-11-01-v0.3.8-ctc-fix-and-release-plan.md) — every open box in that plan is carried forward here under the same IDs. If `desktop-v0.3.8` ships before this plan starts, its shipped sections drop out; the remaining work is unchanged.
- **Merged sources (20):** the v0.3.8 plan, the v0.3.7 completion plan (repo + vault copies), the v0.3.4–v0.3.6 release ledger, the revised recovery plan, the master v0.3.4→v0.3.6 blueprint (Codex), the owner's vault documents (`Greeneek v0.3.4 Plan`, `new add in -v0.3.4`, `new add in -v0.3.5`, `new add in -v0.3.6`, `v0.3.4 to v0.3.6`, `this cahtgpt opinion`), and the seven open process proposals: discover-package-inventory, human-review-skill-maintenance, remove-packed-session-fixture-migrator, artifact-first-npm-baseline-publication, audience-first-documentation-quality, port-tool-owned-render, api-extractor-reports, architectural-conformance, supply-chain-and-vendor-drift. Section 11 maps every source to the section that absorbed it — nothing is dropped silently.

## Problem

Work for the next release is scattered across twenty planning documents written over five months by five different models. They overlap, contradict each other in places, and each session that reads one inherits only part of the program. v0.3.7 shipped; the v0.3.8 plan carries the CTC conversion and the red-CI fix; six older process proposals (package-inventory discovery, npm publication, docs quality, tool-owned render, conformance gates, supply chain) were never scheduled into any release. This plan is the single hand-off: one vehicle (`desktop-v0.3.9`), one checkbox list, one ledger, one execution protocol.

## Proposal

One vehicle, `desktop-v0.3.9`, carries everything still open: the CI gate, the CTC conversion, the npm baseline, the repository architecture gates, the documentation and render work, the six owner-correction reworks, and the Maestro fleet-orchestration mission pasted into Phase 5c below. Each phase is a checkbox list with an ID so the ledger can cite it without quoting prose; the phases run in the order the Sequence table states, and a phase may start only when the previous phase's gate evidence is recorded.

## Reading the checkboxes (contract for the executing model)

A box is checked **only** when all five are true:

1. The implementation exists in the shipping path.
2. Focused tests cover the behavior and its failure modes.
3. The relevant repository gate passes on the exact commit.
4. User-visible or artifact-level evidence exists where the claim is user-visible.
5. The Execution ledger (end of this file) names the command, result, artifact hash, or file that proves it.

A mock presented as live is not done. A skipped test counted as green is not done. A box whose proof is blocked gets a `BLOCKED:` note naming the missing input — never a tick. Every item carries an ID (`S1-03` style) so the ledger can reference it without quoting prose.

## What is DONE-VERIFIED and must NOT be redone

Trust these; re-verify only if a later change touches them:

- v0.3.7 shipped: packaged-runtime self-containment (staging closure, installed-artifact smokes on both platforms), release workflow (dry-run, checksums, pwsh-only Windows steps, single-pipeline gate), pi-ai 0.87.1, Dependabot lockstep groups, generated-artifact freshness gate, pnpm single pin + Node-24 actions, session-snapshot deadline preservation, brand/secret/lint hook parity, Vercel landing scoping.
- Product: light `#EEEEEE` ramp with the WCAG gate (signal-hue `TODO(theme-contrast)` is a documented owner exception — do not "fix" it), PTC pill retired with the `agent-preset/default-sanitized` fallback, wave engine + `tool-wave` + durable `wave/*` events, Fleet surfaces with honest `lastTool`/`durationMs`/`budgetUsed` and 12.5 Hz coalescing, Maestro G1 (promotion, cross-preset lanes, descriptor v4, worktree lanes, tool guards, serial gated integration, confirmation card, repo-config hash trust, benchmark scaffold), Maestro G2 fleet settings/favorites, Arabic dictionaries + registry flip + logical-CSS gate, Dashboard token/cost projection with honest unknown pricing, product inventory index, Maestro UI copy fix, desktop dev-launch sanitize.
- Owner standing directives (do not reopen): never move or delete an existing `desktop-*` tag; live Greeneek-endpoint model flows stay BLOCKED without `GREENEEK_API_KEY`; Greeneek starts and non-AI features run without any key, each other provider gates only on its own key, and keyless Free Models Router turns are evaluated separately and are not blocked by its absence (nothing invented, borrowed, or mocked-as-live); `mode: 'both'` never a default; free flash-tier subagents do mechanical work while the main session directs engines, concurrency, credentials, git/worktrees, and release workflows, and agent waves never commit or push; `docs/plan-v0.3.4.md` stays the decision record — status lives in ledgers; Western Arabic numerals only; no macOS packaging/signing; no npm auto-publish on `main`.

## Owner corrections to shipped work (2026-11-01, round 2 — these override older "done" claims)

The owner reviewed the shipped product against the three vault notes (`new add in -v0.3.4`, `new add in -v0.3.5`, `new add in -v0.3.6`) and ruled that six surfaces were implemented wrong or not as intended. **These rulings supersede any DONE-VERIFIED row for the same surface** (the Dashboard token projection, the Arabic dictionaries, Maestro G2 fleet settings, the Fleet tab, and the Pixels/Instructor preview scope all stay as engineering base, but their product shape is being reworked below):

1. **Pixels Designer and Instructor must be FULL modes.** No "preview" label, not one flagship flow each. Pixels Designer does real design, drafting, image editing, vector illustration, video editing, color and sound, motion graphics, page layout and publishing, and audio editing/mixing through the Craft apps. Instructor does roadmaps, explanations, tutorials, research, and complete CV creation/review/edit.
2. **ArtCraft integration is an auto-detect gateway.** One smart gateway discovers any installed ArtCraft app (launcher manifest + OS registry) and works with it automatically; per-app hand-written adapters are not the primary path. The capability matrix is generated from each app's live command registry at runtime, cached, and drift-hashed.
3. **Dashboard is a full page.** A first-class destination opened from the sidebar (the Settings-as-page pattern), showing every metric the user needs with real, reconciled numbers.
4. **Arabic text must be fixed.** Translations are wrong, incomplete, or unnatural; every `ar` dictionary gets a full quality review and rewrite, verified by native-level review or a recorded owner waiver.
5. **Maestro must visibly control the other modes.** It dispatches tasks to Standard, Pixels Designer, Instructor, and Army lanes in parallel, and the user watches all agents working in a dedicated, always-available right sidebar panel — not a tab hidden inside the details column. Fleet favorites in Settings (this CLI, this model, this effort) are reusable in one click or one sentence. (Specifics defaulted from the original v0.3.4 vault note after the owner confirmed the surface is wrong without adding new constraints.) you should go and see the [[Projects/Greeneek/what is Maestro]]  to get it right
6. **Army must feel like Kimi's swarm.** One prompt → visible task decomposition → many parallel agents visibly working live in that sidebar.

## Phase 0 — Baseline and green CI (gate for everything else)

No product work starts on red CI. *(Carries v0.3.8 FIX-01 / FIX-02.)* The Permanent engineering principles (below, from the 2026-11-08 amendment) govern this phase's work: CI matrix rows covering both supported platforms are the authority where this host's local behavior (for example `core.symlinks=false`) cannot prove them.

- [x] **S0-01.** Diagnose the `consumers` job's `Expected-output processes` failure from the runner log; compare against the passing per-lane probe; isolate `CI: true` and surrounding steps as candidate differences. **Qoder:** the difference was never the environment — the lane runs four real compositions that no shipped layer fully provides. Three causes read from the runner log and reproduced on this host: a duplicate `agent-team` loader entry, `modelOverrides` on a declared pi-ai route, and `image-offload`'s `newSession` rejecting with `no adapter registered for provider "greeneek-official"` because the base bundle deliberately does not mount the native adapter (`packages/bundle/base/cordis.patch.yml:540-552`) while the fixture only overrode that row.
- [ ] **S0-02.** Fix the root cause. If the lane cannot support the job's environment, correct the job and pin the environment with a workflow policy test. Never delete an assertion to clear a step. **Qoder:** fixes applied (three fixtures insert the rows their scenario owns; pi-ai declares its model); pending the runner's verdict on the pushed SHA.
- [ ] **S0-03.** Bring `ci (22)` back green on the same SHA; confirm `repository` and `windows-shell` hold; re-run the whole workflow once to prove it is not a one-runner coincidence. **Qoder:** all three are green on `35968505` today; re-check on the fix SHA.
- [ ] **S0-04.** Keep `pnpm run test:web:built`, `doc-typecheck:contracts-ready`, and the built-artifact smokes green as CI steps, not just probe results. **Qoder:** they are steps in the `consumers` job and were skipped behind S0-02; they run once that step passes.
- [ ] **S0-05.** Prove the ACP frame nondeterminism is gone by repeated runner runs (the drop rule removed the flake, not hid it).
- [x] **S0-06.** Baseline record: current `main` SHA, green-run IDs, and Node/pnpm versions appended to the Execution ledger before Phase 1 starts. **Qoder:** recorded in the 2026-11-01 Phase 0 row, with OWN-01 (no `desktop-v0.3.8` tag → folded), OWN-02 and OWN-06 absent, and this host's `core.symlinks=false` limit named.
- [x] **S0-07.** Make a bare `- id:` patch entry that names a row no layer carries fail loud at load, instead of being dropped silently. `image-offload` and `greeneek-defaults` both sat red for an unknown length of time on exactly this silent drop, and the repository rule is that misconfiguration fails at the earliest resolvable point. Loader-semantics change: its own slice, its own tests. **Qoder:** implemented, with the premise corrected first — the include does *warn* (`vendor/include/src/index.ts:112`, `patch: entry %C not found`), but the warning goes to a root logger a mounted tree may not configure, and `packages/boot/app-boot/src/index.ts:322-334` documents why the leniency exists: one shared overlay must not have to match every tree. Refusing everywhere would break `$GNK_HOME/cordis.patch.yml` and the shipped `config/examples/` overlays, so strictness became a property of the layer: `checkPatchComposition` refuses shipped bundle layers and the profile's own `cordis.patch.yml` before anything mounts, while a lenient layer's skip keeps the two channels that already carry it (the mounted loader's logger and `--dump-config`'s labelled line) rather than being echoed from the boot path — printing them there broke the headless clean-stderr contract and is the subject of the 2026-11-02 ledger row. `--dump-config` applies the same refusal, because a dump printing a tree the same invocation's boot rejects would mislead. Evidence: `vitest run packages/boot apps/cli/tests/shipped-patch-composition.spec.ts apps/cli/tests/args.spec.ts` → 169 tests / 11 files, with `profile.ts` at 100% on all four coverage metrics; the new apps/cli gate reads the real `packages/bundle` patches in every `PROFILE_TEMPLATES` order and no shipped template trips it; live against a temporary `GNK_HOME` — profile-layer ghost → exit 1 naming the remedy, `--patch` ghost → exit 0 with the labeled stderr line, clean profile still dumps; `tsc -b tsconfig.host.json` exit 0; `test:docs` 12 gates passed.
- [x] **S0-08.** Retire the rows the private Agent Teams layers no longer own. `packages/bundle/base/cordis.patch.yml:373` mounts `agent-team` and `packages/bundle/web-app/cordis.patch.yml:270` mounts `ui-agent-team`, while `packages/bundle/agent-team-profile/cordis.patch.yml` and the `agent-team-web-profile` layer's own `cordis.patch.yml` still `insert` the same ids — so any profile that follows those packages' own READMEs refuses to load with `duplicate loader entry id`. **Qoder:** reproduced through `apps/web/tests/agent-team-panel.e2e.ts` (first `agent-team`, then `ui-agent-team`), then fixed. `agent-team-profile` now inserts only `tool-agent-team` and drops its `@greeneek/gnk-agent-team` dependency; `agent-team-web-profile` had no content left to own — its only row duplicated `web-app`'s — so the package is deleted, with `tsconfig.base.json`, `tsconfig.host.json`, `packages/bundle/README.md`, `packages/client/ui-agent-team/README.md`, the README model-experience allowlist, the Web overlay and its equality test, the 2026-08-06 note's now-false composition claim, the lockfile, and the module, plugin, and config catalogs updated in the same commit. Reproduced on this host and on the runner: `TimeoutError: locator.click: Timeout 30000ms exceeded — waiting for getByRole('button', { name: 'Collapse sidebar', exact: true })`. **Qoder:** resolved as the product gap the owner's directive names, not as a test problem — the tests were reporting a missing feature and were not rewritten. `SidebarOwnerProps` now carries the frame's existing `desktopHost` fact and `SidebarRoot.tsx` renders the fold row only when it is absent, so exactly one control exists per host by rendering rather than by hiding (`display: none` would leave a second focusable, announced toggle in the desktop a11y tree). The button drives the already-injected `toggleSidebar()` — no new state, store, or service method — names itself from the existing `toggle.collapse` / `toggle.open` keys in all three locales, and reports `aria-expanded` from the frame's live `collapsed`; the rail rule centers it in the 56px track. Sidebar ARIA goldens re-record follows in its own commit, per the note's Consequences.
- [x] **S0-11.** Normalise nondeterministic stream timing out of the Web session fixtures, so a golden refresh produces a reviewable diff. The second `recore-web-goldens` run (41476176383, on the SHA that added the Web fold control) reported 16 changed files, and only two carried a product change: the `lifecycle-chrome` ARIA goldens that gained `- button "Collapse sidebar" [expanded]`. The other fourteen were `snapshots/web/*/session.jsonl` whose `createdAt` and per-chunk `data.dt` delta arrays moved (`[0,0,1,0,…]` → `[0,0,0,1,…]`) because they record wall-clock arrival gaps. `apps/web/tests/scaffold.ts:841` already proves the pattern: normalise at write, not at compare. **Qoder:** the churn had one cause — the recorder and the comparator disagreed about which fields mean anything. `normalizeSessionLog` already zeroed `createdAt`, `time0`, `data.dt`, event `time`, `hook/result.durationMs` and `goal/change` timestamps before comparing, and the seeder already rebuilds event instants from order when the header reads `createdAt: 0`, yet `scrubSessionSnapshot` passed the header through "byte-identical" and never touched the chunk gaps. That rule is now one function (`zeroRecordClocks`) called by both ends. Evidence: `vitest run packages/test-support/session-snapshot` → 274 passed / 1 skipped with 100% coverage on all four metrics of the package `src`; a real `GNK_SNAPSHOT=refresh` of `steering` now moves **one line** (`createdAt → 0`) and no `dt` array, where it previously moved many; the new specs assert scrub-of-scrub idempotence, which is the property that keeps later refreshes empty.
- [x] **S0-12.** Decide whether the Settings shell card should expose the timeout the mounted composition actually enforces. `apps/web/tests/plugin-config.e2e.ts` asserted a composed default of `60000`, but the card edits the `shell` settings namespace, which the local providers install from their own schema (`packages/shell/bash-local/src/index.ts:129`, `timeoutMs` defaulting to `120_000` — unchanged since `6df25d95`, so no regression produced it). `packages/bundle/base/cordis.patch.yml:236-240` separately sets `timeoutMs: 60000` on `bash-sandbox`, which this row first read as installing no settings namespace and which is `disabled` on `win32`. On that reading the shipped Linux composition showed the user 120000 while the sandbox row capped commands at 60000, and Windows agreed at 120000 because the sandbox is off. Either base configures the provider that publishes `shell`, or the card reports the effective cap — the choice changes documented runtime behaviour, so it is recorded rather than guessed. The test's three stale `60000` literals now name the value the card actually produces, with its source inline. **Decided and closed 2026-11-06: the card reports the value the mounted provider enforces, because that provider is the namespace's only source.** `packages/shell/bash-sandbox/src/index.ts:44` extends `LocalBashExecutor` and inherits its `Config` verbatim, and the base row's `timeoutMs: 60000` therefore arrives as the composition entry that `packages/shell/bash-local/src/index.ts:129` installs as the `shell` section — on Linux 60000 is both what the card shows and what bounds a command; on Windows `bash-sandbox` is disabled and `packages/shell/pwsh-local/src/index.ts:133` publishes 120000. The cap the earlier row mistook for a competing source is `maxTimeoutMs`, default 600000, and it is not editable here. `apps/web/tests/plugin-config.e2e.ts` now reads the composed value from the Host through `scaffold.ctx.settings.get('shell')` in the staged-write scenario, before any user layer exists, and the discard and reset scenarios restore to that reading; the `12000` and `7000` overrides, the badge, the invalid-input refusal and the document checks are unchanged. Evidence: `CI=true GNK_SNAPSHOT=replay apps/web/tests/plugin-config.e2e.ts` on this Windows host → 7 passed with the 120000 path; the runner's 60000 path is now derived rather than asserted as a literal, pending its verdict on the pushed SHA.
- [x] **S0-13.** `captureStableAria` timed out capturing the Settings region. **Qoder:** root cause is a stale capture selector, not a snapshot-serialization problem, and the tests were not rewritten around it. `5dde9feb` ("feat(web): make settings a full page and refine desktop ui", 2026-10-28) replaced the modal shell — `<div role="dialog" aria-modal="true">` plus a mask — with `<main className={css.panel} aria-labelledby={titleId}>`, and migrated the `getByRole('dialog', { name: '设置' })` queries in eight scenario files while leaving two `captureStableAria(page, '[role="dialog"]', ...)` call sites behind: `apps/web/tests/plugin-config.e2e.ts:89` and `apps/web/tests/onboarding-usable-provider.e2e.ts:70`. Playwright's message — `waiting for locator('[role="dialog"]').first()` — is what a zero-match locator says, and the live app confirms it: with Settings open the page holds one `<main>` and **no** `[role="dialog"]` node at all. Both sites now capture `main:not([hidden])`, the region `models-settings.e2e.ts` already pinned, so the lane keeps asserting the whole Settings region while targeting the element the product actually renders. `CI=true GNK_SNAPSHOT=replay apps/web/tests/plugin-config.e2e.ts` → 6 passed, 1 failed, and that one failure is the golden compare itself: `- dialog "设置"` → `+ main "设置"`, `navigation` gaining its accessible name, and a `Fleet 设置` section the golden predates. The goldens are re-recorded from the ubuntu lane, not from this host, because the card inventory depends on platform-gated rows (`bash-sandbox` is disabled on `win32`). Stale prose in `SettingsRoot.tsx` that still called the panel a modal is corrected with it.
### Browser-lane failure classification (run 41522504396 on `7cf11d8f`: 26 failed tests across 14 files, 283 passed)

Every runner failure is assigned one category from the owner's list, with the message it produced. Nothing here was re-recorded or skipped to buy green.

**Actual product defects — fix in the product, first.**

- `preview-boot` — `packVfsImage` refuses the packed worker deployment: `node_modules/@greeneek/gnk-craft-local/lib/index.js` requests `node:string_decoder` and "no static module is registered for it" for webworker modules (`packages/experimental/webworker-packer/src/pack.ts:442`). An undeclared dependency reaches the worker image, so the failure is the pack's contract working and the dependency being wrong.
- `plan-control-row` — at the 800×720 viewport the captured fact reads `Click areas disjoint: false` where the pin says `true`: the plan chip and the model trigger overlap, so one tap can hit the wrong control.
- `chat-scroll-contract` — `flow row call:chat-scroll-live-tool-call moved relative to the transcript viewport: expected 19 to be less than or equal to 2` (`apps/web/tests/chat-scroll-contract.e2e.ts:409`): scroll ownership leaks while a live tool row streams.
- `onboarding-greeneek-config` (two of three) — after `keyInput.fill(secret)` and 保存并继续, the credential step "resolved to visible" and never detaches, so an inline key write does not dismiss its step; and `never paints the takeover chrome on a configured reload` collected 81 alternating `chrome`/`inert` entries where the scenario asserts none, i.e. the onboarding coordinator re-enters its deciding loop on every configured load.

**Legitimate snapshot drift — source named, re-recorded from the ubuntu lane.**

- `plugin-config` (1 test) and `onboarding-usable-provider` (1 test) — the `5dde9feb` page conversion above, and the `github-ready-review` header gaining the `Agent Team` control (`ui-agent-team`, mounted by `gnk-web-app`, already attributed to the earlier 96-file refresh). The `replay-round-trip` prompt pin says `You are an AI agent powered by Greeneek Harness.` while the shipped prompt emits `You are an AI agent powered by Greeneek.` — the same persona change already recorded for `cordis-tool-round`.
- `github-ready-review` — the session-title button inside `navigation "Session hierarchy"` loses its `[disabled]` state. **Attributed 2026-11-06:** `5d9e76fe` made the current title the return-to-Chat control, so an enabled title is the intended product, not a defect; the same refresh carries the `Agent Team` button (`ui-agent-team`, mounted by `gnk-web-app`) and the `CTC` chip label the S1 rename shipped. Both goldens were refreshed and replayed twice on this host with no platform-gated content in the capture.
- `plugin-config` (3 further tests: stages an edit, drops a staged edit, clears the field) — no assertion message of their own on the runner; they follow the one 30-second `ariaSnapshot` stall in a file that shares one page across scenarios, so they are recorded as cascades and re-checked against the runner rather than assumed fixed.
- `pwa-manifest` — the manifest now lists `/favicon.png` at `1347x1168` where the pin expects `/favicon.svg` with `sizes: any`. Drift only once the icon generator's intended output is confirmed; recording it as drift without that check would bless an unintended install icon.

**Test infrastructure issues — fix the scenario, not the product.**

- `github-ready-review` — `strict mode violation: getByText('Review greeneek-harness/greeneek-harness#314', { exact: true }) resolved to 2 elements`, one inside the Sessions tree and one in a button outside it. The product rendering the title twice is legitimate, so the click is now scoped to `getByRole('tree', { name: 'Sessions' })`, the region the same test captures two lines later.
- `trajectory-image-display` — `Found multiple elements with the role "tabpanel"`: Chat's details column and the Trajectory details panel are mounted together. The query now targets `#trajectory-detail-panel`, the id `TrajectoryTable.tsx:2805` declares, so the assertion still reads the panel it means.
- `shipped-composition` — `LlmError: no adapter registered for provider "greeneek-official"` at `apps/web/tests/shipped-composition.e2e.ts:87`: the scenario asserts the native adapter is mounted, which `packages/bundle/base/cordis.patch.yml:540-552` deliberately does not do. Same root cause class as the `image-offload` fixture fixed in S0-01, so the scenario inserts the row it owns.
- `smoke-real` (3 tests) — `session/model-unavailable: no model is configured; add a provider API key in Settings → Models, then select a model`. The dev app served real turns with no `GREENEEK_API_KEY` at all, so this is the isolated harness home not resolving the keyless free router rather than a missing credential; the harness composition is the thing to correct.
- `ptc-round` (3 tests) — `waiting for locator('[data-subcalls]').first() to be visible`: the PTC parent row renders no sub-call container. Classified only after Phase 1 decides the PTC surface, because re-recording around it would bless whatever CTC conversion leaves behind.

**Platform-specific behaviour.**

- `cordis-tool-round`'s system-prompt pin carries a Windows-only sentence about exit-code-1 termination, so one fixture cannot be true on both hosts; the ubuntu lane is its authority (recorded earlier, unchanged).
- `web-search-round` (3 tests) — messages not yet read from this run; no disposition claimed.

**Nondeterministic recording behaviour.**

- `normalizeAria` split `workspaceCwd` on `/` only, so on Windows a capture kept the random workspace basename literal (`gnk-web-e2e-ws-crHK0C`) where the golden holds `{{workspace}}`. Re-recording from this host would have baked a per-run random string into the golden and made the next run fail; fixed at the normalizer (`workspaceCwd.split(/[\\/]/).pop()`) so both hosts collapse to the token.

## Phase 1 — CTC conversion (carries v0.3.8 CTC-01…CTC-04)

Protocol change lands before copy change (two commits) so a regression cannot hide behind a rename.

### CTC-02 first: reversible presentation

- [x] **S1-01.** Record the model: the user may change their own declaration; composition-time (preset) declarations stay locked. This must hold for both. **Qoder:** [the reversible-presentation Agent Note](../../implemented/feature/2026-11-01-ctc-reversible-presentation.md) owns the decision and its rejected alternatives, and cross-links the 2026-08-05 note it narrows.
- [x] **S1-02.** Toggle path goes dispose-then-declare through the exact disposer `presentAs` returns (`packages/api/session-controller/src/commands.ts`), never a second `presentAs` on the same live layer. **Qoder:** `presentation.ts` keeps a `WeakMap` from the agent's own context to that disposer; `agent.ts` registers the resumed re-declaration so a re-adopted Session is withdrawable too.
- [x] **S1-03.** The remote accepts a desired state (`enabled` or explicit mode) and returns the active presentation. **Qoder:** `SessionPresentCodeToolsRequest.enabled` (absent means `true`) → `SessionPresentCodeToolsValue.presentAs: 'both' | 'native'`; repeating the state already in force returns it and logs nothing.
- [x] **S1-04.** Append one `tool/presentation` session event **per change**; extend the replay projection to apply declarations in order instead of keeping the first. **Qoder:** the existing `tool/present-as` event is appended once per accepted toggle in both directions, and its fold already returns the latest `to`; `session-present-code-tools.host.spec.ts` asserts the `both, native, both` sequence and the matching projected value.
- [x] **S1-05.** Request-boundary safety: a turn's tool list comes from the same watermark as the prompt, never from a later toggle. **Qoder:** withdrawal requires an idle Agent, because `native` removes `run_code` from the scope's dispatch table and would deny a call the running turn was already offered; the refusal is the typed `tool/presentation-busy`. Enabling stays available mid-turn because an added presentation cannot strand a planned call. Deferring a withdrawal to the request boundary is recorded as the rejected alternative.
- [x] **S1-06.** Update the TypeScript SDK and Python SDK expected outputs in the same change (neither is covered by `pnpm run test`). **Qoder:** neither projects this surface — `git grep presentCodeTools` over `python/`, `snapshots/`, and `packages/sdk` returns nothing; the only documentation mention is the unchanged `@Remote('presentCodeTools')` signature line in `docs/subsystems/session.md`.
- [x] **S1-07.** Replace the "locked" refusal copy; keep the typed lock only for declarations that are not the user's own. **Qoder:** `.locked` is reachable only from a composition-declared scope; a mid-turn withdrawal gets `.busy`, a missing runtime keeps `.runtimeRequired`.
- [ ] **S1-08.** Tests: enable→disable→enable in one session; tool set and system-prompt sections follow each state; preset composition-time declaration still refuses a user re-declare; cancellation mid-toggle keeps the previous state; a replayed session reproduces every state. **Qoder:** covered are the cycle, the idempotent repeat, the never-declared withdrawal, the mid-turn refusal, both undo paths when the log write fails (an `Error` and a non-`Error` throw), the composition-lock refusal, the missing-runtime refusal, and the silent re-declaration on re-adopt. Still open: a registry-level assertion that each state carries the `run_code` schema and the SDK prompt section against a *toggled* session, and a recorded-session replay of a toggling session.

### CTC-01: rename the opt-in to CTC

- [x] **S1-09.** Name the capability **CTC** ("code tools called from a program", expanded once where a reader needs it). **Qoder:** the chip reads CTC in zh/en/ar and the menu's first footer line states the mechanism in each language, beside the measured cost.
- [x] **S1-10.** Replace every user-facing string in `packages/client/ui-conversation/src/client/locales.ts` (`input.codeTools.*`) across zh/en/ar, keeping the byte-cost caveat figures intact. **Qoder:** `.about`, `.busy`, `.caveat` (33,133 bytes / +102.8%), `.locked`, `.off`, `.offAction`, `.on`, `.runtimeRequired` and the label are all CTC-named; `verify-client-ui-i18n` passes over 523 Client UI sources.
- [x] **S1-11.** Rename the component and identifiers in `CodeToolsSelect.tsx` and the `codeToolsSelect` seat in `InputBar.tsx`; code, aria labels, and CSS class names agree with the copy. **Done 2026-11-06:** the composer component and its CSS module are `CtcSelect`, its props interface is `CtcSelectProps`, and the bar's local seat is `ctcSelect`; the locale namespace keys stay `input.codeTools.*` because S1-10 pinned their copy and key parity, and the CSS classes are already mechanism-neutral (`trigger`, `chevron`, `triggerLabel`).
- [x] **S1-12.** Rename the host vocabulary in `packages/api/session-controller` (`presentCodeTools` remote + `SessionPresentCodeTools*` types); regenerate `docs/tool-catalog.md` and the API catalog; update every README naming the old label. **Done 2026-11-06:** the remote is `presentCtc`, the types are `SessionPresentCtcRequest`/`SessionPresentCtcValue`, and the client contract is `PresentCtcResult`; the host spec file is `session-present-ctc.host.spec.ts`. `gen-cordis-catalog` and `gen-cordis-api` report 53 artifacts current, `verify-cordis-api` passes, and the affected lanes run 821 tests across 65 files.
- [x] **S1-13.** Re-record only the browser goldens whose text the label change moves; every unrelated golden stays byte-identical. **Qoder:** four `snapshots/web/lifecycle-chrome/*.expected.md` lines changed from `button "Code tools"` to `button "CTC"`; the suite replays 8/8 against the rebuilt client bundle.
- [x] **S1-14.** Add a `verify-client-ui-i18n`-visible check that the three locale dictionaries agree on the new key set. **Qoder:** the existing typed parity contract already enforces it and passes; no new gate was needed.

### CTC-03: carry the useful PTC tool set

- [x] **S1-15.** Read `the retired PTC preset's agent.cordis.yml` and list, tool by tool, what PTC mounts that a plain session does not. **Qoder:** it mounts nothing extra. Its row set is `standard`'s 30 rows plus exactly one — `tool-presentation` with `mode: ptc` — and it *removes* capability by setting `disabled: true` on `tool-workflow`. PTC is therefore the standard composition with one tool withheld and the presentation collapsed to `run_code`.
- [x] **S1-16.** Enabling CTC mounts that same surface without losing ordinary tool calls. **Qoder:** proven, not assumed — under `both` the assembled request carries the session's entire capability catalog, and that catalog already includes `run_code`, so CTC is a strict superset of what the `ptc` preset sends the model.

## Phase 2 — Release engineering: artifact-first npm baseline + cleanup

*(Merges artifact-first-npm-baseline-publication, remove-packed-session-fixture-migrator, and recovery-plan Lock 2.)*

### Artifact-first npm publication

- [ ] **S2-01.** Finish the pack phase to spec: discover targets from `packages/*/*/package.json` + `apps/*/package.json` at one fixed commit; reject duplicate names, mixed base versions, unexpected privacy state, unknown bundle packages.
- [ ] **S2-02.** Version = `<base>-<YYYYMMDDHHmmss>-<short10>` from a UTC timestamp captured at command start; dist-tag `dev-<base>`; retrying one bundle retains its version, repacking creates a new one.
- [ ] **S2-03.** Isolated detached-worktree staging: frozen lockfile install, source-manifest constraints, staged manifests with exact internal pins across `dependencies`/`devDependencies`/`optionalDependencies`/`peerDependencies`, `private` markers removed.
- [ ] **S2-04.** Static + tarball-content gates both reject published `src/**` and `**/*.d.ts.map`; source manifests keep `exports["./src/*"]` (source-plane only, checked separately).
- [ ] **S2-05.** Tarball inspection: manifest, file inventory, internal versions, name, version; reject missing/duplicate/extra tarballs; no `workspace:` specifiers anywhere.
- [ ] **S2-06.** Release manifest + SHA-256 checksums (commit, version, tag, registry, per-tarball integrity); publish consumes only this bundle, never workspace directories, never rebuilds.
- [ ] **S2-07.** Artifact-plane integration test: fresh temp project outside the monorepo, plain Node, package-manager `node_modules`, installs from the local `.tgz` files; runs `gnk --version`, `gnk --dump-default-config`, and one keyless TUI startup in a PTY to a ready signal; every other published `bin` gets a package-owned smoke; critical modules resolve to real paths inside the temp consumer. Deleting a required dynamic chunk fails the test deterministically.
- [ ] **S2-08.** Publish recovery: idempotent resume (skip on matching integrity, fail on conflicting integrity), registry + `npm ping` + `npm whoami` preflight, deterministic upload order, final whole-set integrity and dist-tag verification.
- [ ] **S2-09.** `.github/workflows/release-npm.yml`: credential-free pack-and-test job (may run on PRs) + protected publish job (`workflow_dispatch`, GitHub Environment, token only there) that downloads the same workflow artifact and revalidates before publishing. Never auto-publish on `main`.
- [ ] **S2-10.** Optional `dry_run` input on `release-desktop.yml` exercised once on a throwaway tag so the desktop pipeline's gate/publish path is no longer configuration-only.

### Packed-session migrator removal

Retain the canonicalizer, its unit tests, and the snapshot check. **Done 2026-11-06:** the CLI and root command are gone, `docs/testing.md` and the package test-support README name only the canonical packed default and the layout check, and the spec's diagnostic is command-independent. Live inventory before removal: `gh pr list --state open` returned four Dependabot PRs (`#100`–`#103`), none touching session-format JSONL.

## Phase 3 — Repository architecture gates

*(Merges discover-package-inventory, architectural-conformance, supply-chain-and-vendor-drift, api-extractor-reports.)*

- [ ] **S3-01.** Generated project references: a generator emits the aggregate configs' (`tsconfig.host.json`, `tsconfig.client.json`) `references` from the `packages/<group>/<pkg>` hierarchy; a `--check` mode in `hygiene` fails on a stale committed copy. Adding a package then requires zero hand-maintained gate lists. `doc-sync` owns and prints its own sub-gate list; docs link to the command instead of restating inventories. Keep it boring: read manifests, filter on explicit fields, print the resolved list, fail loud.
- [ ] **S3-02.** dependency-cruiser in CI with pattern-based (`gnk-*`) rules: nothing outside agent-loop's own tests/examples imports `@greeneek/gnk-agent-loop`; no cross-package deep imports into `src/`; no import cycles in `packages/`; `vendor/` never imports from `packages/`; the layering table in `packages/README.md` enforced.
- [ ] **S3-03.** Adapter conformance kit at `@greeneek/gnk-llm/conformance`: a reusable vitest suite parameterized by an adapter factory asserting the chunk protocol (index monotonicity per block, no deltas after `block-end`, exactly one `finish`, usage at most once, every `tool-call-delta` carries the call id, prompt abort). Runs against the mock adapter and both shipping adapters; a new adapter inherits it by invoking it with its factory.
- [ ] **S3-04.** API reports (adopt per OWN-05): api-extractor or `tsc --emitDeclarationOnly` + normalized dump producing a checked-in `etc/<pkg>.api.md` per published package; CI fails when regeneration differs. Scope to the npm publication set only — that is where a stable diffable API earns its keep.
- [ ] **S3-05.** License inventory script in CI: every vendored package carries its LICENSE; `package.json#license` fields match the `vendor/README.md` inventory (vendored MIT vs our BSD-3). Do first — it is trivial.
- [ ] **S3-06.** Advisory scanning: osv-scanner or `pnpm audit` on the lockfile, scheduled + on lockfile-touching PRs.
- [ ] **S3-07.** Vendor drift check (nightly CI or a local scheduled agent task if upstream credentials block CI): reconstruct `vendor/` from manifest SHAs plus checked-in patch files per logged modification; fail on any unexplained diff. Convert the two existing logged modifications into patch files.
- [ ] **S3-08.** Renovate (or a scheduled agent task) proposing small npm update PRs that ride the full gate suite; vendored packages excluded (they follow the manifest sync procedure).

# Mission: Transform Maestro into a Full Multi-Agent Orchestration System

I want to transform **Maestro Mode** into the central intelligence and orchestration layer of this application.

Maestro must have the ability to control, coordinate, and delegate work to all other available AI modes and supported CLI-based coding agents.

The goal is to build something significantly more capable than a traditional AI chat with a few agents. I want a genuinely intelligent multi-agent system where multiple agents can work simultaneously, each with its own CLI, model, reasoning effort, configuration, responsibilities, and execution context.

## 1. Research and Reference Implementation

Use this repository as a major architectural and functional reference:

[https://github.com/amElnagdy/delegate-skills](https://github.com/amElnagdy/delegate-skills)

Study the entire repository, especially:

- Individual delegate skills and supported CLI integrations.

- `delegate-setup`.

- Fleet and lane configuration.

- CLI discovery and capability detection.

- Model, effort, and variant configuration.

- Task delegation and execution.

- Structured execution results.

- Session management and resuming.

- Parallel task queues.

- Execution monitoring.

- Diff review and integration.

- Error handling and recovery.


Understand how the existing implementation works before designing ours.

Important: Do not simply copy this project or install its skills and call the task complete. Use its ideas and implementation patterns as a starting point. Design a more unified, intelligent, extensible, user-friendly, and performant system.

Respect its MIT license and preserve applicable attribution if reusing code.

## 2. Maestro: The Central Orchestrator

Maestro must become the primary manager of every other AI mode.

Instead of simply responding to the user's request itself, Maestro should be able to:

1. Understand the user's objective.

2. Analyze the complexity of the request.

3. Break the objective into smaller, independent tasks.

4. Determine which tasks can run simultaneously.

5. Select the most appropriate available modes and CLI agents.

6. Assign each agent a specific role and task.

7. Configure its CLI, model, reasoning effort, and other supported settings.

8. Execute tasks concurrently whenever possible.

9. Monitor every agent's progress.

10. Evaluate the results.

11. Identify conflicting, incomplete, or incorrect results.

12. Coordinate additional work when necessary.

13. Combine the results into one coherent final outcome.

14. Report what was accomplished, what failed, and what requires attention.


Maestro must be able to delegate to ALL available modes, not just a predefined selection.

It should discover the available modes and their capabilities dynamically through a central agent/mode registry.

Examples of potential responsibilities:

- Planning and architecture.

- Research and investigation.

- Software development.

- Frontend and UI development.

- Backend development.

- Debugging.

- Code review.

- Testing and QA.

- Documentation.

- Performance optimization.

- Security analysis.


These are examples, not restrictions. Use the actual capabilities available in the application.

### Maestro must retain central authority.

Other modes should execute their assigned responsibilities, while Maestro remains responsible for:

- Task allocation.

- Execution coordination.

- Priorities.

- Dependencies.

- Progress monitoring.

- Agent communication.

- Conflict resolution.

- Result evaluation.

- Final integration.


Maestro should be able to give agents additional instructions, revise their assignments, stop unnecessary work, and request corrections.

## 3. Intelligent Parallel Execution

This is one of the most important requirements.

I don't want Maestro to execute everything sequentially when multiple agents could work at the same time.

For example, when I request:

"Analyze this project, identify problems, improve its architecture, implement missing features, improve the UI, and test everything."

Maestro could create a task plan similar to:

|Agent|Responsibility|Assignment|
|---|---|---|
|Agent 1|Architecture|Analyze the existing architecture and identify structural problems.|
|Agent 2|Code Review|Investigate bugs, technical debt, and potential improvements.|
|Agent 3|UI/UX|Analyze the interface and propose improvements.|
|Agent 4|Backend|Analyze backend functionality and identify implementation requirements.|
|Agent 5|QA|Analyze existing tests and prepare a testing strategy.|

These agents should begin working concurrently when their assignments are independent.

Maestro should then coordinate their findings and create subsequent tasks based on the results.

### Execution requirements

Implement:

- Parallel task execution.

- Task dependency graphs.

- Dynamic task scheduling.

- Configurable maximum concurrency.

- Agent availability detection.

- Resource-aware scheduling.

- Priority management.

- Task cancellation.

- Task retry and reassignment.

- Timeout management.

- Automatic recovery from recoverable failures.

- Dependency-aware execution.

- Incremental result collection.


Do not create parallel execution artificially. Agents must actually execute as independent processes or supported independent agent sessions.

For coding tasks, use isolated workspaces or Git worktrees where appropriate. Prevent multiple agents from accidentally overwriting each other's changes.

Maestro must understand which tasks can safely run together and which tasks must wait for other tasks to finish.

For example, implementation should generally happen after the relevant architectural decisions are established. Integration testing should run against the integrated result, not an arbitrary unfinished workspace.

## 4. Fleet Management and Custom Configurations

I want users to be able to create their own agent fleets.

A fleet represents a reusable collection of agents and their configurations for a particular type of work.

Users must be able to configure fleets from Settings or directly through natural-language instructions to Maestro.

### Example: Settings → Agent Fleets

Allow users to:

- Create a fleet.

- Rename a fleet.

- Delete a fleet.

- Duplicate a fleet.

- Edit its agents.

- Enable or disable agents.

- Change execution priority.

- Configure concurrency.

- Select a default CLI for each responsibility.

- Select a model.

- Configure reasoning effort.

- Configure other CLI-specific options.

- Save changes.

- Set a default fleet.


Each agent configuration should support:

- Agent name.

- Assigned mode or CLI.

- Responsibility.

- CLI executable.

- Model.

- Reasoning effort or provider-specific equivalent.

- Permissions.

- Timeout.

- Priority.

- Enabled/disabled state.

- Other supported provider settings.


**Important:** Different CLI tools expose different capabilities and configuration parameters. Build provider-specific adapters and capability detection. Do not assume every CLI supports the same model, effort, permission, or execution flags.

Never silently ignore an unsupported configuration.

### Example fleets

Provide a few optional starter configurations:

**Development Fleet**

- Planning and architecture agent.

- Frontend development agent.

- Backend development agent.

- Code review agent.

- Testing agent.


**Research Fleet**

- Research agent.

- Investigation agent.

- Analysis agent.

- Verification agent.

- Report-writing agent.


**QA Fleet**

- Test planning agent.

- Test execution agent.

- Bug investigation agent.

- Code review agent.

- Regression testing agent.


These are starter templates. Users must be able to customize everything.

## 5. Natural-Language Fleet Configuration

Users shouldn't have to open Settings every time they want to change how Maestro works.

Maestro must understand instructions such as:

"Create a fleet for software development using OpenCode for frontend, Codex for backend, and Claude for code review."

"Use Codex with GPT-5.6 and high reasoning effort for this task."

"Run this test using OpenCode with this model and medium reasoning effort."

"Use my QA fleet for this project."

"Use the fastest available agents for this simple task."

"Use the strongest available models for this architecture review."

"Run three agents in parallel and let them independently investigate the issue."

"Save this configuration as my favorite debugging setup."

Maestro should be able to:

1. Understand the requested configuration.

2. Discover whether the requested CLI and model are available.

3. Validate provider-specific settings.

4. Apply them to the current execution or saved fleet.

5. Clearly explain any unavailable configuration.


Do not change saved defaults when the user only requests a one-time configuration.

## 6. Favorite Fleets and Reusable Work Presets

Add the ability to save favorite configurations for repeated tasks.

For example:

**Favorite: Full Project Audit**

- Architecture analysis: CLI A, Model A, High effort.

- Code review: CLI B, Model B, High effort.

- Security review: CLI C, Model C, High effort.

- QA analysis: CLI D, Model D, Medium effort.


**Favorite: Quick Testing**

- Testing agent: CLI A, Model A, Medium effort.

- Maximum concurrency: 2.


**Favorite: Feature Development**

- Planning agent.

- Frontend agent.

- Backend agent.

- Testing agent.

- Code review agent.


Users should be able to save, edit, duplicate, rename, delete, and execute favorites.

Maestro should understand natural-language instructions to select these configurations.

Examples:

"Use my Full Project Audit setup."

"Save this fleet as my favorite for debugging."

"Next time I ask you to test a project, use this configuration."

Users must also be able to define a default fleet for particular project types or recurring tasks.

## 7. Right-Side Chat Sidebar: Live Agent Activity

This is a major UI requirement.

When Maestro starts delegating work, open a new right-side sidebar inside the current chat.

This sidebar must show the agents working in real time.

I want to see the activity without having to ask Maestro repeatedly what each agent is doing.

### Sidebar layout

The sidebar should include:

**A. Overall task information**

- Main task title.

- Maestro's current status.

- Overall progress.

- Number of active agents.

- Completed tasks.

- Pending tasks.

- Failed tasks.

- Execution duration.


**B. Agent cards**

Every delegated agent should have its own card displaying:

- Agent name.

- CLI or mode.

- Selected model.

- Reasoning effort.

- Assigned task.

- Current status.

- Progress information.

- Current activity.

- Start time.

- Execution duration.


**C. Agent statuses**

Support statuses such as:

- Queued.

- Starting.

- Running.

- Waiting.

- Completed.

- Failed.

- Paused.

- Cancelled.

- Requires attention.


**D. Agent details**

Clicking an agent should reveal more information:

- Complete assignment.

- Execution timeline.

- Progress updates.

- Live output or streamed activity.

- Important messages.

- Results.

- Errors.

- Changed files.

- Test results.

- Session information.


**E. Task visualization**

Show the relationship between tasks and agents.

Users should be able to understand which agents are working simultaneously and which tasks are waiting for other tasks.

**F. Controls**

Where supported, allow users to:

- Pause an agent.

- Resume an agent.

- Cancel an agent.

- Retry a failed task.

- View its full output.

- Inspect its results.

- Send Maestro an instruction about that agent.


Include global controls for pausing or cancelling the overall execution.

### Sidebar behavior

- Open automatically when delegated work starts.

- Update in real time.

- Remain visible while Maestro works.

- Be collapsible.

- Preserve execution information when collapsed.

- Support multiple executions without mixing their agents.

- Preserve useful task history when navigating away and returning.

- Fit naturally into the existing chat layout.

- Work responsively on smaller screens.


Do not create a separate dashboard that forces users to leave the chat.

The experience must feel like watching a coordinated team of AI agents work together inside one conversation.

## 8. Communication Between Maestro and Agents

Maestro must maintain control throughout execution.

It should not simply launch agents and wait for their final responses.

Implement a communication and coordination layer that allows Maestro to:

- Receive progress updates.

- Receive partial results.

- Send additional instructions.

- Clarify assignments.

- Reassign tasks.

- Request corrections.

- Share relevant findings between agents.

- Detect blockers.

- Resolve task conflicts.

- Trigger subsequent tasks.


Agents should receive self-contained task briefs containing the necessary context, constraints, relevant files, expected outputs, and acceptance criteria.

Do not blindly send the entire conversation history to every agent. Provide relevant context to reduce unnecessary token consumption and improve execution speed.

Agents should return structured results using a consistent internal contract, including:

- Execution status.

- Exit code.

- Final report.

- Progress information.

- Touched files.

- Generated artifacts.

- Test results.

- Errors.

- Session identifier, when supported.


## 9. Intelligent Agent Selection

Maestro should not always use the same agent for everything.

Implement an intelligent selection mechanism based on:

- Task type.

- Required capabilities.

- CLI availability.

- Model availability.

- User preferences.

- Selected fleet.

- Model configuration.

- Reasoning effort.

- Agent performance history, if available.

- Execution speed.

- Resource consumption.

- Cost, when measurable.


For simple tasks, Maestro should avoid unnecessary expensive agents.

For difficult architecture, debugging, and research tasks, it should be able to select stronger configurations.

Do not invent model names or claim that a model is available without checking.

If a requested agent is unavailable, Maestro should inform the user and offer an appropriate alternative rather than silently switching.

## 10. Performance and Efficiency

I want this system to be faster and smarter than a basic delegation implementation.

Prioritize:

- Genuine concurrent execution.

- Efficient task decomposition.

- Minimal context duplication.

- Selective context sharing.

- Streaming updates.

- Efficient process management.

- Event-driven state updates where possible.

- Controlled polling when streaming is unavailable.

- Resource-aware concurrency.

- Intelligent retries.

- Avoiding unnecessary repeated work.

- Reusing sessions when appropriate.

- Incremental result processing.


Avoid spawning agents when a task is too simple to benefit from delegation.

Avoid waiting for every agent when a particular independent result is already sufficient for the next operation.

Maestro should be able to dynamically adjust the execution plan when new information becomes available.

## 11. Integration, Review, and Quality Control

Maestro must be responsible for the final result.

Every agent's output must be evaluated before it is integrated.

For development tasks:

1. Inspect the implementation.

2. Review the modified files.

3. Review Git diffs.

4. Detect conflicts and overlapping changes.

5. Run relevant tests.

6. Run linting and formatting.

7. Run build checks.

8. Verify that requested functionality actually works.

9. Request corrections where necessary.

10. Integrate verified changes into the main working tree.


Do not trust an agent's claim that its work is complete without verification.

Agents must not automatically commit, push, or deploy changes.

Maestro should own integration. Git commits, pushes, deployments, and other consequential actions must follow the application's existing permission model and user approval requirements.

## 12. Architecture and Extensibility

Design this as a proper, maintainable orchestration architecture.

Consider separating these responsibilities:

- Maestro orchestrator.

- Agent/mode registry.

- CLI discovery.

- Provider adapters.

- Fleet manager.

- Task planner.

- Task dependency manager.

- Execution scheduler.

- Process/session manager.

- Agent communication layer.

- Event and activity manager.

- Result collector.

- Review and integration manager.

- Persistence layer.


Use the existing application's architecture and conventions rather than introducing unnecessary frameworks or rewriting unrelated components.

Provider adapters should make it easy to add new CLI tools and modes in the future.

Adding another CLI should not require rewriting Maestro's orchestration logic.

The system should be designed to support additional execution backends and modes without major architectural changes.

Persist execution state and history appropriately so that refreshing the application does not destroy active task information.

## 13. Reliability and Security

This system will execute external CLI tools, so reliability and security are critical.

Implement:

- Secure process spawning.

- Proper argument escaping.

- No shell-string interpolation for untrusted user input.

- Process-tree termination.

- Timeouts.

- Cancellation handling.

- Resource limits.

- Error isolation.

- Retry limits.

- Session recovery where supported.

- Secure API-key handling.

- Permission-aware tool execution.

- Workspace isolation where appropriate.

- Protection against one agent corrupting another agent's work.


Never expose credentials in logs, agent cards, task history, or chat messages.

Do not bypass a CLI's permission restrictions just to make parallel execution work.

Clearly communicate when an operation requires additional permissions.

## 14. Implementation and Testing Requirements

Do not stop after producing an architecture document or a UI prototype.

I want the complete functionality implemented in the existing application.

Follow this workflow:

**Phase 1 — Investigation**

- Inspect the entire existing project.

- Understand all current AI modes.

- Identify their execution mechanisms.

- Inspect available CLI integrations.

- Understand existing settings and chat architecture.

- Identify the best integration points.

- Study the reference repository.

- For every significant problem in later phases, follow the Permanent engineering principles above (root-cause, reference study, cross-platform validation, reuse ledger).

- Document architectural decisions.


**Phase 2 — Core orchestration**

- Implement the agent registry.

- Implement CLI/provider adapters.

- Implement the fleet manager.

- Implement task decomposition and scheduling.

- Implement parallel execution.

- Implement task dependencies.

- Implement communication and result collection.


**Phase 3 — User configuration**

- Implement fleet settings.

- Implement model and effort configuration.

- Implement natural-language configuration.

- Implement favorite fleets.

- Implement execution-specific overrides.


**Phase 4 — Chat interface**

- Implement the right-side activity sidebar.

- Implement live agent cards.

- Implement execution timelines.

- Implement task visualization.

- Implement agent controls.

- Implement responsive behavior.


**Phase 5 — Integration and quality**

- Implement review and result integration.

- Implement execution persistence.

- Implement recovery and error handling.

- Implement permissions and security controls.


**Phase 6 — Testing** Test the entire system, not just isolated components.

At minimum, test:

1. Discovering available modes and CLIs.

2. Creating and editing fleets.

3. Saving and executing favorite configurations.

4. Overriding CLI, model, and effort for a single execution.

5. Running multiple agents simultaneously.

6. Waiting for dependent tasks.

7. Sending additional instructions to running agents.

8. Cancelling individual agents.

9. Cancelling an entire execution.

10. Handling unavailable CLIs.

11. Handling invalid model configurations.

12. Recovering from agent crashes and timeouts.

13. Reviewing and integrating multiple agent changes.

14. Keeping the sidebar synchronized with actual execution.

15. Preserving execution state after a page refresh.

16. Running a complete end-to-end orchestration scenario.

17. Validating the affected workflows on each supported platform (at minimum Windows and Linux) and recording the compatibility matrix; real-provider and real-platform evidence stays separate from mocks (see Permanent engineering principles above).


Use mock CLI processes for deterministic automated tests. Also perform real CLI integration tests for the providers that are actually installed and authenticated.

Never claim that a real CLI integration works based only on mocked tests.

## 15. Definition of Done

The implementation is not complete until all of the following work:

- Maestro can discover and manage the other available modes.

- Maestro can delegate tasks to multiple agents.

- Multiple agents can work concurrently.

- Maestro understands dependencies between tasks.

- Every agent can use its own supported CLI, model, and reasoning effort.

- Users can configure fleets from Settings.

- Users can configure fleets through natural language.

- Users can save and reuse favorite configurations.

- Users can override configuration for individual executions.

- The right-side chat sidebar displays actual agent activity.

- Users can inspect individual agents and their results.

- Users can pause, resume, cancel, and retry supported operations.

- Maestro can evaluate and integrate agent results.

- Execution information survives page refreshes.

- Errors are handled without crashing the entire orchestration.

- A reference-implementation record (source, revision, license, reuse decision) exists for every significant problem solved by reference study, and the supported-platform compatibility matrix records green vs unvalidated rows (see Permanent engineering principles above).

- Tests, linting, and builds pass.


## Permanent engineering principles (all releases, from the 2026-11-08 owner amendment)

These rules bind every Greeneek subsystem and every release phase, not only Harness, Fleet, or Maestro. The mandate they rest on is permanent (2026-11-08 amendment); this plan remains its requirement home for v0.3.9.

### Reference-driven problem solving

- For every significant bug, defect, limitation, architectural weakness, missing capability, performance problem, integration issue, or UX problem: root-cause first, then inspect the four reference repositories for relevant implementations, fixes, patterns, and lessons — actual source, tests, and history, not READMEs alone.
- References (DeepSeek Harness is primary): [deepseek-harness](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness), [pandacode](https://github.com/aicodeking/pandacode), [t3code](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code), [delegate-skills](https://github.com/amElnagdy/delegate-skills).
- Compare approaches (correctness, maintainability, performance, security, compatibility, cost); take the strongest or combine the best parts. Substantial code reuse, including adapting complete implementations, is authorized when it is the best result — recorded per § Reference ledger below.
- Integrate into Greeneek's architecture and contracts; no disconnected patches. If no reference answers the problem, investigate other credible approaches and document the decision.

### Cross-platform requirement

Every solution preserves compatibility with the supported Windows and Linux environments at minimum. Never design around one machine, path, username, shell, package manager, executable location, OS-specific process/filesystem/env/permission behavior, installed tools, credentials, or network. Use platform abstractions, capability detection, configuration, and isolated adapters (the `subprocess` Win32 seam is the template). Maintain the compatibility matrix (OS × workflow) in the Execution ledger; validated rows are green, unvalidated rows are stated, never claimed.

### No temporary or machine-specific fixes

No one-off workarounds, hard-coded exceptions, test-only behavior differing from production, silent fallbacks, one-environment fixes, or special cases that miss the root cause. External limits (credentials, quotas, services) are classified honestly as BLOCKED with the exact missing input, never disguised as code fixes.

### Reference, licensing, and attribution ledger

Every reuse decision records: Greeneek issue/requirement ID, repositories and revisions inspected, relevant files, alternatives compared, reuse decision (copy/port/adapt/redesign/retain/reject) with reason, license verification (actual LICENSE, not assumption — including file-level exceptions), required notices, dependency licenses, Greeneek modifications, and the integration tests. Required notices are preserved; third-party records are updated.

### Definition of done (per significant problem)

Root cause addressed, clean integration, regression coverage against recurrence, supported-platform validation, remaining limitations explicitly documented. "Works on this machine", "one test passed", "it compiles", "one OS works", or a hidden workaround is not resolved.

## Final Instructions

Think of Maestro as the manager of an intelligent, configurable fleet of AI agents, not just another AI mode.

The user should be able to say:

"Maestro, analyze my entire project, find every problem, improve the architecture, implement the necessary features, optimize the UI, and test everything. Use my development fleet."

Maestro should independently plan the work, assign the appropriate agents, run independent tasks simultaneously, coordinate dependencies, monitor their progress through the right-side sidebar, review their output, and deliver one unified result.

The user should also be able to give Maestro individual CLI/model/effort instructions or select a saved favorite.

**Most importantly: implement this inside the existing application, preserve existing functionality, and make it production-quality.**

Do not replace working functionality with placeholders.

Do not build fake agents that only display simulated progress.

Do not implement a frontend-only demonstration.

Do not claim completion without executing the available tests and verification gates.

Make sensible architectural decisions independently, document important trade-offs, and continue through implementation and verification rather than stopping at planning.

Follow the Permanent engineering principles above: every significant problem gets a root cause, a reference study, and supported-platform validation — not a first-apparent fix or a machine-specific workaround.

- [ ] **S5-11b.** Maestro dispatches to the other modes as lanes: code → standard, design → pixels-designer, docs → instructor, parallel swarm → army/wave. Per-lane presets already exist (`SubagentStartRequest.agentPreset`); the missing piece is mode-level routing UX and copy that makes Maestro's control of the other modes explicit to the user.

## Phase 8 — Maestro fleet orchestration (owner mission, 2026-11-01)

*The mission text is preserved verbatim inside Phase 5c above; this is its executable form. Reference implementation for ideas and patterns: `amElnagdy/delegate-skills` (MIT) — its attribution is preserved where code is reused, and copying its skills in place is not completion.*

- [ ] **S8-01.** Agent/mode registry: Maestro discovers the modes and CLI agents that actually exist, with each one's capabilities, and never delegates to an entry discovery did not return.
- [ ] **S8-02.** Provider adapters per CLI (model, reasoning effort, permissions, session resume, structured result), so adding a CLI never touches orchestration logic. An unsupported setting is reported, never silently ignored.
- [ ] **S8-03.** Fleet manager: create, rename, delete, duplicate, edit members, enable/disable, priority, concurrency, per-member CLI/model/effort/permissions/timeout, and a default fleet — persisted and read back after a reload.
- [ ] **S8-04.** Starter fleets (Development, Research, QA) shipped as templates a user can edit, not fixed configurations.
- [ ] **S8-05.** Natural-language fleet configuration to a structured proposal with the confirmation card before spend; a one-time instruction never rewrites a saved default; an unavailable CLI or model is named as unavailable with an alternative offered.
- [ ] **S8-06.** Favorite fleets: save, edit, duplicate, rename, delete, execute, and per-project-type defaults.
- [ ] **S8-07.** Task planner: decompose an objective into tasks with dependencies, run independent tasks concurrently for real (separate processes or independent agent sessions), and wait only on genuine dependencies.
- [ ] **S8-08.** Scheduler controls: configurable maximum concurrency, resource-aware admission, priority, cancellation, retry and reassignment with a retry limit, and timeout handling.
- [ ] **S8-09.** Communication layer: progress updates and partial results reach Maestro, and Maestro can send instructions, clarify, reassign, request correction, and share a finding between agents without forwarding whole conversation histories.
- [ ] **S8-10.** Structured result contract on every agent return: status, exit code, report, progress, touched files, artifacts, test results, errors, and session id where supported.
- [ ] **S8-11.** Isolation: worktree or separate workspace per writing agent, no overwrite collisions, and one agent cannot corrupt another's work.
- [ ] **S8-12.** Right-side chat sidebar as a real sidebar surface: overall task state, per-agent cards with CLI/model/effort/status/duration, click-through detail and timeline, task visualization of concurrency and waiting, per-agent and global pause/resume/cancel/retry, auto-open on delegated work, collapsible without losing state, responsive at narrow width, and it survives a page refresh.
- [ ] **S8-13.** Intelligent selection from task type, capability, availability, user preference, fleet, speed, resource use, and measured cost; simple tasks do not summon expensive agents.
- [ ] **S8-14.** Review and integration: inspect the implementation, read the diffs, detect overlapping changes, run the relevant tests, lint, and build, request corrections, and integrate only verified work. An agent's own claim of completion is never the evidence. Agents never commit, push, or deploy; consequential actions follow the existing permission model.
- [ ] **S8-15.** Reliability and security: secure spawning with argument escaping and no shell interpolation of untrusted input, process-tree termination, timeouts, resource limits, error isolation, session recovery, credential handling that never appears in logs, cards, history, or chat, and permission-aware execution that is not bypassed to make parallelism work.
- [ ] **S8-16.** Tests for all sixteen mission-listed behaviors with mock CLI processes for determinism, plus real integration for each CLI actually installed and authenticated. Mocked coverage is never reported as real-CLI verification.
- [ ] **S8-17.** Performance: event-driven updates where a stream exists and controlled polling where it does not, incremental result collection, session reuse where appropriate, and no spawn for work too small to benefit.

### Fleet settings: measured gap between the shipped section and S8-03…S8-06

Read from `packages/subagent/agent-team/src/fleet-settings.ts` (274 lines) — the whole `fleet` settings namespace is `FleetSettings = { favorites: FleetFavorite[] }`, where a favorite is `{ id, name, members[] }` and a member is `{ name, description, prompt, provider, context, preset, model, reasoningEffort, readOnly, writeScopes, maxTokens }`. Favorites validate on save (`validateFleetSettings`, unique ids), launch re-asserts provider readiness without substitution (`assertFavoriteProviderReady`), and availability rows come from `fleetProviderAvailability`. Against the requirements above, the section therefore **misses** every following field and behaviour, which is the owner's "done wrong" in concrete terms:

- [ ] **S8-03a.** Fleet-level controls required by S8-03: `duplicate`, per-fleet `enabled`, `priority`, `concurrency`, per-member `timeout`, and a `default` fleet. Status after 2026-11-05: `enabled` and `concurrency` are enforced by `launchFleetFavorite` **and now editable in Settings** (`fleet-disabled` refusal, batched launch, Enabled checkbox, Members-at-once field, `Disabled`/`N at once` row tags, persistence across reload verified in the app); `duplicate` is reachable as a product feature through `@Remote('duplicateFleet')` and its row action. Still open: `priority` and a `default` fleet have no consumer — priority belongs to the S8-08 admission path and a default flag has no launcher — so storing them now would recreate the stored-but-unenforced shape this plan already rejected; per-member `timeoutMs` has a consumer (the roster enforces it for a Melody member and refuses it for a lane) and is the next field to add, inside S8-21.
- [x] **S8-04a.** Starter fleets: `FLEET_STARTER_TEMPLATES` (Development/Research/QA), `starterFleet`, `fleetStarterTemplates`, and `TeamService.installStarterFleet` shipped with domain and service tests, and installed rows are ordinary editable favorites. The product path closed on 2026-11-05: `@Remote('fleetStarters')` and `@Remote('installStarterFleet')` plus the Starter fleets block that previews each template's members and installs it, verified in `gnk web` — see the 2026-11-05 ledger row.
- [ ] **S8-05a.** Natural-language fleet configuration: `fleetProposals` + `fleetApprovalReason` + the `allowed-once` approval card exist in `tool-agent-team` and echo each member's provider, context, preset, model, effort, budget, and write capability before any child is created, so the proposal and confirmation halves are shipped for the model path. `fleetProviderAvailability` names an unavailable provider without substituting one. Open: the Settings-side natural-language entry. **One gap closed 2026-11-06:** `packages/subagent/tool-agent-team/tests/tool-team.spec.ts` → `never rewrites a saved favorite for a one-time approved fleet instruction` pins the one-time rule end to end — it mounts the real file settings provider, saves a favorite, runs two `spawn_fleet` instructions with different labels/models under `allowed-once`, and asserts two independent approval requests, no `saveFleetFavorite` call, and a byte-identical favorites view. A temporary mutation that wrote settings from `spawn_fleet` made it fail with `a one-time instruction must not write fleet settings: expected 2 to be +0`; the mutation was reverted (`git diff` on `src/index.ts` empty) and the full suite reads 22 passed. This is regression coverage for an invariant the shipped code already held, not a defect fix.
- [x] **S8-06a.** Favorite fleets: save, edit, rename, delete, and execute are in the Settings page; **Duplicate is now user-facing too** through `@Remote('duplicateFleet')` and a per-row action, verified in the app on 2026-11-05. Per-project-type defaults remain unimplemented and are held inside S8-21, because no launcher reads a default flag yet.
- [x] **S8-03b.** Resolved by the owner's decision, recorded in [Fleets hold native agents and external CLI agents through one execution seam](../architecture/2026-11-03-hybrid-fleets-use-the-subagent-provider-seam.md). Fleets are heterogeneous: native in-harness agents and external CLI agents coexist, reached through the existing `SubagentProvider` seam rather than a new parallel interface, with eligibility derived from descriptor capability (`prepareContinuable` presence, `packages/subagent/agent-team/src/index.ts:502-504`) instead of a hard-coded native pair. Native modes are never modelled as processes and CLI agents are never forced to behave like in-harness children; unsupported settings keep refusing loud (`UNSUPPORTED_CAPABILITY`). The decision is authorized but not yet implemented — S8-03c and S8-03d carry the work.
- [ ] **S8-03c.** The measured blocker for mixed fleets, found while resolving S8-03b: a Team lane requires a **continuable** child. `TeamRoster.spawn` is documented and built as "one named, continuable direct child of the Team Lead" (`packages/subagent/agent-team/src/roster.ts:242,247`) and session recovery re-activates a member only when its folded descriptor reads `mode === 'continuable'` (`roster.ts:558`), while `packages/subagent/agent-team/tests/fleet-settings.spec.ts:27` recorded a CLI provider as `state: 'unsupported-version', continuable: false` (that row now reads `state: 'one-shot'`). Add the one-shot lane mode (roster, `lane-lifecycle.ts`, projection, result contract) so a provider without `prepareContinuable` can still be a fleet member that runs to completion and returns a structured result, and keep mailbox/follow-up refused for it rather than ignored. Do not widen availability to `ready` before this mode exists: that converts a pre-flight refusal into a launch-time failure.
  - **Two constraints pinned on 2026-11-03 that decide the shape of the work.** (1) The member phase set is `'provisioning' | 'active' | 'failed'` and the projection enforces exactly one edge — first snapshot must be `provisioning`, and `projection.ts:256-258` rejects any transition that does not come *from* `provisioning` — so a terminal `completed` state needs the transition law relaxed **and** the dispatch path in the same change; adding the phase alone creates unreachable branches that fail the per-file 100% gate. (2) Recording *any* provisioning failure durably is intentional: `tests/team.spec.ts:342-353` asserts that an unknown provider leaves a `failed` member row that reserves the name and counts toward `maxMembers`. So a Team-owned pre-flight capability refusal is the wrong instrument — a 2026-11-03 attempt to add one at `roster.ts:357` broke that assertion and was reverted unpushed. Rejection of continuation, mailbox, and follow-up belongs in the mailbox/interrupt paths keyed on the member's own mode once one-shot members exist, not before dispatch.
  - **Roster half implemented on 2026-11-03, measured.** `roster.ts` selects the execution model from the registered provider capability, `types.ts` adds `TeamMemberMode`, `TeamMemberResult`, the `completed` phase and `SpawnTeammateRequest.timeoutMs`, and `projection.ts` permits `active → completed` only for a one-shot row.
