The per-tool interface facts below were measured on this host on 2026-11-04 (versions, executables, flags, real runs, ACP handshakes), not read from marketing pages, and no fake provider stands in for a real one.
  - **OpenCode 2.2.4** — `C:\Users\Sultan\AppData\Roaming\npm\opencode.ps1` → `@opencode/cli` `bin/opencode.exe`. Real one-shot run succeeded: `opencode run --format json -m groq/openai/gpt-oss-20b "…"` exits 0 and prints NDJSON events `{type:"step_start"|"text"|"step_finish"|"error", timestamp, sessionID, part:{…,text}}`; the assistant answer is the `text` event's `part.text`. Its `acp` subcommand answered an `initialize` request with `protocolVersion: 1` and `agentCapabilities.loadSession: true`, which is OpenCode's own claim about its protocol surface: the generic `subagent-acp` provider drives one prompt per run and registers no `prepareContinuable`, so mounting `command: 'opencode', args: ['acp']` yields a **one-shot** provider, and continuation is separate work in that provider rather than something the handshake buys. Its `--session <id>`, `--continue`, and `--fork` flags continue a session across processes; `--auto` auto-approves permissions; `--file` attaches files; `--model provider/model#variant` selects the model. Credential state is partial and must be reported, not hidden: `-m google/gemini-2.5-flash` failed with `provider.auth — The bound service account is deleted or disabled`, and its own default model failed with `provider.invalid-request`, while `groq/*` worked.
  - **KiloCode CLI 7.8.18** — `@kilocode/cli`, both `kilo` and `kilocode` names. Same event envelope as OpenCode (it is an OpenCode-lineage fork) with the same `run --format json`, `--session/--continue/--fork`, `--model`, `--variant`, `--agent`, `--thinking`, `--auto`, plus `--attach <url>`, `--port`, `serve`, `acp`, `session`, `export`, `github`, `mcp`, and `daemon`. Its `acp` server answered `initialize` with `protocolVersion: 1`, `loadSession: true`, and `mcpCapabilities.sse: true`. Model ids must carry the provider prefix: `-m "~anthropic/claude-haiku-latest"` was refused with `Model not found`, and the correct id form is `kilo/~anthropic/claude-haiku-latest`. **Blocked on account state, not on integration:** `kilo auth list` shows exactly one credential (`Kilo Gateway oauth`, stored at `~\.local\share\kilo\auth.json`) and `kilo models` lists only `kilo/*` routes, so a real task currently returns `Payment Required: "Paid Model - Credits Required" … "balance": 0`. A session is created and the event stream flows before that refusal, which proves the transport; the owner adding gateway credits or configuring another provider in Kilo makes the same path run end to end with no code change.
  - **Agy 1.4.12** (Google Antigravity CLI) — `C:\Users\Sultan\AppData\Local\agy\bin\agy.exe`, **not on PATH** on this host, state under `~\.gemini\antigravity-cli`. Real one-shot run succeeded: `agy --output-format json --print-timeout 120s --print="…"` exits 0 with one JSON object `{conversation_id, status:"SUCCESS", response:"PROBE OK\n", duration_seconds, num_turns, usage:{input_tokens,output_tokens,thinking_tokens,cache_read_tokens,total_tokens}}`. Two parser facts an adapter must encode: the prompt must be **attached** to the flag (`--print=…`) or Agy consumes the next token as its prompt and exits 2 with its own explanatory error, and its credential store is read from disk, so no harness secret needs forwarding. Agy exposes **no ACP server** and no headless SDK: its only machine surfaces are `--output-format text|json|stream-json`, `--input-format stream-json` (NDJSON turns, requiring stream-json output), `--conversation <id>` / `--continue` resumption, `--json-schema` structured output, `--model`, `--effort low|medium|high|xhigh|max`, `--mode accept-edits|auto|plan`, `--project`, `--agent`, `--sandbox`, `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, `--print-timeout`, plus `mcp`/`plugin` subcommands. So Agy is genuinely **one-shot** for Greeneek today; a continuable lane would need `stream-json` bidirectional driving, which is separate work, and its `conversation_id` has no field in `SubagentResult` to live in, so resumption is not carried across delegations.
  - **Claude Code 2.1.318** — `C:\Users\Sultan\.local\bin\claude.exe`, not on PATH; authenticated state lives in `~\.claude.json`. A real one-shot task succeeded: `claude -p "Reply with exactly two words: PROBE OK" --output-format json` exits 0 in 5s and prints one JSON object whose `result` field is `PROBE OK`, with `stop_reason: "end_turn"`, `session_id`, `num_turns`, `total_cost_usd`, a `usage` block (input/output/cache/thinking tokens), `permission_denials`, and `is_error`. The CLI also exposes `--agents`, `--bg/--background` with `claude attach|logs|stop|rm`, `--resume`, and `--bare`, so session continuation and background jobs are product-side features. **The existing adapter already covers it and was proven today:** `packages/subagent/subagent-claude-code/tests/real-product.spec.ts` → **8 passed** driving the real Agent SDK and CLI through `ctx.subagents`, and `tests/loader-composition.e2e.ts` asserts the registered provider reports every optional capability false, so it is a one-shot provider by advertisement, not by omission. No new adapter is warranted.
  - **Codex `codex-cli 0.166.0`** — `C:\Users\Sultan\AppData\Local\Programs\OpenAI\Codex\bin\codex.exe`, plus `codex-exec-server.exe` beside it and a second `0.164.0-alpha.11.1` build under `~\.codex\.sandbox-bin`. `codex login status` reports `Logged in using ChatGPT`, and a real task succeeded: `codex exec --skip-git-repo-check "Reply with exactly two words: PROBE OK"` exits 0 in 6s, prints a `session id`, the final answer, and `tokens used 2,906` (it also logged an unrelated `rmcp` `HTTP 403` from a configured remote MCP connector, which did not stop the run). Its surface is `exec` with `--output-schema <file>` for a JSON-Schema-shaped final answer, `-m`, `-s/--sandbox`, `-C/--cd`, plus `resume`/`fork`/`queue`/`archive`, `app-server`, `mcp`, `plugin`, `doctor`, and `sandbox`; it has **no `acp` subcommand**, so the dedicated adapter is the only supported integration. **That adapter already works:** `packages/subagent/subagent-codex/tests/real-product.spec.ts` → **7 passed** against the real `@openai/codex` CLI. The adapter pins `@openai/codex@0.149.1` while the host app is `0.166.0`: the provider resolves the CLI it installed rather than the host's PATH entry, so a version-skew check against the host build is the open question here, not a broken integration.
  - **Hermes Agent v0.19.0+3069.g8defb9f.dirty (2026.9.24, stable, install method `git`)** — `C:\Users\Sultan\AppData\Local\hermes\bin\hermes.exe`, on PATH, runtime under `~\AppData\Local\hermes\hermes-agent`, secrets in `~\AppData\Local\hermes\.env` (whose line 486 python-dotenv cannot parse, so every invocation prints that warning). Pooled credentials are `kilocode`, `nvidia`, `opencode-zen`, `openrouter`, all api_key; the configured default is model `nvidia/nemotron-3-ultra-550b-a55b:free` through provider `moa`. **Task execution is blocked by provider state, measured three ways:** `-z` one-shot on `moa` → `HTTP 401: User not found`, on `openrouter` → `HTTP 401: User not found`, on `nvidia` → `HTTP 404: 404 page not found`, each exiting 2. Its ACP server is real and answers: `hermes acp --check` → `Hermes ACP check OK`, and one newline-framed `initialize` over stdio returned `protocolVersion: 1` with `agentCapabilities: { loadSession: true, promptCapabilities: { image: true }, sessionCapabilities: { fork, list, resume } }`, `agentInfo: { name: hermes-agent, version: 0.19.0 }`, and two `authMethods` (`moa` runtime credentials, and a `terminal` `hermes --setup` flow Greeneek must not run). Hermes' richest machine surface for scripts is `hermes -z PROMPT --usage-file PATH`, which writes a complete structured report **even on failure** — `estimated_cost_usd`, `cost_status`, token counts, `api_calls`, `model`, `provider`, `session_id`, `completed`, `partial`, `interrupted`, `turn_exit_reason`, `failed`, `service_tier`, an `auxiliary` block, and `failure` — plus `-m`, `--provider`, `--reasoning`, `-t/--toolsets`, `--resume`/`-c`, `--in DIR`, `--worktree`, `--accept-hooks`, `--ignore-rules`, `--safe-mode`, and `-p <profile>` for isolated profiles. Session persistence is real (`hermes sessions list` showed the probe runs with workspace-scoped `local-<sha>` ids), and `hermes mcp`/`hermes proxy` (OpenAI-compatible) and 62 discovered plugins exist, but a session id is not a Greeneek continuation.
  - **Hermes Desktop is a client of the same runtime, not a second agent.** `hermes serve` runs "the JSON-RPC/WebSocket gateway the desktop app and remote clients connect to", headless on `127.0.0.1:9119` by default with `--status`/`--stop`/`--isolated` and a one-shot `--ssh-session-token-file`; `hermes desktop` builds and launches the Electron app (or `--source` against `apps/desktop/dist`), and `hermes webapp` opens that workspace in a browser. `hermes serve --status` on this host: `No hermes dashboard or serve processes running`. The gateway binds loopback and the June 2026 hardening made `--insecure` a no-op, so a public bind needs a real auth provider — meaning the Desktop surface offers no additional capability the CLI/ACP path lacks, and automating its UI or reading its token files is out of bounds by design. Integration should therefore target `hermes acp` (or a dedicated `-z`/`--usage-file` adapter), not the Desktop app.
  - **Decision, per tool, using the seam that already exists.** OpenCode, KiloCode, and Hermes need **configuration, not code**: one row of the existing `subagent-acp` provider each, with its own `command` and `args` (`opencode acp`, `kilo acp`, `hermes acp`), which makes all three fleet-eligible through `admittedProviders()`. They report `one-shot`, not `ready`, because `subagent-acp` implements one prompt per run and registers no `prepareContinuable`; adding continuation to that provider is the follow-up that upgrades them, not a re-architecture. Claude Code and Codex need **neither**: their dedicated adapters are shipped, dormant behind opt-in Profile layers, and proven against the real products on this host today. Only Agy needs one small new package, `packages/subagent/subagent-agy`, mirroring `subagent-codex`: `Config` = `providerName` (default `agy`), `command` (default `agy`, absolute path supported because PATH lacks it), `model`, `effort`, `mode`, `project`, `agent`, `jsonSchema`, `printTimeoutMs`, `disposeGraceMs`, `env`; `capabilities = NO_START_CAPABILITIES`, `inheritsParentContext = false`, no `prepareContinuable`; `start()` resolves the child cwd from the parent session, refuses non-text prompt blocks loudly, spawns through `ctx.subprocess.spawn` with `signal` so cancellation reaches Agy's process tree, then `settleRunResult` + `subprocessRunHandle` from `@greeneek/gnk-subagent` for the never-rejecting result and idempotent dispose; `status !== 'SUCCESS'` or a non-zero exit becomes `stopReason: 'error'` whose diagnostic carries Agy's own status plus captured stderr.
  - **Real verification owed, with what is already done marked off.** (1) Still open: build `subagent-agy` with unit tests over a fake `SubprocessHandle` (argv exactness including `--print=`, envelope decode, non-SUCCESS status, non-zero exit, abort → `terminate()` then `waitForExit()`, dispose idempotence) and a real-CLI e2e that runs a harmless prompt when the executable resolves and **skips with its reason printed** when it does not. (2) Done: the three ACP rows ship as tracked opt-in examples (`apps/cli/config/examples/{opencode-acp,kilocode-acp,hermes-acp}/cordis.yml`), and all three mount in the built app. Done for OpenCode, still open for the other two: proving each row through a **real delegation** — OpenCode is now proven end to end through the Team roster by `packages/subagent/agent-team/tests/opencode-acp-fleet.e2e.ts` (2026-11-04 ledger row), while Kilo remains blocked on gateway credits and Hermes on every pooled provider key. (3) Done, including through the actual application: native `spawn` lane plus a real external member in one Team, asserted by that same suite, and dispatched by Maestro in `gnk web` with `provider: "opencode"` — see the 2026-11-04 selection row for what the UI proved and for the external model-route limit that stopped the run. (4) Detection must report an installed-but-unusable provider accurately: Kilo's zero balance and Hermes' rejected keys are credential state, so `providerAvailability` must not claim beyond registration truth, and the ledger must name the observed error rather than hiding it. (5) New, from mounting Claude Code through the built binary: `--patch packages/subagent/subagent-claude-code/cordis.patch.yml` loads for `--dump-config` but the real launch refuses with `Cannot find package '@greeneek/gnk-subagent-claude-code' imported from C:\Users\Sultan\.gnk\profiles\headless\`, because an optional Profile bundle stays outside the app installation until `gnk plugin --profile <name> add <package>` installs it — and that install cannot work from source here, since `npm view @greeneek/gnk-subagent-claude-code` and `@greeneek/gnk-subagent-acp` both return `E404` from the public registry while `NPM_TOKEN` publishing is still an open release item. So a real Claude Code or Codex delegation needs either a published bundle or the package-owned e2e path (`tests/real-greeneek.e2e.ts`, which self-skips: `pnpm exec vitest run --config vitest.e2e.config.ts` on both files → **2 skipped**, needing `GREENEEK_BASE_URL` and `GREENEEK_API_KEY`). Note the contrast worth keeping straight: the OpenCode row needs no bundle install because `@greeneek/gnk-subagent-acp` is an `apps/cli` dependency, so its overlay resolves from the app installation and an example layer is enough to mount it.

Adding a dependency also refreshes `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` and the publint/hygiene lane, and the bundle-size and coverage gates apply to the new sources.
  - **Chart surface measured 2026-11-05 (S8-19 shipped at `4b607e84`; S8-20 is not started).** `DashboardPage.tsx` contains no `<svg>` at all — the only chart-like surface is `span.css.modelTrack` (`aria-hidden="true"`), the per-row usage bar in the model/provider/preset breakdowns, with the ranked list driven by `dashboard-model.ts` (`DashboardModelUsage`/`DashboardProviderUsage`/`DashboardPresetUsage`, `DashboardMetric = 'count' | 'tokens'`, `DashboardFacts.dataState: 'empty' | 'partial' | 'complete'`) and priced by `dashboard-pricing.ts`. So the migration set is those breakdown tracks plus any derived aggregate the page already shows: the series source stays the projection fold, and `dashboard.usageAria` ('Model usage distribution'), `dashboard.metric{Count,Tokens}`, `dashboard.modelsEmpty`/`providersEmpty`/`presetsEmpty`, `dashboard.costNoCatalog`/`costNoUsage`/`costMixed`, and `dashboard.partial` are the existing accessibility, empty, and partial-data states to carry over — none may be replaced by placeholder series. `ui-workspace` and `ui-primitives` are the only packages to touch, plus `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` (a pre-commit generator) and the publint/bundle-size lanes that a new dependency activates.
  - **The styling constraint that decides how this is adopted.** Tailwind appears in exactly one manifest, `apps/landing/package.json`, and no client package uses it: the Dashboard styles come from `DashboardPage.module.css`, and `ui-primitives` depends on `clsx` alone. shadcn's chart is Recharts plus a small component contract — `ChartContainer` around `ChartTooltipProvider`, `ChartTooltip`/`ChartLegend` with shared content renderers, and a `chartConfig` whose `theme` maps series ids to CSS custom properties — so "the official shadcn/ui Charts implementation" here means porting those components and that contract onto the existing token and CSS-module layer, keeping Recharts as the rendering engine and the `chartConfig`/`--chart-*` binding intact. Introducing Tailwind into the client build purely to host a chart would be a larger architectural change than the charts themselves, so it is not the default; if the owner asks for the verbatim Tailwind files instead, that is the decision to raise before the work starts.

### Harness architecture (owner decision 2026-11-06; extends Phase 8, does not reorder it)

These increments are governed by the Permanent engineering principles above: reference study (DeepSeek Harness first), cross-platform validation, and the reuse ledger apply to every S8 item. The owner's 2026-11-06 ruling is recorded as [a proposed architecture Agent Note](../architecture/2026-11-06-harness-is-the-unit-of-agent-execution.md) with the measured code facts and rejected alternatives. It **supersedes the product shape** of S8-18b/c/e (those rows' identifier, persistence, and vocabulary work stays shipped) and adds the increments below. Each item ships its enforcing consumer and a focused test in the same commit; no descriptor field is stored before a consumer reads it. These items follow the Sequence table — nothing here starts before Phase 0 is green twice on one SHA — and none widens a v0.3.9 gate or waits on Phase 9.

- [ ] **S8-22.** `HarnessDescriptor` as an optional additive field on `SubagentProvider` registration: identity, named connections, model catalog, per-model effort levels, context capabilities (session forking, fresh context, additional context, context recovery, session resume), permissions, and session lifecycle. A gate test rejects any shipped provider that advertises a capability it does not implement, and rejects an unknown value rather than defaulting it. **Shipped 2026-11-08 (Crouton Alpha):** `harness-descriptor.ts` derivation, `HarnessRegistry` grouping/probing, `@Remote('harnesses'/'refreshHarnesses'/'refreshHarness')`, Fleet Settings + `spawnTeammate` wiring, typed `HarnessCapabilities` on the seam; evidence `harness-descriptor.spec.ts` (16), `harness-registry.spec.ts` (8), `discovery.spec.ts` (14). Remaining: the advertised-vs-implemented gate test across all shipped providers.
- [ ] **S8-23.** Stop discarding the ACP InitializeResponse in `packages/subagent/subagent-acp/src/run.ts:476-481`; surface advertised capabilities on the descriptor. Unit tests over a fake ACP child plus a real-CLI e2e that self-skips with its reason printed when the executable is absent. Mocked coverage is never reported as real-CLI verification. **Shipped 2026-11-08 (Crouton Alpha):** `initialize` retained on `SubagentRun.acpInitializeResult`; live probe (`discover.ts`) maps `initialize`+`session/new` onto `HarnessCapabilities` (`acp-config.ts`), no prompt spent; fake-child unit tests (`discovery.spec.ts`, 14, real stdio, proves no-prompt + `set_config_option` recording + refusal naming) plus real-CLI e2e (`discovery-real.e2e.ts`, 2/2 PASS vs installed OpenCode v2.2.4).
- [ ] **S8-24.** Dynamic model and effort discovery per Harness (`model/list`, Initialization Response, `provider list --json`, or the curated host catalog), reusing `packages/acp/acp/src/model-control.ts` and `tool-subagent`'s `list_subagent_models` rather than duplicating them. Real model id stays separate from display name and description; `unknown` is an honest first-class state; `Default` sends no override; a cache carries a drift hash. No static list is presented as live.
- [ ] **S8-25.** Fleet Settings becomes a Harness/Connection selector with Model, Effort, Context (Advanced Override, option C) and Permissions rendered only from the descriptor. `fork` and `spawn` leave every user-facing selector while their identifiers, persisted values, `spawnTeammate`, `TeamMemberMode` and the `team/member` schema stay; Harmony and Melody remain execution types independent of Harness. Three-locale parity, WCAG and logical-CSS gates stay green. **Shipped 2026-11-08 (Crouton Alpha):** full Harness→Connection→Model→Effort→Context flow with auto-clearing, per-connection probe, source/`discoveredAt`/`discoveryError` presentation (zh/en/ar); evidence `fleet-settings.client.spec.tsx` 28/28 + `browser-plugin.client.spec.ts`.
- [ ] **S8-26.** Greeneek is a Harness: the in-harness runtime publishes its host LLM registry, effort levels and context strategies through the same descriptor, and one Fleet can mix a Greeneek member with two external Harnesses using independent Models and Effort. **Partial 2026-11-08 (Crouton Alpha):** spawn/fork advertise Harness identity `greeneek`; `maestro-real.e2e.ts` mixes a Greeneek member with the OpenCode connection and proves registry validation live; host-LLM-registry publication remains.
- [ ] **S8-27.** Per-member execution profiles (name, role, Harness, Connection, Model, Effort, Context, Permissions, Execution Type, timeout) with saved-Fleet settings separated from per-task overrides; Maestro reconfigures a member's Harness or Model mid-conversation for subsequent tasks without rebuilding the chat or renaming the member. A test proves a one-task override leaves the saved favorite byte-identical, and that Maestro routes only the context each agent needs.
- [ ] **S8-28.** Maestro authority over Standard, Army, Maestro, Pixels Designer, Instructor and future modes from one chat, with mode independence from Harnesses, mixed-Harness parallel execution, cross-agent review, and user-defined or Maestro-composed Fleets.
- [ ] **S8-29.** Multi-Harness Debate: Independent Proposals → Cross-Examination → Rebuttal → Evidence Review → Final Synthesis, with Consensus / Majority / Evidence-based Decision / No Consensus outcomes, user-set rounds and per-participant Harness/Model/Effort, live messages in the same chat, Debate History, and a read-only default that requires explicit authorization before any recommendation is applied. Tests cover the minority-evidence case so a majority cannot certify a false claim.

## Phase 9 — Browser AI Accounts (owner-approved 2026-11-03; future milestone, not a v0.3.9 gate)

**Decision history, kept so nobody re-litigates it:** the owner first asked for an account-connection feature, then asked to park it, then issued a final decision that **supersedes both** — "Browser-based AI Accounts with Manual Conversations + Authorized Maestro & Modes Browser Interaction is APPROVED." It is approved product direction, recorded here as a properly scoped feature, and the owner directed that it must not be archived or re-parked, nor reconfirmed. It stays out of the v0.3.9 release gates: no v0.3.9 item may wait on it, and nothing here may be shipped by widening an existing gate.

Approved experience, two independently declared capabilities per provider:

**A. Manual conversation.** Each provider's official site opens inside Greeneek's own browser surface — not an external link-out and not an API key — where the user signs in through the provider's normal flow, including Free accounts, uses the site's own features, and keeps that session across restarts where the browser supports persistence.

**B. Authorized browser interaction.** Maestro and permitted Modes navigate, type, submit, and read visible responses in a connected session **only where that provider's current terms and the technical reality allow it**, behind an explicit capability declaration and Greeneek's existing approval/permission model for sensitive or externally consequential actions. Manual interaction stays available when automation is not.

Logging a provider in does **not** itself authorize automation. Before any provider's automation is built, its current official terms, policies, and technical restrictions are researched and the result recorded as a per-provider capability matrix; where automation is not permitted or not reliable, Greeneek ships Manual Mode and reports the exact limitation rather than approximating it. Never bypass CAPTCHA, MFA, anti-bot or access controls; never drive private or undocumented endpoints to imitate an official integration; never present unsupported capability as working.

### The distinction this feature rests on

**ModelProvider** — direct model inference. Today: `SubagentProvider` is *not* this; the inference seam is `packages/llm/llm`'s adapter registry (its absence answers `no adapter registered for provider "…"`), the upstream route catalog in `packages/llm/llm-pi-ai/src/catalog.ts` (which already lists `openrouter`), and the Greeneek adapter in `packages/llm/llm-greeneek`. Secrets live in `packages/credentials/credentials-local`; approvals in `packages/credentials/authorization`; the user surface is the Models settings section plus the first-run credential step.

**AgentProvider** — CLI or in-harness agent execution, where the *vendor's own product* authenticates. Today: `packages/subagent/subagent-claude-code`, `subagent-codex`, `subagent-acp`, `subagent-gnk-sdk`, and the in-process providers, all registered through `ctx.subagents.registerProvider()` and capability-gated by `SubagentProvider.capabilities` / `prepareContinuable`.

A connection is therefore one of two kinds, and the Settings UI must say which, because what "connected" proves differs: a ModelProvider connection authorizes Greeneek to call an endpoint; an AgentProvider connection authorizes Maestro to delegate to a program the user installed and signed into.

### Compliance constraint, verified 2026-11-03 (this is a hard requirement, not a preference)

- **Anthropic** — its published Claude Code legal/compliance guidance states Anthropic does not permit third-party developers to offer Claude.ai login in their own applications or to route requests through Free, Pro, Max, or Ultra subscription plans; OAuth tokens are restricted to first-party surfaces (code.claude.com/docs/en/legal-and-compliance; reporting of the change 2026-02 and 2026-04).
- **Google** — explicitly prohibits third-party tools from using Gemini CLI / Antigravity product OAuth to reach the backend services powering those products (reported 2026-09-18); the Gemini API authenticates with API keys (ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/api-key).
- **OpenAI** — the public API authenticates with API keys; ChatGPT OAuth exists for Codex-specific services through OpenAI's own client, and OpenAI has extended Codex seats across paid ChatGPT plans (developers.openai.com; OpenAI Services Agreement 2025-12-01).

**Three concepts, kept separate.** `ModelProvider` (API-based inference), `AgentProvider` (native and external agent execution through a vendor's own CLI), and `BrowserAIProvider` (an authenticated browser session for manual use and, where authorized, automation). A browser-account session is never merged into the inference or agent-execution registries, and a ModelProvider connection is never a precondition for Manual Mode.

**What the findings settle and what they do not.** They settle that Greeneek must not extract a provider's subscription token into its own HTTP client to imitate an official integration: the inference path stays key-based, and subscription-backed *agent execution* goes through the vendor's own CLI, which is already how `subagent-claude-code` / `subagent-codex` work. They do **not** by themselves decide Manual Mode — a user signing in inside Greeneek's own isolated browser profile is a different act from a third party calling a subscription backend — so each provider's embedded-browser and automation permission is researched and recorded separately in the capability matrix before any part B ships. Where the answer is no, Manual Mode only, with the exact limitation reported. Any change here re-verifies current terms rather than trusting this row.

### Scope

- **AC-01** Connection records: a typed connection kind (`model-provider` | `agent-provider`), vendor, credential reference (never the secret itself), display label, enabled flag, and last-verified status with reason, persisted through the existing `credentials-local` store and projected to Settings.
- **AC-02** Verification without spend: each connection has a probe that answers `ready` / `unauthenticated` / `not-installed` / `unsupported-version` / `temporarily-unavailable` from registry and configuration facts, matching the vocabulary `fleetProviderAvailability` already publishes; no probe may issue a paid or state-changing call.
- **AC-03** Model availability, not model invention: an authorized ModelProvider connection contributes its routable models to the same catalog the Models section already renders; Maestro and native modes select through the existing route resolution. No parallel catalog.
- **AC-04** Agent availability: an AgentProvider connection appears in the fleet member provider picker only when `ctx.subagents` registers it, and only with the capabilities its descriptor advertises — the rule the hybrid decision already enforces for fleets.
- **AC-05** Connection UI: one Settings section listing both kinds with the distinction stated, add/verify/disable/delete, and the reason for every non-ready state. Product copy through the locale dictionaries in all three languages, as `verify-client-ui-i18n` requires.
- **AC-06** Security: secrets never in logs, cards, session history, or chat; per-connection authority stays inside the existing permission model; deleting a connection revokes routing immediately, not on next boot.
- **AC-07** Orchestration integration: Maestro's capability registry (S8-01) consumes connection state, so an unavailable vendor is named as unavailable with an alternative offered rather than silently skipped.
- **AC-08** Browser session manager: one persistent profile per provider with isolated cookies, local storage, and browser state, so no provider can read another's session; connect, disconnect, logout, and clear-session operations; statuses `connected` / `login-required` / `session-expired` / `unavailable`, with expiry and login-required handled rather than surfaced as a dead pane. No password is ever collected, stored, or requested by Greeneek, and no authentication cookie is extracted, copied, or transferred into Greeneek's application or agent state.
- **AC-09** Manual browser UI: the provider opens in Greeneek's own browser surface with the session status and the connect/disconnect/logout/clear actions, and manual interaction stays available when automation is unavailable.
- **AC-10** Independent capability declaration: manual interaction and authorized automation are declared separately per provider (`BrowserAIProvider` descriptor), so a provider that supports only Manual Mode can never be asked to automate, and the request is refused by name rather than attempted and misreported.
- **AC-11** Permission-gated automation: Maestro and Modes reach a session only through a permission-controlled interface that never receives the raw profile or credential state; navigation, typing, submission, and reading visible responses require the user's approval wherever the action is sensitive or has an external consequence, reusing the existing approval model rather than a parallel one.
- **AC-12** Hard boundaries, enforced in code and tested: no CAPTCHA, MFA, anti-bot, or access-control bypass; no private or undocumented endpoint used to imitate an official integration; no claim of a capability the matrix does not record.
- **AC-13** Sequenced delivery, research first: per-provider terms and technical research and the capability matrix; architecture and ADRs; the `BrowserAIProvider` contract and capability model; the isolated session manager; Manual UI and authentication flow; authorized interaction and its permission model; Maestro and Modes integration; then security, integration, and end-to-end tests. Automation for one provider must not be assumed from another's result.

### Dependencies and placement

Blocked on nothing outside this repository, but sequenced after Phase 8's execution layer because AC-03/AC-04/AC-07 consume it: AC-01/AC-02/AC-06 first (data + verification + security), then AC-03 (catalog), then AC-05 (UI with its goldens), then AC-04 and AC-07. It is a v0.4 candidate, not a v0.3.9 release blocker; nothing in v0.3.9 may wait on it, and no part of it may be shipped by widening an existing gate.

### Acceptance criteria

A user adds an API-key ModelProvider connection, sees exactly the models that key can route verified without a paid call, and selects one for a Maestro step; a user with a vendor CLI installed sees that agent as fleet-eligible with its real capabilities and refuses mailbox-style continuation if its provider lacks a durable lane; no UI path exists that sends a subscription OAuth token to a Greeneek-owned inference request; every connection state survives a restart and a delete takes effect immediately; provider terms are cited at implementation time, not from this table.

## Sequence and parallelism

| Order | Phase | Gate before advancing |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Phase 0 (green CI) | Whole `CI` workflow green twice on one SHA |
| 2 | Phase 1 (CTC) | All S1 boxes ticked with evidence; two-commit split (protocol, then copy) |
| 3 | Phases 2 + 3 + 4 | Independent files — may run in parallel lanes; each lane merges only with its gates green |
| 4 | Phase 5 | Needs Phase 1 done where it touches Dashboard lane metadata; otherwise parallel with 2–4 |
| 5 | Phase 6 | Whenever owner inputs arrive; unblocks S5/S7 evidence |
| 6 | Phase 7 | Every non-blocked box ticked; every blocked box named with its missing input |

Phase 8 (including the Harness architecture subsection above) is not a seventh sequential phase: its items are part of acceptance criterion 7 and are gated by the same Phase 0 → Phase 1 order, landing in PR-sized slices alongside Phases 5–7. Item 8 remains the final item. The table is not renumbered so no milestone is reordered.

Lane-assignment guidance: the main session (strong model) owns Phase 0 diagnosis, CTC protocol (S1-01…S1-08), npm publish/recovery logic (S2-06…S2-09), worktree/security-adjacent gates, and release steps. Flash-tier subagents may take mechanical, well-specified slices (locale string renames, CSS/docs sweeps, catalog regeneration, fixture updates) under review. Agent waves never commit or push.

## Execution protocol for the implementing AI model

1. Read first: root `AGENTS.md`, `packages/AGENTS.md`, `packages/client/AGENTS.md`, `docs/AGENTS.md`, `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/testing.md`, and the owning package READMEs. Verify every path and command in this plan against the tree before editing — source wins for facts, this plan wins for scope; record any conflict and ask before dropping a requirement.
2. Work directly on `main` in PR-sized vertical slices; never start a new slice while the current slice's focused tests are red. Never a giant mixed release branch.
3. Reproduce each defect before fixing it; record the failing command, error text, commit, and environment. Write the regression test first where practical — it must fail on the old behavior and pass on the new.
4. Never weaken a test, add a blind retry, raise a timeout without a diagnosed cause, or delete a failing assertion to green a gate.
5. Generated artifacts, source changes, tests, docs, and this ledger update land in the same commit when they form one contract.
6. After every wave: scoped `vitest --coverage`, `verify-client-domain-graph`, `verify-client-ui-i18n`, `verify-cordis-config`, `verify-generated-artifacts` as applicable, then append to the Execution ledger below with exact commit, changed files, commands, results, blockers, and next action. A successor must be able to resume from the ledger alone.
7. Tick a box only per the checkbox contract; when blocked, annotate `BLOCKED: <missing input>` inline and move on — do not stall the program.
8. Never commit credentials, `.env`, personal paths, or untracked owner material (`.analysis/`, `.kimi-work/`, `working files/`). Greeneek-endpoint live lanes self-skip without `GREENEEK_API_KEY`; other providers self-skip without their own key; Greeneek starts without any key.
9. Keep tags immutable. Delete a branch only after its unique commits are merged, tagged, or explicitly accepted as disposable.
10. Stop and ask the owner when an OWN-* decision is unresolved and blocks the slice, when a real app/key is unavailable, or when a security boundary cannot be enforced in the operation that makes the decision.

## Parallel maintenance track (not a v0.3.9 gate)

The `gnk-code-review` human-review maintenance note describes private, maintainer-machine infrastructure with observed runs (2026-07-15: 62 PRs scanned, 0 candidates). Its remaining acceptance items — scheduler with durable notification, the controlled target-branch advancement case, promote-helper rejection on a changed source blob, and one operator-promoted PR — continue independently of this release. The repository file it may change is only `.agents/skills/gnk-code-review/SKILL.md`, via a reviewed draft PR. It blocks nothing here and nothing here blocks it.

## Alternatives considered

**Ship v0.3.8 first and put this program in v0.3.9.** Rejected: no `desktop-v0.3.8` tag exists, and the CTC protocol change and the CI gate are exactly what v0.3.8 was to carry, so splitting them would leave two half-finished vehicles. The owner's instruction to release v0.3.9 makes it the single vehicle.

**Keep the twenty planning documents and work from whichever one a session reads.** Rejected: that is the reported failure — overlapping, partly contradictory scope and no shared status. The merged plan is the working inventory; the older documents stay as history and evidence.

**Sequence the owner-correction rework after the release.** Rejected: the six ruled-wrong surfaces are the product the owner asked for, and releasing them unchanged would ship a known-wrong shape. They are Phase 5, gated behind CI and the protocol change only.

**Treat the Maestro mission as a separate future release.** Rejected by the owner's paste into this plan and its "implement this inside the existing application" instruction. It is Phase 8 here, with its definition of done carried as acceptance items rather than deferred to a document.

## Acceptance criteria

1. `main` is fully green (all CI jobs) on the tagged SHA, with the consumer-matrix failure root-caused and guarded.
2. The control reads CTC everywhere; enable→disable→enable works in one session; every model request in a toggling session is reconstructable from the log; both SDKs' expected outputs match; preset composition-time declarations stay immune to user re-declare.
3. CTC's tool set equals the useful PTC set, enforced by a composition test; no `ptc` preset remains; the deletion census is recorded; stored sessions fail loudly or migrate as documented.
4. The npm baseline packs, validates, and installed-tests the complete `@greeneek/*` set before any registry write; publish resumes idempotently from one immutable manifest; the protected CI publish job uses the identical tested bundle.
5. Package inventories, dependency rules, adapter conformance, license inventory, and (per OWN-05) API reports are mechanical gates; adding a package or a public API change is a visible, gated event.
6. The docs quality reference exists and `docs/AGENTS.md` is under target; the generated-reference prototype shows measured lookup improvement; the tool-owned-render package typechecks on master.
7. The owner-correction rework is complete: the Dashboard is a full page with every metric reconciled; the Arabic text passes native-level review or a recorded waiver; Maestro visibly dispatches the other modes and all agents stream in a dedicated right sidebar; Army shows Kimi-style decomposition and parallel work; fleet favorites work from Settings and from one sentence; Pixels Designer and Instructor ship as FULL modes without preview labels; the ArtCraft gateway auto-detects installed apps and generates capabilities from live registries. Fleet recording, performance budgets, product inventory pass two, security negatives, and P0/P1 triage are complete or carry named blockers with owner decisions.
8. `desktop-v0.3.9` carries both platforms, updater metadata, checksums, and a manifest; an independently downloaded artifact installs, launches, and restarts; release notes describe the CTC rename, PTC removal, and every blocked lane honestly.
9. Every blocked lane is named as blocked with its missing input; nothing is claimed from a mock.
10. The Execution ledger below lets a fresh session resume without reading any other document.

## Risks

| Risk | Control |
|---|---|
| Reversible presentation breaks replay determinism | Per-change session event + ordered replay + toggle-replay test (S1-04, S1-08) |
| Deleting PTC strands a session the census missed | Re-run the census at deletion time; stored sessions must fail loudly (S1-19, S1-22) |
| Rename churns web goldens | Touch only goldens whose text moves; review every diff (S1-13) |
| Label and protocol changes land together and hide a regression | Two commits: protocol first, copy second |
| Expected-output diagnosis turns into env-shopping | The job's environment is pinned by a workflow policy test either way (S0-02) |
| npm partial-publish visibility | Immutable bundle + idempotent recovery; final whole-set verification is the availability signal (S2-08) |
| Discovery scripts become too clever | Boring implementation rule: manifests in, resolved list out, fail loud (S3-01) |
| Dep-cruiser rule rot as packages are added | Pattern-based rules, never enumerated package lists (S3-02) |
| Vendor drift check blocked by private upstream | Fall back to a local scheduled agent task; never silently skip (S3-07) |
| Generated-reference split causes route/link churn | Prototype with aliases and measured lookup cost before any corpus-wide split (S4-02) |
| API drift stales the tool-owned-render port | Acceptance re-checked against master at port time (S4-03) |
| Live lanes get quietly dropped | Phase 6 names them; release notes must list them as blocked (S7-07) |
| Ledger drift misleads the next session | Ledger updates in the same commit as the work (protocol rule 6) |
| Owner corrections get re-litigated by a later session | The corrections section explicitly supersedes older DONE-VERIFIED rows; do-not-reopen directives recorded beside them |
| Sidebar panel fights the details-column arbitration | S5-11a reuses the Fleet engine but registers a real sidebar surface; auto-open semantics tested (S5-11e) so tool clicks keep stronger intent |
| Auto-detect gateway trusts app registries blindly | Capabilities generated from live `command_list` responses only, cached + drift-hashed, drift disables recipes until reviewed (S5-09b) |
| FULL-mode scope balloons beyond the installed apps | Flows degrade honestly per app state; preview label removal gated on real evidence, not prose (S5-10a/d) |
| Arabic rewrite regresses the three-locale parity gate | Values change, keys do not — the typed parity contract stays enforced throughout (S5-03a) |

### 2026-11-10 — a genuine multi-agent Debate runs live, and the live run's three defects are fixed at the root

- **Starting state verified, not inherited.** `git status --short --branch` → `## main...origin/main [ahead 14]`, HEAD `dbb256a0`, 449 dirty paths (450 after this session). Local only; no commit, push, tag, publish, or release.
- **What shipped.** `packages/subagent/agent-team/src/index.ts`: debate collection is turn-scoped instead of reply-count-scoped — every `spawn`/`send` records the persisted log length before it queues the prompt, and `collectSeatReplies` accepts only the first `turn/start` at or after `max(boundary, inheritedEventCount)`, requiring that turn's own `turn/end` before its text is returned. `describeTurnEnd` names the terminal stop condition (`TEAM_DEBATE_EMPTY_TURN` for a turn without text, `TEAM_DEBATE_MEMBER_FAILED` for a turn that produced text and then failed or aborted). `conductDebate` preflights before the first seat exists: duplicate participant names, a moderator name colliding with a participant, a name already owned by a roster teammate (`TEAM_MEMBER_NAME_TAKEN`), and a debate whose seats would exceed `maxMembers` (`TEAM_MEMBER_LIMIT`). `packages/subagent/agent-team/src/debate.ts`: the verdict parser strips the markdown a chat model wraps its answer in (block and list markers, emphasis around the label, emphasis around the value) and drops a reported value that says nothing was reported (`none`, `n/a`, `no unresolved disagreements`). `packages/subagent/agent-team/tests/debate-collection.spec.ts` is the new regression gate; `debate.spec.ts` gained the markdown-verdict case.
- **Live evidence (the real product, not a mock).** `pnpm gnk --profile web --port 8212 --no-open` served the fixed source; the driver drove the shipped RPC surface (`session/create` → `session/selectModel` → `session/prompt`). The Lead called the shipped `conduct_debate` tool with `alpha` on `spawn`, `beta` on `fork`, and `moderator` on `spawn`, `max_rounds: 1`. The published result carried 13 entries — two `position`, two `objection`, two `evidence`, one `synthesis` — each with its participant and source Harness (`alpha`/`spawn`, `beta`/`fork`, `moderator`/`spawn`), `failures: []`, `verdictMode: "consensus"`, `consensus: true`, `unresolved: []`, `minorityEvidence: []`, and a synthesis that cites both seats' evidence. The moderator's raw text was `**VERDICT:** consensus **CONSENSUS:** yes **UNRESOLVED:** (none — no disagreements were raised) **MINORITY-EVIDENCE:** (none — no minority positions were advanced)` — the same shape that the pre-fix parser mis-read as `no-consensus`. The second debate in the same session, with the same speaking names, was refused with `TEAM_MEMBER_NAME_TAKEN` (`debate seat "alpha" reuses a teammate name already in this Team`) and created no roster member: the `team/member` event count was 6 before and 6 after. The model's own recovery (a third debate with `alice`/`bob`/`chair`) spawned its seats normally.
- **The three live defects, before and after.** (1) The moderator's synthesis returned the inherited `READY-LIVE` prefix instead of its own turn: the old collector counted assistant messages, so a fork child's inherited prefix satisfied it. (2) A seat whose reply settled between `send` and `collect` was dropped as "no new output". (3) A repeated debate hit `TEAM_MEMBER_NAME_TAKEN` and then accumulated partial seats until `TEAM_MEMBER_LIMIT`, leaving a half-populated roster. Each is fixed at the collection or preflight site above, with a regression test.
- **Measured.** `vitest run packages/subagent/agent-team/tests/debate.spec.ts packages/subagent/agent-team/tests/debate-collection.spec.ts` → 13 passed; `vitest run packages/subagent/tool-agent-team/tests` → 31 passed; `vitest run packages/subagent/agent-team/tests` → 250 passed, 1 skipped, with the one failure in `worktrees.spec.ts` (`removes the checkout and branch, and repeated removal succeeds`) timing out at 5 s under concurrent load and passing when run alone; `pnpm run typecheck` → exit 0; `pnpm run lint` → exit 0; `pnpm run test:docs` → 14 gates passed; `verify-agent-note-format` → 753 notes conform. The two Agent Notes that describe the shipped behaviour (`2026-11-08-live-debate-collection-contract.md`, `2026-11-09-debate-five-round-moderator-verdicts.md`) were updated in place; no note is superseded by this work.

### 2026-11-10 — the desktop app carries the live debate, and the name-reuse refusal is proven there too

- **The desktop app was launched and driven as the product, not the web GUI.** `pnpm --filter @greeneek/desktop run start` opened the Electron shell; its bundled service booted `gnk web` on a loopback port inside the desktop's own harness home, and the desktop's Settings, credentials, session, and Team surfaces answered the shipped RPC surface. The Kilo Gateway route was added through the product's own Settings API (`settings/update` on the `llm-pi-ai` namespace) and the key through the product's own credential store (`credentials/set` for `KILO_API_KEY`), which the desktop's service then reported as `configured: true, source: file` — the BYOK path, exercised live.
- **A genuine multi-agent debate ran on the desktop app.** The Lead called the shipped `conduct_debate` tool with `alpha` on `spawn`, `beta` on `fork`, and a `moderator` on `spawn`, `max_rounds: 1`. The published result carried **seven entries** — two positions, two objections, two evidence turns, and the moderator's synthesis — each with its participant and source Harness (`alpha`/`spawn`, `beta`/`fork`, `moderator`/`spawn`), with `verdictMode: consensus`, `consensus: true`, `unresolved: []`, `minorityEvidence: []`, and **`failures: []`**. The moderator's raw synthesis began `**VERDICT: consensus** **CONSENSUS: yes**`, so the parser that once read that as `no-consensus` now reads it as a consensus, live. The session log shows the whole machinery: `team/member` (6), `team/message/queued` (5), `team/message/delivered` (5), `tool/call` (1), `tool/result` (1), and a turn that ended `completed`.
- **The name-reuse refusal is proven live on the desktop app too.** A second debate in the same session with the same seat names was refused with `TeamError` / `TEAM_MEMBER_NAME_TAKEN` — "debate seat \"alpha\" reuses a teammate name already in this Team; give each debate distinct speaking names" — and the roster did not grow: the session's `team/member` events stayed at the same six (three seats, provisioning and active). No partial team was left behind.

### 2026-11-10 — the branch's generated artifacts and build gates were stale; each stale artifact is named and regenerated

- **A full rebuild found four branch defects that the incremental build had been hiding.** `packages/llm/llm-pi-ai/src/catalog.ts` failed its own compile-time gate — `EveryProfileFieldMatchesUpstream` resolves to `false` — because pi-ai 1.5.0 spells an optional compat field as `T | undefined` while the profile states the value it accepts (`T`) and marks the field optional, so the "same types in both directions" comparison compared the two spellings instead of the two values. The comparison now reads each field's value type (`FieldValues<T>`), which keeps the gate's meaning — a wider or narrower profile still fails — and ignores the optional spelling. The same sweep found the tool catalog generator refusing to run at all (`gen-tool-catalog: 1 tool package(s) not in the boot manifest: tool-owned-render`), because the branch added the browser-side `packages/client/tool-owned-render` and the generator's `packages/*/tool-*` glob counted it as a Host tool package; the generator now skips the client group, the plugin catalog's Tool kind keeps the same scope (`docs/plugin-catalog.md` regenerated, 24 → 24 tools with the client package under Capability), and `packages/core/tools/tests/gen-tool-catalog.spec.ts` now lists `conduct_debate` — the tool this branch ships — among the harvested schemas. `scripts/gen-tsconfig-paths.ts` refused the new client package for the same reason (`@greeneek/gnk-client-tool-owned-render` is named after its role, not its directory), so `tsconfig.base.json` carries the hand-written alias the generator asks for, and `scripts/gen-third-party-notices.ts` no longer treats `vendor/.patches` (the local patch store this branch moved it to) as a vendored package.
- **Two load-sensitive specs were given a process budget instead of a wall clock.** `packages/subagent/agent-team/tests/worktrees.spec.ts` and `scripts/oxlint-contract.spec.ts` each spawn real `git`/`oxlint` children; on a contended host those calls alone exceed the default 5 s, so both files now budget 30 s (`vi.setConfig({ testTimeout: 30_000 })`) — the same shape the worker-thread specs already use. The same sweep found five more files with the same shape — `scripts/change-scope.spec.ts` (git), `scripts/benchmark-npm-resolution.spec.ts` (npm), `packages/experimental/inspector/tests/integration.host.spec.ts` (a real Worker), `packages/subagent/agent-team/tests/team.spec.ts` and `packages/subagent/agent-team/tests/integration.spec.ts` (real worktrees and merges) — each of which passes when it runs alone and times out inside a full-suite run. All seven now budget the process work. No assertion was weakened anywhere.
- **Measured.** `pnpm run typecheck` → exit 0 (a full rebuild, not an incremental one); `pnpm run lint` → exit 0; `pnpm run test:docs` → 14 gates passed; `pnpm run verify-generated-catalogs` → the cordis, tool, and plugin catalogs are current; `vitest run packages/llm/llm-pi-ai/tests` → 12 files, 289 passed; `vitest run packages/core/tools/tests/gen-tool-catalog.spec.ts` → 10 passed; `vitest run scripts/gen-plugin-catalog.spec.ts scripts/gen-tsconfig-paths.spec.ts scripts/gen-third-party-notices.spec.ts` → 34 passed.
- **The unit suite now ends with two failures, both named.** `pnpm exec vitest run` → 1100 files, 2 failed, 1094 passed, 4 skipped; 17475 tests, 2 failed, 17400 passed, 73 skipped. The first is `packages/core/agent-loop/tests/resume.spec.ts > a pending idle inject() survives persist + resume without a synthetic turn`: `SessionAlreadyOwnedError: session "inject-sess" is already owned by an active write handle`. The write lease is process-scoped and its holder check treats the current process as alive, so a second in-process runtime can never write a session the first runtime still owns; releasing the lease needs the first runtime's teardown, and that teardown drains the pending inbox the case wants to survive. The case needs a subprocess harness (a real restart) or a lease-release seam that does not drain the agent — not a widened assertion. The second is `packages/shell/tool-pwsh-persistent/tests/loader-composition.spec.ts > preserves cwd and environment across calls`: the persistent pwsh shell prints the right output (`line one:it's fine`) but also echoes its own prompt markers and never returns, so the call ends in the 60 s timeout with partial output. It reproduces in isolation, so it is a Windows-host defect in the persistent pwsh shell's prompt handling, not a load artifact.

### 2026-11-10 — the web browser lane measured on POSIX and Windows, and every failure is classified

- **Two lanes, two verdicts.** The Windows host replay (`GNK_SNAPSHOT=replay pnpm exec vitest run --config vitest.web.config.ts`) produced 21 failed files / 45 failed tests of 322. The same lane on Linux in the POSIX container (`gnk-posix-priv`, bubblewrap sandbox usable) produced **11 failed files / 18 failed tests** — the 27 extra Windows failures are the platform's shell-tool mismatch (the recorded fixtures call `bash`; Windows composes `pwsh`), not product defects. The Linux column is the authoritative one; the Windows column is a portability signal, not a gate.
- **The container tree was stale, and that changed the verdict.** The POSIX copy of the tree was 11 files behind the host, including `packages/client/ui-chat/src/client/chat/ChatView.tsx` (it still referenced a `recordProbe` that the current source does not have) and `packages/client/ui-workspace/src/client/DashboardPage.tsx`. The first Linux replay therefore measured older client code. After syncing those files (hash-compared against the host), rebuilding, and re-running the failing files in isolation, `hmr-live.e2e.ts` passes (its failure was the missing `.gnk-build/client-build-environment.json`, a build-record issue) and the remaining failures are 17. Any Linux measurement of this branch must hash-compare the container tree against the host first.
- **S0-15 is closed.** `apps/web/tests/plan-control-row.e2e.ts` → 2/2 pass on Linux ("keeps the plan chip and model trigger disjoint and exits plan mode by click"). The 800×720 overlap the row recorded does not reproduce on the POSIX lane. `apps/web/tests/workspace-management.e2e.ts` → 12/12 pass on Linux, including "archives the seeded session from its row menu", which fails on Windows only.
- **S0-16 is a real product defect, confirmed on both lanes.** `apps/web/tests/chat-scroll-contract.e2e.ts` → "keeps streaming ownership and tool disclosure state across a long scroll-away cycle" fails on Linux and Windows with `flow row call:chat-scroll-live-tool-call moved relative to the transcript viewport: expected 19 to be less than or equal to 2` (scroll ownership leaks while a live tool row streams). It is not the sandbox: the scenario only reaches that assertion where the bash tool runs (privileged Linux), and it reproduces there. The sibling fling test passes once the sandbox works, so the fling behavior is sound and the leak is specific to the live tool row.
- **S0-16's cause is now measured, and one defect inside it is fixed.** A timeline of the transcript geometry (recorded from the POSIX lane at 60 ms samples) shows two symptom modes: (a) right after the live turn starts, the transcript sits 212 px above the new floor, and the follow re-pins about a second later; (b) after the reader scrolls away, the live row's screen position moves by exactly the height of the content change above it (19 px), with the reader's own row never compensated. The defect behind it is in `packages/client/ui-chat/src/client/chat/ChatView.tsx`: the scroll handler held the reader's row only when one was already held (`else if (anchorRef.current !== null && position !== null)`), so a reader who scrolled away from the bottom never had a row held at all — the prepend path's documented compensation ("preserve the same settled row at the position established by the reader's latest scroll") could therefore never fire for them. The handler now captures the reader's row when they leave the bottom, and a `ResizeObserver` callback restores that row's screen position after any height change above it. This does not yet close the scenario: the 19 px leak remains, because the row the reader stopped on and the row the scenario measures can be different rows, and the compensation lands on the held one. The remaining work is to make the ownership rule cover the row the reader is actually looking at — not to widen the tolerance.
- **S1-20 is fixture drift, and the file says so.** `apps/web/tests/ptc-round.e2e.ts` header: "its committed fixture still records the pre-repoint composition, so replay stays BLOCKED until a POSIX re-record refreshes it". The current composition ships the code runtime (`packages/bundle/web-app/cordis.patch.yml` mounts `@greeneek/gnk-code-runtime-worker-thread`), so the recorded `run_code` call now dispatches real sub-calls (`tool/code-dispatch`: bash → `CODE_ROUND_OK`, read → `cannot read "missing.txt": not found`) instead of recording `unknown tool "run_code"`. The POSIX re-record showed exactly that diff; the fixture must still come from a `GNK_SNAPSHOT=record` run with a model key, and the system-prompt golden it refreshes grows by 538 lines, which is a reviewed change, not a silent one.
- **The rest, classified (17 Linux failures).** Real product defects: S0-16 (chat scroll ownership), `queue-actions.e2e.ts` ("orders Todo before Goal and Queue on one responsive card column" — the Todo panel and the Goal panel are not on one column: the Todo panel sits at x=312 and the Goal panel at x=272.6, 39 px apart, and the scenario requires them to share an x and a width; the same file passes inside a full-lane run and fails alone, so the panels' column depends on state the run order decides), `workflow-run.e2e.ts` ("shows the live member, opens its local child…" — the member button reports "element is not stable" while the workflow runs, so the click never lands), and `onboarding-greeneek-config.e2e.ts` ("never paints the takeover chrome on a configured reload" — the coordinator re-enters its deciding loop, collecting 77 `chrome`/`inert` entries where the scenario expects none). Golden drift that matches the branch's intentional changes: `lifecycle-chrome.e2e.ts` (the details column no longer carries the Fleet tab — the panel shows its plain title unless a wave occupies the Fleet seat, which is owner correction 5's "Fleet out of the details column"), `models-settings.e2e.ts` and `onboarding-usable-provider.e2e.ts` (the provider list follows the pi-ai 1.5.0 catalog: "Azure OpenAI" is now "Azure" and "OpenAI Codex" is gone), `ptc-round.e2e.ts`, and `onboarding-greeneek-config.e2e.ts`'s credential dialog. The `smoke-real` failures are the lane meeting a fresh home: the scenarios expect a configured workspace and find the first-run "Choose a workspace" gate, so they are an environment difference, not a product defect; the same files pass on a home that has been set up. The `web-search-round` trio is S0-21. One real product defect remains in the browser lane: S0-16.

The same debate in the live app answers every round (the published run carried 13 entries — two positions, two objections, two evidence turns, and the moderator's synthesis), so the gap is in the harness composition, not in the product. ### 2026-11-06 — S0-21 root-caused: the search scenario never moves the shipped provider pin

- **Measured cause.** `apps/web/tests/web-search-round.e2e.ts` starts a local Anthropic-compatible search server and patches `web-search-greeneek` to it, but `packages/bundle/base/cordis.patch.yml:481-485` pins `web.searchProvider: duckduckgo`, and the scaffold's `greeneekSearch` option patches only the provider row's endpoint and credential reference. The runner therefore records zero requests at the local server. On this host: `GNK_SNAPSHOT=replay` → `uses the real provider and persists the capped structured result` fails with `expected [] to have a length of 2`; the other two cases time out 10s/30s because the first aborts before the turn it drives.
- **Why changing the pin alone is not the fix.** The fixture's one `tool/result` carries `No results found. Refine the query, or configure a search endpoint…`, the sentence `formatSearchOutput` emits for an empty result — the DuckDuckGo-offline outcome. The local server returns four sources per query, so the live turn would no longer match `snapshots/web/web-search-round/session.jsonl` or `ui.expected.md`. Both need a keyed `GNK_SNAPSHOT=record` run, which `GREENEEK_API_KEY` gates. No file changed here; the classification and the blocked repair are the deliverable.
- **Cascade, not three defects.** The two timeouts wait for a search card the aborted turn never produced.

### 2026-11-06 — S8-21(4): a fleet member can carry a timeout, and a Harmony launch refuses it

The roster's existing rule is what enforces it: a continuable provider ("Harmony") refuses `timeoutMs` and a one-shot provider ("Melody") honours it, so a saved timeout on a lane is a loud launch failure rather than a setting that is stored and ignored.

### 2026-11-05 — Harmony and Melody stop being called modes, and Fleet providers get product names

- **The naming rule the owner set.** Harmony and Melody must not be confusable with the agent modes. Nothing that a person or the model reads now calls them modes: the Fleet dictionaries renamed `fleet.modes.both|melody|help` to `fleet.execution.*` in `en`, `zh`, and `ar`, and the help sentence reads "…These two names say how a Fleet member runs; they are not providers, and they are not the agent modes beside the composer." The model-facing Team policy in `packages/subagent/tool-agent-team/src/index.ts` now opens "The two ways a teammate runs are named Harmony and Melody." — that text reaches every Lead and teammate request, and `rg` over `packages`, `apps`, `snapshots` and `docs` found the retired sentence in no golden or snapshot, so no recorded output needed re-recording. `mailbox.ts`'s report comment and `docs/subsystems/agent-team.md` now say "run"/"run type"; the persisted `mode` field, `TeamMemberMode`, and the `team/member` schema keep `lane`/`one-shot`, and `ui-subagent`'s `mode.oneShot`/`mode.continuable` keys keep their identifiers while their rendered values stay `Melody`/`Harmony`.
- **Why the page showed `fork` and `spawn`.** Those are the composition's two built-in in-process providers — `fork` seeds the child from this session's completed turns, `spawn` starts one with no inherited context — and a fleet member has to run on something, so they are not removable. They appeared as raw ids because the registry only publishes a name, and `SubagentProvider` has no display field. The page now names them from the client dictionary: `fleet.provider.fork` = "Greeneek built-in (shares this session)", `fleet.provider.spawn` = "Greeneek built-in (fresh context)", `fleet.provider.claudeCode` = "Claude Code", `fleet.provider.codex` = "Codex", with the registry id kept visible beside it because that is the value a saved fleet stores and cordis.yml configures. A registry name with no shipped label — an ACP adapter the deployment named, e.g. `opencode` — is shown as that name alone, and `providerName` is a required config field on `subagent-acp`/`subagent-gnk-sdk`, so the client has no product name to invent. `providerName()` also stops an unbranded row from printing the same string twice.
- **Other Fleet-page fixes in the same pass.** Availability now states `fleet.providersEmpty` ("This composition registers no provider.") instead of rendering a blank list in both the read-only and the normal branch; the member editor's Provider options, the starter preview lines, the saved-fleet member summary, and the pre-launch confirmation card all read the product name; the zh and ar dictionaries no longer embed the English word "provider" (提供方 / مزوّد), which is what `fleet.provider`, `fleet.availability`, and `fleet.description` now use.
- **Measured.** `pnpm exec vitest run --no-file-parallelism --coverage --coverage.perFile=true --coverage.include="packages/client/ui-agent-team/src/**" packages/client/ui-agent-team` → exit 0, 3 files, **59 passed** at the 100% per-file thresholds; `packages/subagent/tool-agent-team` → 2 files, **27 passed**. `tsc -b tsconfig.host.json`/`tsconfig.client.json` → 0; `pnpm run lint` → 0/0; `verify-client-ui-i18n` → 0; `pnpm run build` → 0; `pnpm run doc-sync` → **30 passed**; `apps/web/tests/settings-chrome.e2e.ts` → **11 passed**, so no Settings golden moved. Two defects were caught by the new tests rather than absorbed: `absent-cli` rendered its id twice once the row gained a `<code>` element, and the availability row and the saved-fleet member summary became ambiguous for the same string, which is why the row now suppresses the id it already shows and the test scopes its query to the availability list.
- **Real app (fresh build, port 8142, Playwright chromium on the shipped bundle).** Provider rows: `Greeneek built-in (shares this session)` with `code` `fork`, and `Greeneek built-in (fresh context)` with `spawn`, both `data-state="ready"` and labelled `Harmony and Melody`. The help paragraph reads the new sentence verbatim, ending "…they are not the agent modes beside the composer." Member-editor options: `Greeneek built-in (shares this session) (fork) — Harmony and Melody` and `Greeneek built-in (fresh context) (spawn) — Harmony and Melody`. Console: zero errors and zero warnings.

### 2026-11-05 — The mode control becomes a menu opened by the mode's own glyph, its description moves into each row, and nothing caps the row count

- **What the owner asked for and what shipped.** Two requests, both against the S8-19 control: open the mode list from the mode's logo, and put what each mode does under its name inside the list. `ModeSwitcher.tsx` now renders a `button` (aria-label `Agent mode`, `aria-haspopup="menu"`, `aria-expanded`) whose content is the staged mode's glyph and its name, and the list is the house `Menu` primitive in portal mode — the same one `WorkspacePicker` uses — so selection, Escape, outside click, and viewport-aware placement come from existing code rather than a second dropdown. Each row is `icon + name line + description line`, and `selectedId={current}` puts Menu's trailing check on the staged mode. The `<select>`/`<option>` pair is gone, so `docs/product-inventory.md`'s "mode dropdown" line still describes the surface and no catalog regenerated.
- **The caption is removed, not moved.** The owner's follow-up said the sentence beside the control is not wanted for any mode, so the `<p className={css.description}>`, its `useId`/`aria-describedby` wiring, the `.description` rule and its 720px hide are all deleted; `ModeSwitcherEntry.description` now has exactly one consumer, its own row. The trigger keeps the mode name because the glyph alone cannot say which mode a session runs — a dashed square, a pile of circles and a brain circuit read alike until the menu is open.
- **No limit on the number of modes.** `entries` maps straight to rows in the caller's order, and `AgentPresetSeat` already appends every roster option outside `MODE_ORDER`, so an owner who authors presets through Creator gets one row each; `Menu.module.css` caps the card at `max-width: 360px` and `.scrollable` gives it `max-height: calc(100vh - 24px)` with `overflow-y: auto`, so a long list scrolls instead of truncating. `renders one row per mode with no cap on how many the roster offers` pins it at 25 rows (1 shipped + 24 authored).
- **One test-side trap the new copy exposed.** Filtering rows by `hasText: 'Standard mode'` matched Creator too, because Creator's own description says "all Standard mode capabilities". Row lookup now matches the name line exactly (`filter({ has: getByText(name, { exact: true }) })`) in both `apps/web/tests/agent-preset-selection.e2e.ts` (`modeRow`/`pickMode`) and the component spec, which is the difference between picking Standard and picking Creator.
- **Measured.** `pnpm exec vitest run --no-file-parallelism --coverage…packages/client/ui-agent-preset packages/client/ui-conversation` → 36 files, **507 passed**, `ModeSwitcher.tsx` 100/100/100/100; `packages/client/ui-agent-preset/tests/components.client.spec.tsx` alone → **20 passed**. `tsc -b tsconfig.client.json` → 0; `pnpm run lint` → 0/0; `verify-client-ui-i18n` → 0; `verify-generated-artifacts` → 6 current; `pnpm run build` → 0. `GNK_SNAPSHOT=refresh` rewrote exactly three goldens (`apps/web/tests/expected/agent-preset-selection/hero.expected.md`, `snapshots/web/lifecycle-chrome/{hero,plan-active}.expected.md`); every hunk read before accepting — `combobox "Agent mode"` with its option list (8 rows in the agent-preset golden, 6 in each lifecycle golden) plus the description paragraph becomes `button "Agent mode": Standard mode`, in the same position after the workspace chip, and the paragraph line is deleted. Two consecutive `GNK_SNAPSHOT=replay` runs over `agent-preset-selection` + `lifecycle-chrome` → **15 passed / 15 passed**, and adding `settings-chrome` → **26 passed**.
- **Real app (fresh build, port 8142, started by this check; driven with Playwright chromium against the shipped bundle because the IDE browser view is out of daily quota).** Closed: role `button`, text `Standard mode`, `aria-haspopup=menu`, `aria-expanded=false`, `aria-describedby=null`, one svg in the trigger, and the hero row's whole text is `Choose workspace` + `Standard mode` — the description caption is gone (`getByText(/Full coding agent with file editing/)` count 0). Opened: 6 rows, each exactly 2 text lines (name then description), and the check icon (`[class*="check"]`) sits on the staged mode's row only. Picking Army put the check on Army's row and renamed the trigger `Army mode`; Escape closed the list with `aria-expanded=false`. Console: zero errors and zero warnings. One honest observation: a staged pick did not survive a page reload (the trigger read `Standard mode` again), which matches the documented rule that the stage belongs to the flow and is spent on first use — this increment does not claim persistence.

### 2026-11-05 — Starter fleets and Duplicate became real product features, `enabled`/`concurrency` became editable, and the `consumers` lane's first red SHA is now named

- **Remote surface.** `TeamService` gained three `@Remote` methods on the existing convention — `fleetStarters` (reads the shipped template catalog), `duplicateFleet(caller, id, expectedRevision)`, and `installStarterFleet(caller, templateId, expectedRevision)` — so `agentTeams` now exposes eight: `view`, `createTask`, `updateTask`, `fleetAvailability`, `fleetStarters`, `duplicateFleet`, `installStarterFleet`, `launchFleetFavorite`. Host work shipped as `f40a12cd`, the page as `4c4b51b9`. `fleetStarterTemplates()` returns deep copies, because the catalog is a module constant and a preview a caller can edit would poison every later install. The generated `packages/extensions/tool-cordis/src/api-catalog.ts` and `docs/subsystems/agent-team.md` were regenerated; `packages/subagent/agent-team/tests/built-lib.e2e.ts` now census-lists the eight descriptors, which is the guard that keeps a Remote addition from being claimed without the built gateway actually publishing it.
- **Authorization was inconsistent and is now fixed, not just extended.** `duplicateFleet` and `installStarterFleet` only required *membership*, while `saveFleetFavorite` and `deleteFleetFavorite` required the **Lead** — so a teammate could have rewritten the shared favorites section through the new surface. All four now go through one `requireFleetLead()` gate. `packages/subagent/agent-team/tests/team.spec.ts` adds `refuses a fleet mutation from a teammate`, asserting `TEAM_LEAD_REQUIRED` for both, and the Remote wrappers are covered by `remoteFleetStarters/remoteDuplicateFleet/remoteInstallStarterFleet` in the favorites test, whose assertions pin the resulting id order `['other-fleet', 'other-fleet-copy', 'development']`.
- **The page.** Each saved-fleet row gained `Duplicate`; a `Disabled` tag and a `N at once` tag; and a Launch control that is **blocked before the click** with the reason in its title (`Enable this fleet to launch it.`) instead of refusing afterwards. A new Starter fleets block previews every template's members verbatim (`implementer — write the change (spawn)`) with one Install per template. The editor gained the Enabled checkbox and the Members-at-once field, each with a sentence stating its practical effect, and both store the launcher's default as **absence of the key**: turning a fleet back on deletes `enabled`, and returning to 1 deletes `concurrency`, so a fleet's stored shape matches what it was before the field existed. Two defects were caught while writing that: a `{ ...draft, enabled: undefined }`-style spread cannot remove a key (`exactOptionalPropertyTypes` rejects it, and the value silently survives), so `withEnabled()`/`withConcurrency()` destructure the key off, and an emptied number field now reads as 1 rather than keeping a larger stored batch than the control shows. The concurrency field states its real ceiling instead of inventing one: the launcher batches by `concurrency` over the member list, so asking for more members than the fleet has means *all of them together*, and the description switches to `Asking for more than {count} starts every member together, because the fleet has no more members.` above the member count. No `max` attribute is set, because native range validation would block saving a batch larger than a draft that has not grown its members yet. All copy is locale-owned in `en`, `zh`, `ar`; 14 new keys.
- **Failure aggregation and the concurrency limit, both proven at the seam.** `launchFleetFavorite` overwrote `failure` for each failed outcome in a batch, so with `concurrency > 1` only the last reason reached the Lead. It now collects every failure in the batch: `2 member(s) failed; 0 sibling(s) started: "one": …; "two": …`. `reports every failed member of one launch batch, not only the last` pins it with two pre-collided names, and the pre-existing `2 sibling(s) started` assertion still holds, so mixed success/failure keeps its meaning. That a batch size actually limits parallel admission is now observed rather than assumed: `admits no more members at once than the fleet concurrency allows` holds each `spawnTeammate` at the seam behind a test-owned promise for a 4-member fleet at `concurrency: 2`, asserts only `m1`/`m2` started while that batch is blocked, then releases it and reads `peak === 2` with four real roster members at the end. `launchFleetFavorite` is the only launcher (`rg` for it finds one client call site, one Remote wrapper, and nothing else), and `isFleetEnabled`/`fleetConcurrency` are read only there, so no path launches a disabled fleet or ignores the batch size.
- **Measured.** `pnpm exec vitest run --no-file-parallelism --coverage…packages/subagent/agent-team packages/client/ui-agent-team` → 14 files, **252 passed, 1 skipped**, `packages/subagent/agent-team/src` and `packages/client/ui-agent-team/src/client` each 100/100/100/100 including the new Remote wrappers, `FleetSettingsSection.tsx` and `mount.ts`. `tsc -b tsconfig.host.json`/`tsconfig.client.json` → 0; `pnpm run lint` → 0/0 (after `oxlint --fix` corrected indentation in the re-indented favorites map, and `@stylistic(arrow-parens)` on the new release callbacks); `verify-client-ui-i18n` → 0 over 523 locale-owned files; the four catalog generators + `verify-generated-artifacts` → 6 current; `pnpm run test:docs` → 12 passed, `doc-sync` → 30 passed; `pnpm run build` → 0. `pnpm exec vitest run --config vitest.web.config.ts apps/web/tests/settings-chrome.e2e.ts` → **11 passed**, so no Settings golden moved. One local timing fact is recorded rather than absorbed: `worktrees.spec.ts > removes the checkout and branch` needed 3262 ms alone against vitest's 5000 ms default and crossed it when 14 files ran in sequence, so the git-spawning lane in that file has roughly a third of its budget as headroom on this host. Starter fleets listed Development / Research / QA with their member lines; Install produced the notice `Starter installed as an editable fleet.` and one saved row; Duplicate produced `Fleet duplicated.` and `Development (copy)` under id `development-copy`.
