### 2026-11-05 — S8-19: the modes collapse into one dropdown in the hero's workspace row

- **What shipped.** `ModeSwitcher.tsx` is a native `<select aria-label={t('switcherLabel')}>` with one `<option>` per registered mode, the staged mode's glyph before it and its one-line description beside it, linked through `aria-describedby` (React `useId`) so an elided caption is still announced. `ConversationRoot.tsx` renders that slot inside `heroWorkspaceRow` immediately after `WorkspaceChip` (`data-testid="hero-mode"` on the wrapper span) and the centered `heroModeRow` under the input card is gone, with `.heroModeRow` replaced by `.heroModeSlot { display: inline-flex; align-items: center; min-width: 0 }` so the control can shrink with the row. `ModeSwitcher.module.css` keeps the pill vocabulary (`.row`, `.pill`, `.pillActive`, `.pillIcon`, `.pillLabel`, the horizontal-scroll rules, the reduced-motion block) out and states one reason the caption elides rather than wrapping. Selection still runs through the same store: `AgentPresetSeat`'s `choose`, `useAgentPresetSeat`, and the refusal `Toast` are untouched, so no second mode state exists.
- **Two behaviours the change surfaced, both fixed rather than absorbed.** A controlled `<select>` whose value matches no option displays the first option, which would have shown `Standard mode` to a session staged on a retired preset — so an unlisted `current` now renders one hidden disabled option carrying its own id, and `components.client.spec.tsx` asserts `select().value === 'ptc'` while the option list still excludes PTC. `disabledReason` and the `switcherComingSoon` key in all three dictionaries had no producer and no consumer, so both are deleted rather than kept as dead copy; `disabled` stays because an `<option>` genuinely renders it.
- **Consumers moved together.** `components.client.spec.tsx`'s mode describe became dropdown-shaped (value instead of `aria-checked`, `getAllByRole('option')` order, `fireEvent.change` instead of pill clicks, one glyph instead of one per pill, and a new case proving the caption stays on the staged mode while a switch is in flight — the control is not self-committing). `apps/web/tests/agent-preset-selection.e2e.ts` selects by option label (`selectOption({ label })`), reads the staged mode through `inputValue()`, reads option labels and disabled flags in one `evaluateAll`, and captures one region instead of two. `pnpm run doc-sync` and `verify-generated-artifacts` needed no catalog change; `docs/product-inventory.md` and the package README now describe a dropdown instead of pills, and the README states the hidden-option rule.
- **Measured.** `pnpm exec vitest run --no-file-parallelism … packages/client/ui-agent-preset packages/client/ui-conversation` with per-file thresholds on `ModeSwitcher.tsx` → **36 files, 504 passed**, `ModeSwitcher.tsx` 100/100/100/100. Web lane with `GNK_SNAPSHOT=refresh` rewrote exactly three goldens (`apps/web/tests/expected/agent-preset-selection/hero.expected.md`, `snapshots/web/lifecycle-chrome/{hero,plan-active}.expected.md`); every hunk was read before accepting — `radiogroup "Agent mode"` with `[checked]` radios becomes `combobox "Agent mode"` with `[selected]` options in the same order, and in the two lifecycle goldens the block moves to sit directly after the workspace chip and before the composer textbox, which is the adjacency this item asked for; no timing or path churn appeared. Two consecutive `GNK_SNAPSHOT=replay` runs → **15 passed / 15 passed**, so the new goldens are stable. `tsc -b tsconfig.host.json` and `tsconfig.client.json` → 0; `pnpm run lint` → 0/0; `verify-client-ui-i18n` → 0; `verify-generated-artifacts` → 6 current; `doc-sync` → 30 passed; `build` → 0.
- **Real app (fresh build, port 8131, started and stopped by this check; the pre-existing port-8099 dev server untouched).** The control is a native `<select>` reported to accessibility as `combobox "Agent mode"`, living in the same `display: flex` row as the workspace chip with measured geometry `chip x345-476`, `select x502-677`, `caption x683-1069`, all at `y=156` — one line, immediately after the chip. No mode name exists anywhere as a button, radio, or tab any more, and the only element under the input card is `hero-recent-row`. All six modes are present in order: `Standard mode`, `Army mode`, `Maestro mode`, `Pixels Designer (preview)`, `Instructor (preview)`, `Creator mode`. Selecting Maestro kept the value, swapped the caption to the Maestro sentence, raised no banner, and left model, access mode, and CTC untouched. Zero console messages. Harmony and Melody appear nowhere in this dropdown; they stayed on the Fleet page, whose availability rows now carry name + label only (`fork — Harmony and Melody`, `spawn — Harmony and Melody`), which also confirms the `detail` removal in the running app.
- **What the app pass could not prove, and where the proof actually comes from.** The IDE's browser view reported `visible=false`, so clicks, real key presses, viewport resizes, and screenshots were unavailable; keyboard selection and the 420px/1280px render were therefore **not** verified by eye. They are verified by the Playwright lane instead, which drives a real browser headfully: `offers the mode dropdown…`, `lists every mode as one option each…`, `applies the staged pick to the blank session, and the host honors it` (staged value reaches `livePreset`), `says why a switch was refused…`, and `re-reads the slash catalog through the composition the switch installed` all pass, so selection updating the existing mode state is real-browser evidence. A human still opening the popup visually is the one uncovered sliver; it is recorded here rather than claimed.

- **Three defects fixed in the page.** (1) `FleetProviderAvailability.detail` was host-authored English prose rendered by `<small>` on every availability row — a locale-owned-copy violation I introduced in `2d033ff1` and reworded in `8d4d89c1` without noticing; `rg` proved it had exactly one consumer (that line) and two producers, and its content restated the localized label, so the field, both strings, and the render are gone and `packages/extensions/tool-cordis/src/api-catalog.ts` regenerated to the facts-only interface. (2) "Add member" built its own provider default (`providers.find(p => p.state === 'ready')?.name ?? 'spawn'`) instead of the `defaultProvider` the "New fleet" path uses, so a fleet over one-shot-only providers silently pinned `spawn`; both paths now call `defaultProvider`, and a new test adds a member under laneless providers and asserts both selects land on `claude-code`. (3) Editing a stored fleet's **id** appended a second row and orphaned the first, because `submit` matched the new id against `favorites`; the draft now remembers the id it was opened from and replaces that row, pinned by a test asserting the saved array is exactly `['review-renamed']` with the original member.

Real-app verification of these three rows runs with the S8-19 app pass on the same fresh build rather than being claimed now.

Both are collected as **S8-21** with the RPC-surface prerequisite written out, so no later session reads the page as complete.

### 2026-11-04 — Fleet Settings speaks Harmony and Melody, and the labels come from the provider's own capability

- **What changed, and what deliberately did not.** A usable provider row now names the execution systems it supports — `Harmony and Melody` for a provider with a continuable start, `Melody only` for a registered provider without one — and any other row keeps stating why it is not usable (`Not installed`, `Unauthenticated`, `Temporarily unavailable`), so status and modes never repeat each other. The same label appears in the member editor's Provider option text, and a sentence under the heading defines both names and says they are execution modes, not providers. `FleetProviderState`, `TeamMemberMode`, the persisted `mode` values, `spawn_teammate`, `fleetProposals`, and every stored fleet are untouched, so no migration or compatibility break exists. Keys added to all three dictionaries (`en`, `zh`, `ar`): `fleet.modes.both`, `fleet.modes.melody`, `fleet.modes.help`; `fleet.state.ready` and `fleet.state.oneShot` stay defined and mapped, and no new dead string was introduced — the initial `Harmony only` and `No execution mode available` keys were dropped after measurement showed `fleetProviderAvailability` cannot produce either combination.
- **Why Harmony-only is not modelled.** `fleetProviderAvailability` emits exactly three forms: not-installed (`oneShot: false, continuable: false`), one-shot (`true, false`), ready (`true, true`). `SubagentProvider.start` is required by the seam, so a provider that holds a lane can always also run one delegation, which makes a Harmony-only row unreachable. A `modesKey` branch for it would have been an unreachable branch failing the per-file 100% gate, so it is not written, and the JSDoc records the reasoning instead.
- **Measured.** `pnpm exec vitest run --coverage.enabled=true --coverage.include="packages/client/ui-agent-team/src/**/*.tsx"` at the 100% thresholds → exit 0, 3 files, 47 passed, `FleetSettingsSection.tsx` 100/100/100/100; the availability test was rewritten to assert both mode labels, the explanatory sentence, that no option is offered under a mode name, and that `Melody only` replaces `One-shot only` in the dropdown. `pnpm run verify-client-ui-i18n` → 0 (523 locale-owned files); `pnpm exec tsc -b tsconfig.client.json` → 0; `pnpm run lint` → 0 warnings 0 errors; `pnpm run doc-sync` → 30 passed; `pnpm run build` → 0; `pnpm exec vitest run --config vitest.web.config.ts apps/web/tests/settings-chrome.e2e.ts` → 11 passed, so no keyless golden moved.
- **Real app, read-only.** A fresh build served on port 8125 with the OpenCode row supplied through the web profile layer rendered `fork — Harmony and Melody`, `opencode — Melody only` with `provider runs one delegation to a result and holds no durable Team lane`, and `spawn — Harmony and Melody`; the Provider dropdown read `fork — Harmony and Melody`, `opencode — Melody only`, `spawn — Harmony and Melody`; `One-shot only` and `Ready` no longer appear as visible text anywhere in the section, and the console had zero messages. The layer was then restored to its original `[]` and only the 8125 server was stopped. One honest observation from the same pass: availability is carried by the rendered label plus `data-state`, with no separate visual status chip, so a row's state is text-first by design.
- **Scope boundary, stated so it is not mistaken for completion.** The Lead-visible settlement sentence and the model-facing tool descriptions still use "one-shot" and "continuable lane"; they are S8-18c, not part of this row. The grep behind that split: `one-shot teammate` appears in `mailbox.ts` plus two test files and in no golden or snapshot, so S8-18c is contained work, while the Fleet Settings surface this row shipped is complete and verified.

### 2026-11-04 — The Lead can name a teammate's provider, and the real app dispatched an OpenCode member whose settlement reached the Lead

- **The selection surface.** `spawn_teammate` and every `spawn_fleet` member take an optional `provider`. An omitted value keeps the configured `freshProvider`/`forkProvider` default byte-for-byte, a named value must be registered in this composition, and an unregistered one is refused with `TEAM_PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` naming what the Team can dispatch on — refused in the tool, before the roster would spend a reserved name on a child that cannot start. The Web fleet member editor now offers a `one-shot` provider as a member (previously only `Ready` rows were selectable) while an unregistered provider stays disabled, and a new member defaults to a `ready` provider, then a selectable one, then `spawn`. No capability was added anywhere: a provider without a continuable start still means no mailbox, no follow-up, no interruption, no write scopes.
- **Package evidence.** `pnpm exec vitest run --coverage.enabled=true --coverage.include="packages/client/ui-agent-team/src/**/*.tsx" --coverage.include="packages/subagent/tool-agent-team/src/**/*.ts"` with the 100% thresholds → `tool-agent-team/src/index.ts` and `FleetSettingsSection.tsx` at 100/100/100/100, 73 passed; `pnpm run typecheck` → 0; `pnpm run lint` → 0 warnings 0 errors; `pnpm run gen-tool-catalog` → `docs/tool-catalog.md` +7 lines carrying the new argument's description. Five new tests cover: explicit provider reaching the request, the two defaults staying, the unregistered refusal creating no member, a mixed fleet approval card echoing `native — provider=spawn` and `coder — provider=fork`, and the three `defaultProvider` branches.
- **Real app evidence, stated exactly.** With the OpenCode row supplied through `~/.gnk/profiles/web/cordis.patch.yml` (restored to `[]` afterwards; server on port 8124 started and stopped, the pre-existing 8099 server untouched) and a fresh build, the Lead passed `"provider": "opencode"`, the member was created as `provider: "opencode"`, `status: "inactive"` with no `model` key, and the Lead received exactly one peer item: `Team message team-message-fb20b18f-… from coder: one-shot teammate "coder" settled with stop reason "error" on provider "opencode". provider diagnostic: Subagent failure (provider: ACP; stage: prompt; category: transport) The run produced no output.` So dispatch, settlement, and the single report all work in the shipped UI; the run itself failed at the ACP prompt stage because the child inherited the session workspace, where OpenCode uses its own default model — the route my package-level suite pins to `groq/openai/gpt-oss-20b` through a project `opencode.jsonc`. That is external model configuration on this host, the same class already recorded for OpenCode's `google/*` route, and it is why no successful external answer *through the UI* is claimed.
- **Three defects the probe exposed, recorded rather than absorbed.** (1) The roster shows the Lead's `Model: openrouter/free` for a member whose provider is external, which reads as a false route. (2) Opening that member's row reports `sessions.selectSubagent: … is not a healthy catalog child`, so the raw external failure is invisible in the UI while it is durable on the member row. (3) After the first spawn the Lead re-sent the same call and got `TEAM_MEMBER_NAME_TAKEN`, then called `send_message` on the one-shot member and got `TEAM_MEMBER_NOT_FOUND` — both correct per the contract, and the tool result text does not tell the model what to do instead.
- **One measurement worth keeping.** `tool-agent-team/src/lane-guard.ts:124` reports 98.66% statements / 98.48% branches under a narrow `--coverage.include` for that package; the same numbers appear with this increment's changes removed, so it is not caused here, and `pnpm run test:coverage` across the repository stays the CI gate.

### 2026-11-04 — A real OpenCode member settles inside a Team beside a native lane and reports its answer to the Lead

- **What shipped.** `packages/subagent/agent-team/tests/opencode-acp-fleet.e2e.ts` (three cases) on top of the existing production path — no parallel orchestration, no second mailbox, no provider stub. The composition mounts the real `@greeneek/gnk-subagent-acp` plugin with `command` pointed at the installed OpenCode executable and `args: ['acp']`, the real `spawn` provider as the continuable lane, and a second ACP row whose command is `process.execPath -e 'process.exit(3)'` to fail at the genuine process boundary. `agent-team` gained `@greeneek/gnk-subagent-acp`, `@greeneek/gnk-subprocess`, and `@greeneek/gnk-subprocess-local` devDependencies because the test mounts and observes them; the package README and `docs/subsystems/agent-team.md` name the suite and its skip condition.
- **The real run, and its exact evidence.** `pnpm exec vitest run --config vitest.e2e.config.ts packages/subagent/agent-team/tests/opencode-acp-fleet.e2e.ts` → **3 passed**, 67.87s of test time. The external member settled `phase: 'completed'` with `mode: 'one-shot'`, `result.stopReason === 'completed'`, and streamed output the test pins to contain `PROBE OK` — text that crossed the ACP boundary from OpenCode's own process. One native lane member in the same Team kept `mode: 'lane'`, `phase: 'active'`, and accepted mail after the external member finished, and `startContinuable` was called exactly once, for `spawn`. Exactly one `team/message/queued` edge and one `team/message/delivered` acknowledgement exist for the member, and the Lead's recorded peer item names `coder`, carries `one-shot teammate "coder" settled with stop reason "completed"`, and contains the real answer.
- **Two production facts this test corrected in me, not in the code.** A provider whose `start()` rejects is recorded as a durable `failed` row **and** re-thrown to the caller (`roster.ts:515-536`), so the sibling case must expect the rejection; and such a member never published a run, so it sends no mailbox report at all — my first version asserted a report for the crashing sibling and failed. The fix followed the shipped contract instead of changing it: the surviving member's result and single report are asserted intact alongside `crashed.phase === 'failed'` carrying `provider: ACP` and `exit code: 3`.
- **Windows spawn truth the suite now encodes.** The npm shim `opencode.cmd` is not spawnable by a subprocess, so `resolveOpenCode()` looks for `opencode.exe` under the npm global prefix, then `~/.opencode/bin`, then `/usr/local/bin`, then each PATH entry, with `GNK_OPENCODE_COMMAND` as an absolute override, and the suite skips with a printed reason when nothing resolves. The child workspace pins OpenCode's model with a project-level `opencode.jsonc` (`groq/openai/gpt-oss-20b`), so no credential is read, forwarded, or printed: the provider row passes `env: {}` on top of the credential-scrubbed parent environment.
- **Cancellation and reaping are observed on the real child.** `ctx.subprocess.spawn` is spied call-through to collect `SubprocessHandle`s; every handle's `done` resolved with an exit code or signal, so no OpenCode process outlived the settled member, and the Lead-side abort settled `stopReason: 'aborted'`.
- **Gates measured for this increment.** Coverage at the repository's 100% per-file thresholds: `pnpm exec vitest run --no-file-parallelism --coverage.enabled=true --coverage.include="packages/subagent/agent-team/src/**/*.ts" --coverage.thresholds.{lines,functions,branches,statements}=100 packages/subagent/agent-team` → exit 0, 11 files, **187 passed, 1 skipped**. Maestro could not dispatch it: `spawn_teammate` exposes no provider argument — `packages/subagent/tool-agent-team/src/index.ts:349-389` fixes `freshProvider: 'spawn'` and `forkProvider: 'fork'` per tool row — and the fleet editor's provider dropdown lists only `Ready` rows, so a one-shot provider cannot become a member there either. Asked in chat to delegate the second teammate to `opencode`, the Lead created two native members; the `PROBE OK` the Lead then reported came from a native `spawn` child answering the task text itself (its teammate conversation shows one turn on `openrouter/free`, no ACP child), so no mixed-fleet UI claim is made. The native sibling that failed showed `400: {"message":"Reasoning is mandatory for this endpoint and cannot be disabled."}` from the free router — a model-route fact of the same class as S0-18, unrelated to the fleet path.

Until that ships, the mixed fleet is proven at the service boundary and in the real external e2e, and is unreachable from the shipped UI.

### 2026-11-04 — The OpenCode and KiloCode overlays are tracked, and the static config gate names the reason they had to move

- **Root cause of the earlier miss, measured rather than guessed.** `.gitignore` line 2 is the global `*.cordis.yml` rule, which is why `git add` staged nothing in `apps/cli/config/examples/external-coding-agents/`. The repository already carries a pattern that the rule cannot match: `apps/cli/config/examples/schedule/cordis.yml`, `…/github-review/cordis.yml`, and `…/cordis/cordis.yml` are tracked because a file named exactly `cordis.yml` does not match `*.cordis.yml` (the pattern needs a literal dot before `cordis`). The three `mcp-memory/*.cordis.yml` files are tracked only because they were force-added, which is why `git check-ignore -v --no-index` names `.gitignore:2` for `engram.cordis.yml` while `git check-ignore -v` stays silent about it: a tracked path is never reported as ignored. The example directory therefore keeps its per-example-directory-and-`cordis.yml` naming, and no force-add is needed for a future contributor to modify it.
- **Relocated into tracked paths.** `apps/cli/config/examples/opencode-acp/cordis.yml` and `apps/cli/config/examples/kilocode-acp/cordis.yml`, with the same rows and the same capability prose as the host-local drafts; the `external-coding-agents/` directory is gone. Evidence: plain `git add apps/cli/config/examples` staged both files and `git ls-files apps/cli/config/examples` lists them, so they are in the index without any `-f`.
- **The static gate explains a second requirement, and it is a real runtime requirement.** `scripts/verify-cordis-config.ts` classifies `apps/cli/config/examples/**/*.yml` as app overlays, meaning the plugin each row names must resolve from the `gnk` app's own dependency surface — the same rule that puts `@greeneek/gnk-subagent-acp`'s sibling `@greeneek/gnk-mcp-client` in `apps/cli` dependencies for the memory examples. Adding the row's package to `devDependencies` left the violation standing because the app-overlay check reads `dependencies` only, so `@greeneek/gnk-subagent-acp` is now a production dependency of `apps/cli` (`pnpm install` re-linked it; the resolution probe `createRequire('file:///…/apps/cli/index.js').resolve('@greeneek/gnk-subagent-acp/package.json')` prints `packages\subagent\subagent-acp\package.json`).
- **Verified through the built binary, beyond a config dump.** `node apps/cli/lib/bin.js --profile headless --patch apps/cli/config/examples/opencode-acp/cordis.yml --patch apps/cli/config/examples/kilocode-acp/cordis.yml --dump-config` exits 0 printing `subagent-opencode` (`providerName: opencode`, `command: opencode`) and `subagent-kilocode` (`providerName: kilocode`, `command: kilo`) beside the native `spawn` and `fork` providers. The same overlays on a real keyless run — `node apps/cli/lib/bin.js --profile headless --patch … --patch … "reply with exactly the word OK"` — exited 0 and printed `OK`, so the Loader mounted both ACP rows in the shipped app rather than merely carrying them in the tree. No external CLI was delegated to in that run, so it is mounting proof, not integration proof.
- **One `hygiene` failure on this host was residue from an earlier retirement, and it is cleared.** `pnpm run constraints` reported `packages/bundle/agent-team-web-profile: expected a package here (no package.json found)` because the S0-08 retirement left an untracked directory holding only `lib/` and a stale `node_modules/` link tree (0 tracked files). `pnpm run clean` — the repository's own tool for "build outputs and safe residue from deleted packages" — removed 275 paths and `pnpm run build` returned exit 0, so `constraints` passes. `agent-team-web-profile` is gone from the working tree; nothing in it was tracked.
- **One `verify-cordis-config` violation remains on this host and is not a repository defect.** It reports `apps\cli\tests\profiles\acp\cordis.yml: root must be a Loader entry array`. That path is a tracked symlink (`git ls-files -s` → mode `120000`, the only one under `apps/cli/tests`) and this checkout has `core.symlinks=false`, so the working tree holds the link's text (`../../../../../snapshots/acp/escalation-approved/cordis.yml`) instead of the file; the target's own root is a Loader entry array (`- id: llm-greeneek`). After the dependency fix this is the gate's only complaint, and the two example files are absent from it. `git config` was not changed to force the point.

### 2026-11-04 — One-shot results reach the Lead as ordinary mailbox items, queued before the terminal edge

- **Delivery path.** `TeamRoster`'s constructor takes a `MemberReportSink`, and `TeamService` wires it to `TeamMailbox.reportSettledMember`, which appends one `team/message/queued` item addressed to the Lead — sender id and name are the settled member's — and returns an **unstarted** dispatch closure. `settleOneShotCompleted` runs, inside one root transaction, report-append first and then the member's `active -> completed` edge; only after that transaction commits does the roster call the closure, so delivery (`root.steer` plus its own `markDelivered` transaction) never begins while the settlement still holds the Lead's Session flush. No parallel queue was added and no live caller membership is fabricated: the report is an ordinary mailbox item, so `recoverFor`, the per-target serialisation, and the `team/message/delivered` acknowledgement apply unchanged.
- **Report content.** Each report names the teammate, its provider, and the recorded `stopReason`; adds `provider diagnostic:` when the run carried one; adds `roster recorded:` when the roster ended the run (rejection text, the timeout budget message, or nothing on a clean finish); then the final assistant output blocks. Because the identity is `team-message-<member id>`, the projection's existing `queued twice` refusal makes a second report for one member impossible, and the target-side receipt check keeps a retried delivery from steering the Lead again. Output that cannot fit `maxMessageBytes` is withheld **by name** — the report says so and the complete blocks stay on the member's durable row — instead of failing the settlement or dropping the result silently.
- **Seven new cases** in `packages/subagent/agent-team/tests/team.spec.ts`: success delivered exactly once (asserting the Lead's own `agent/inbox/spliced` receipt, its text, `pendingMessages` empty, and one queued plus one delivered edge); failure delivered with `stopReason "error"` and the provider's message; budget expiry and caller abort reported as distinct outcomes; an undeliverable report staying queued and retrying to one receipt; a refused roster write leaving the report queued and the row `active` with the warning naming the member, then `recoverFor` delivering it once; two members reporting independently with a sibling's failure keeping the other's report intact; and oversized output withheld by name while the row keeps it whole. The mixed-fleet case now also asserts the Lead received the one-shot result while the lane member stayed live.
- **Two ordering facts found the hard way, not assumed.** With dispatch admitted inside the transaction, the Lead's steered turn competed with the still-open settlement on the same Session flush and six one-shot tests stopped reaching `completed`; returning an unstarted closure fixed the ordering rather than the timing. And settlement now performs two flushed appends, so a single `advanceTimersByTimeAsync` no longer drains them: the fake-timer cases pump the clock while yielding to `process.nextTick`, which keeps the assertion about durable state deterministic. A combined multi-package run once hit the known Windows `git worktree` contention (5000ms timeout, `worktrees.spec.ts`); the identical command re-run passed, and that flake is recorded separately from this change.
- **Evidence.** `tsc -b tsconfig.host.json` and `tsconfig.client.json` → exit 0. `vitest run packages/subagent/agent-team packages/client/ui-agent-team` with `--coverage.include=src/{roster,mailbox,projection,types,index,fleet-settings,fleet-config}.ts` and 100% thresholds on all four metrics → **14 files, 231 passed, 1 skipped, no uncovered locations**. `oxlint` on the changed files → 0 warnings, 0 errors. `pnpm run doc-sync` → **30 gates passed, 0 failed**. `verify-generated-artifacts` → all 6 current; the generated docs changed only in line drift, and `README.md` plus `docs/subsystems/agent-team.md` state the report contract. Deliberately unchanged: `tool-agent-team`'s model-facing description, because editing it would require re-recording snapshot fixtures this host cannot verify (the acp tool-schema lane is blocked by `core.symlinks=false`).
- **What is now true, and what still is not.** A Lead can consume a finished one-shot member through the normal orchestration flow: the result arrives as a peer item and `wait_agent` is woken by the same roster and mailbox edges as any other change. Still not done: the real external CLI adapters (`subagent-claude-code`, `subagent-codex`) have never been run end to end here — no shipped profile mounts them and neither binary exists on this host — so mixed native + CLI remains unproven against a real adapter, and S8-03a's remaining scheduler fields, S8-05a, S8-06a, S8-16a, and the Fleet UI work are untouched.

### 2026-11-03 — Fleet admission and provider availability now follow the capability model, and one mixed fleet launches for real

- **The continuable-only assumption is gone from the fleet layer.** `TeamService` derives two separate sets instead of one: `admittedProviders()` is every provider the composition registers and feeds `RepoFleetConfigStore`'s `resolveAllowedProviders`, and `continuableProviders()` is the `prepareContinuable` subset used for availability rows and lane-only shapes. No provider name is matched anywhere in fleet code. `fleet-config.ts`'s refusal text no longer claims a durable-lane requirement; it names registration and `fleetProviders` exclusion.
- **`unsupported-version` was the wrong label and is removed.** `FleetProviderState` is now `'not-installed' | 'one-shot' | 'unauthenticated' | 'ready' | 'temporarily-unavailable'`, and `FleetProviderAvailability` carries `oneShot` beside `continuable` so availability, one-shot execution support, and continuation/durable-recovery support are three distinct facts instead of one boolean. `ready` means a durable lane; `one-shot` means registered and runnable as one delegation that settles with a result. Every consumer moved: `FleetSettingsSection.tsx`'s `STATE_KEYS` map (`satisfies Record<FleetProviderState, TeamKey>` makes the compiler refuse an incomplete mapping), the three locale dictionaries (`fleet.state.unsupported` → `fleet.state.oneShot`: 'One-shot only' / 仅一次性执行 / فقط یک‌بار اجرا), the client spec fixtures, `docs/subsystems/agent-team.md`, the package README, and `Config.fleetProviders`' JSDoc.
- **Launch admission is per member, per shape.** `assertFavoriteProviderReady` refuses a provider the composition does not register (still without substituting another) and admits a one-shot provider otherwise; it additionally refuses a member whose non-empty `writeScopes` need a lane that provider cannot hold, which is the same rule the roster enforces at spawn. `readOnly` and `maxTokens` members launch on either shape.
- **Proof.** `packages/subagent/agent-team/tests/team.spec.ts` → `launches one fleet that mixes a continuable lane with a one-shot CLI-style member`: one saved favorite with a `spawn` member and a `ext-cli` one-shot member launches `{ ok: true }`, `startContinuable` is called exactly once, the rows report `mode: 'lane'` `active` and `mode: 'one-shot'` `completed` with `result` equal to `{ stopReason: 'completed', output: content('cli answer') }`, the run is disposed once, the settled member reports `inactive` while the lane member still accepts mail, and messaging the settled one-shot is refused. `packages/subagent/agent-team/tests/fleet-settings.spec.ts` adds `admits a one-shot provider and refuses only the lane it cannot hold`, covering plain and `readOnly` admission, the `writeScopes: ['src']` refusal, and an empty scope list staying admissible.
- **Evidence.** `tsc -b tsconfig.host.json` and `tsconfig.client.json` → exit 0. `vitest run packages/subagent/agent-team` with `--coverage.include=src/{index,fleet-settings,fleet-config,roster,projection,types}.ts` and 100% thresholds on all four metrics → **11 files, 180 passed, 1 skipped, no uncovered locations**. `vitest run packages/client/ui-agent-team` → 44 passed. `pnpm run lint` → 0 warnings, 0 errors. `pnpm run test:docs` → 12 gates passed. `verify-generated-artifacts` → all 6 current; the regenerated diffs are the two catalog line-drifts plus `FleetProviderAvailability.oneShot`, the `FleetProviderState` union, and the `fleetAvailability` `@returns` line in `packages/extensions/tool-cordis/src/api-catalog.ts`.
- **What this does NOT deliver.** A settled one-shot result is durable on the member row but still reaches neither the Lead's model nor a `TeamMemberView` field, so the Lead cannot read a finished CLI member's output — that is the next unit, and its shape is decided: a new `TeamMailbox` entry point that queues the member's report as an ordinary `team/message/queued` item addressed to the Lead and dispatches it through the existing `tryDispatch` path, called from a settlement sink the roster receives at construction, because `sendAdmitted` cannot carry it (it requires a live caller membership and resolves an *active* target, while the member is terminal). No `gnk web` mixed-fleet run has been performed yet: the package-level mixed test is the current proof, the shipped web profile still mounts only continuable providers, and this host has no `claude` or `codex` binary.

### 2026-11-03 — S0-22 executed: two stale-row defects fixed, the remaining failures have named causes

- **`apps/cli/tests/built-bin.e2e.ts` had two stale expectations, both corrected to the shipped contract.** The standalone `sdk-minimal` dump still listed `['llm-greeneek', ...]` while `packages/bundle/sdk-minimal/cordis.patch.yml:37` mounts `llm-pi-ai` — the same row its own `packages/bundle/sdk-minimal/tests/sdk-minimal.spec.ts:32` expects. The `composes the profile user layer and a --patch overlay in order` case asserted that a ghost row in the **profile's own** layer exits 0 with a labelled warning, which is the pre-S0-07 behavior; the case now keeps the ordering assertions on a valid layer and proves both halves of the accepted rule: a ghost in the profile layer exits 1 naming the remedy, and a ghost in a `--patch` overlay still exits 0 with its own skip line. Evidence: `vitest run --config vitest.e2e.config.ts apps/cli/tests/built-bin.e2e.ts -t "config dump"` → **4 passed, 18 skipped**.
- **The remaining `built-bin` failures are provider-mount and credential causes, not composition staleness.** `runs a mock-backed ACP turn` fails with `prompt variable "{{model}}" has no value for this assembly (section "deployment:persona")` and `uses the launching endpoint and managed credential` fails with an empty stdout (`expected '' to be 'launching endpoint reached the mock'`), because both stream `provider: greeneek-official` against a composition that no longer mounts the native adapter — the scenario must insert its own provider row, the shape `apps/cli/tests/profiles/headless/tests/fixtures/greeneek-defaults.patch.yml` already uses, and the temporary profile must resolve it. `runs the headless profile through its app-owned task positional` fails with `no model is configured`, which is S0-18's open keyless-router question. **S0-22 stays open for these three.**
- **The Windows snapshot class has a proven cause.** Five tracked files under `snapshots/` are git **symlinks** (`git ls-files -s` mode `120000`): `snapshots/acp/{escalation-approved,image-compaction}/{tool-schemas.expected.json,system-prompt.expected.md}` and `snapshots/session/agent-instructions/workspace/{,nested/}AGENTS.md`. On this host `core.symlinks=false` materializes them as plain text holding the target path, so `JSON.parse` receives `"../../session/text-turn/tool-schemas.expected.json"` — the exact `SyntaxError: Unexpected token '.', "../../sess"...` the acp lane reported, and why the same lane is green on Linux CI. The repository side is correct; this host must enable symlink checkout (`core.symlinks=true` with Windows Developer Mode, then re-check-out those paths), which is the owner's machine configuration and not something I changed. The `sdk` lane's `initialize timed out after 10000ms` failure reproduces on CI as well, so it stays a genuine Phase 0 item rather than a host artifact.
- **Correcting my own earlier claim.** The 2026-11-03 row said thirteen `apps/cli/tests/profiles/headless/**` overlays "need the same audit". The audit is done: those files reach the run as `--patch` layers, and `apps/cli/src/profile-boot.ts:200` marks overlay layers `strict: false` on purpose, so their dead `- id: llm-greeneek / disabled: true` rows produce a labelled skip line and never refuse a launch. They are stale intent worth removing in a dedicated cleanup, not a gate failure, and this row no longer describes them as one.

### 2026-11-03 — S8-03c roster half shipped with its lifecycle tests; mixed fleets still not claimed

- **Production code.** `packages/subagent/agent-team/src/roster.ts` chooses the execution model from the registered provider's capability instead of a provider name: `getProvider(provider)?.prepareContinuable !== undefined` is a `lane` member started through `startContinuable`, and any other registered provider is a `one-shot` member started through `start`. The durable `provisioning` row is still appended first, so an unknown provider keeps producing a durable `failed` member that reserves its name and counts against `maxMembers`; `trackOneShot` then watches the published run without holding the Lead's tool call open, and `settleOneShotCompleted` appends the single `active → completed` edge. `types.ts` adds `TeamMemberMode`, `TeamMemberResult`, the `completed` phase and `SpawnTeammateRequest.timeoutMs`; `projection.ts` carries the schema fields and the transition law that allows `completed` only when the prior row was an active one-shot, so a lane cannot take the edge and a settled row cannot be rewritten. `list()` reports a settled member as `inactive` (the `TeamMemberView.status` union is unchanged, which is why `tool-agent-team`'s member schema and the Web `TeamAction.tsx` rows needed no edit), `resolveActiveMember` refuses mailbox and interrupt with `TEAM_MEMBER_NOT_INTERACTIVE`, and `spawnAdmitted` refuses `timeoutMs` on a continuable spawn and `writeScopes` on a one-shot spawn. No `lane-lifecycle.ts` change and no new session event: `subagent/start`/`subagent/end` already publish the run.
- **Timeout reaches the provider, and disposal happens once.** The budget timer aborts the member's own `AbortController`, which the composed start signal (`request.signal` + lifecycle + budget) carries into the provider, and `awaitOneShot` is the only disposer. The stalled-run test asserts `provider.signal()?.aborted` **and** one disposition; the race tests prove one terminal edge and one dispose whether the result or the timer wins, and that a rejection arriving after expiry is observed and dropped rather than re-settling the row.
- **Review findings fixed before the push, not after it.** Two defects in the first draft (the timer disposing the run *and* the `finally` disposing again; a timeout that only abandoned the local wait) were already corrected. This pass removed a `failure` initializer in `reconcileProvisioning` whose value no path could observe, moved the budget-controller and budget-diagnostic cleanup into a `finally` so a settlement-append failure cannot leak a member's controller, replaced an unreachable `.catch(() => undefined)` on the one-shot watcher with the `finally` the operation actually needs, added the missing warning for a result that could not be appended (pinned by a test that refuses the durable append), and dropped the redundant `String()` conversion. Every `TeamMemberPhase` consumer was inspected: `tryMembership`, the `list()` mapping, `resolveActiveMember`, `reconcileProvisioning`, `settleProvisioning`, and the projection law.
- **Evidence.** `pnpm exec tsc -b tsconfig.host.json` → exit 0; `pnpm exec tsc -b tsconfig.client.json` → exit 0. `pnpm exec vitest run packages/subagent/agent-team` with `--coverage.include="packages/subagent/agent-team/src/{roster,projection,types,fleet-settings,integration,lane-support}.ts"` and 100% thresholds on all four metrics → **11 files, 178 passed, 1 skipped, no uncovered locations**. The new cases are `One-shot members` (**14 tests**) in `tests/team.spec.ts` and `permits the completed edge only for a one-shot member` in `tests/projection-events.spec.ts`, which also rejects the edge for `mode: 'lane'`, for a row with no mode, and for a repeat of a settled one-shot. `pnpm run lint` → 0 warnings, 0 errors. `pnpm run doc-sync` → **30 gates passed, 0 failed**. `pnpm run verify-generated-artifacts` → all 6 current; `verify-cordis-inspect-catalog` → up to date. Regenerated: `docs/config-catalog.md`, `docs/persistence-catalog.md`, `docs/subsystems/agent-team.md` (the `TeamMemberSnapshot` block and its lifecycle prose), `packages/extensions/tool-cordis/src/api-catalog.ts` (`timeoutMs`), plus `docs/event-producer-consumer.md` line drift.
- **Existing assertions were not weakened.** `tests/team.spec.ts` still requires the unknown-provider durable `failed` row, the fleet concurrency/isolation rows, and the lane recovery cases unchanged; the only expectation moved is the one the roster now owns (a one-shot member refuses interactive operations), and the legacy-compatibility case proves a row recorded without `mode` keeps lane semantics.
- **Pre-existing gates that were red on `main`, fixed in their own commit.** `verify-cordis-inspect-catalog` failed because `ILayout.openSettings`/`selectSettingsSection` (from `5dde9feb`) lacked `@param section`. `verify-export-jsdoc` reported 14 violations: two were mine from `a5b7a1a1` (`isFleetEnabled`, `fleetConcurrency`), the rest sat in `craft-local/src/launch.ts`, `craft/src/launch-template.ts`, the `webworker-runtime` `pi-ai` stub, `agent-team/src/integration.ts` (`haltReason`), and `agent-team/src/lane-support.ts`, where `hostLaneGit`'s destructured parameter is now a named `request` so `@param` binds and the body destructures. `verify-doc-graphs` was stale from `288f8368` (the `agent/pre-step` listener row for `agent-team`), and `verify-package-paths` rejected three `packages/*` references to a deleted bundle path, a deleted preset directory, and a globbed test path in this plan and `docs/product-inventory.md`. Nothing was caused by S8-03c; no rule was widened and no gate was skipped.
- **Not claimed.** Mixed native + CLI fleets do **not** work end to end yet: `launchFleetFavorite` still refuses any provider without a durable lane, `providerAvailability` still labels a one-shot provider `unsupported-version` (now a wrong label rather than a missing capability), the settled one-shot result is durable on the member row but reaches neither the Lead's model nor a roster row, and no mixed fleet has been run in `gnk web`. The real one-shot adapters are `packages/subagent/subagent-claude-code` and `subagent-codex`, both opt-in `cordis.patch.yml` layers, and neither `claude` nor `codex` is on this host's PATH, so a successful external CLI member run cannot be produced here at all — only its wiring can be tested.
- **Next step, in order.** (1) Widen fleet launch and availability to admit one-shot members with their own status text and tests, since the roster now supports what the fleet layer still refuses. (2) Deliver a settled one-shot result to the Lead (model-visible means a session-visible projection, per the repository rule). (3) Dev-mode verification in `gnk web` with `agent-team-profile` plus a CLI adapter layer mounted, recording exactly what the absent CLI and absent `GREENEEK_API_KEY` allow and forbid. (4) S8-03a's remaining scheduler fields only where an enforcement exists (`priority`, `default` fleet). (5) S8-16a migration and fleet behaviour tests.

So a one-shot lane needs no new session event (`subagent/start` / `subagent/end` already publish one-shot runs) and no SDK expectation churn, and cancellation has an existing seam. What remains is the work itself: settle a member row from `run.result`, refuse continuation/mailbox/follow-up by name for one-shot members, keep sibling results intact, and cover every new branch — the lane deliberately was not half-edited this turn, because a partial roster change cannot pass the per-file 100% gate and "if a mandatory gate fails, fix it before pushing".

### 2026-11-03 — S8-03d shipped: fleet admission is derived from the provider registry

- **Production code, not documentation.** `packages/subagent/agent-team/src/fleet-config.ts` gained a constructor that requires one admission rule (`allowedProviders` for an explicit narrowing, `resolveAllowedProviders` for a registry-derived set) and throws `TEAM_INVALID_CONFIG` when neither is supplied; `DEFAULT_FLEET_PROVIDERS = ['spawn','fork']` is gone, the refused-member message names the provider and the durable-lane reason instead of asserting an in-process limit, and the omitted-provider fallback (`DEFAULT_MEMBER_PROVIDER`) still passes admission so it cannot bypass the rule. `packages/subagent/agent-team/src/index.ts` adds `laneCapableProviders()` as the single derivation — registered providers whose descriptor carries `prepareContinuable` — used by both the fleet store and `providerAvailability()`, so the Settings page and the launcher cannot disagree. `docs/subsystems/agent-team.md` and the regenerated `docs/config-catalog.md` state the new rule.
- **A schema limit surfaced twice, and the fix was chosen from the library rather than assumed.** `z.array(z.string()).optional()` failed typecheck (`Property 'optional' does not exist on type 'Schema<string[], string[]>'`) and `.nullable()` failed the same way, so the "admit everything" state is the documented empty-list default of `fleetProviders`. The prior behaviour of rejecting `fleetProviders: []` was replaced by rejecting only a blank name, because `[]` now carries meaning; `packages/subagent/agent-team/tests/team.spec.ts` was updated to assert the accepted sentinel with the reason in a comment, which is a deliberate behaviour change with its test, not a deleted assertion.
- **Evidence.** `pnpm exec tsc -b tsconfig.host.json` → exit 0. `pnpm exec vitest run packages/subagent/agent-team packages/subagent/subagent-claude-code packages/subagent/subagent-codex` → 259 passed, 1 skipped, 3 failed. `pnpm exec vitest run …/fleet-config.spec.ts …/fleet-settings.spec.ts` with 100% per-file thresholds on `fleet-config.ts` → 16 passed. `pnpm run gen-config-catalog` → exit 0.
- **Those 3 failures are not mine, and that was tested rather than asserted.** All three are in `packages/subagent/agent-team/tests/worktrees.spec.ts` (existing-checkout refusal, checkout-and-branch removal, held-working-directory report) and they surface only when several packages run concurrently: with my changes present, `vitest run packages/subagent/agent-team/tests/worktrees.spec.ts` alone → 15 passed, 1 skipped; the same file alone at baseline also passed. The suite exercises real `git worktree` add/remove on a Windows host where a held working directory is a lock condition, so this is the known concurrency-and-host class the `gnk-ci-test-reliability` checklist covers, recorded here instead of being silenced or explained away. It is not evidence about fleet behaviour, and no assertion was weakened to hide it.
- **What this does NOT yet deliver.** Mixed fleets still cannot *launch* a one-shot CLI member, because the roster only lanes continuable children — S8-03c is the next implementation step and the ADR stays `proposed` until it lands. Fleet manager fields (S8-03a), starter templates (S8-04a), natural-language configuration (S8-05a), duplication and per-project defaults (S8-06a) are still unwritten, so no dev-mode mixed-fleet verification is claimed by this row.

### 2026-11-03 — S0-14 closed: the image 404 was the scenario's own server, and the mount crash moved the runner to the stub tier

- **The side that was wrong is the scenario's static server, not the lane's asset pipeline.** `respond()` passed the request's URL pathname through `path.normalize`, and on Windows that converts every `/` to `\`, so the override map (`requireVfsAssets`'s produced `preview/vfs-image.tar.gz` among them) could not match and the served answer was 404 → the tunnel's HTTP 503. The path is now read segment-wise with a traversal guard (`..` as a whole segment still refuses), which also removes the last use of `normalize` there.
- **The runner's own failure is accounted for separately.** Ubuntu does not run `path.normalize` into backslashes, so the runner died one stage later at mount with `envApiKeyAuth is not a function`: the worker bundle's `pi-ai` stub predates the curated `kilo` catalog's mount-time call chain (`createProvider` → `envApiKeyAuth`). The stub's `createProvider` now answers structurally and `envApiKeyAuth` resolves to unauthenticated, while every request path (`stream`, `login`) stays loud on the console, matching the existing replaced-externals philosophy and pinned by the moved/new assertions in `node-stubs.spec.ts` (43 tests in the two specs, green). Recorded in the [pi-ai stub note](../../implemented/bug-fix/2026-11-03-replaced-pi-ai-stub-answers-the-catalog-mount-path.md) and the package README's limitation rows.
- **The settle marker change removed a stale assumption, it did not bless old behavior.** The empty preview environment serves no llm adapter, so the hero settles at `routable === false` and the composer input carries the block reason as its placeholder ("This model is unavailable — select one to continue"); the old marker waited for the never-shown prompt placeholder. The downstream sweep only reads bodies the page still renders, so the blocked composer is the correct settle point.
- **Evidence.** `pnpm exec vitest run packages/experimental/webworker-runtime/tests/node/builtins-table.spec.ts packages/experimental/webworker-runtime/tests/node/node-stubs.spec.ts` → pass; `pnpm run build` → green; `pnpm exec vitest run --config vitest.web.config.ts apps/web/tests/preview-boot.e2e.ts` → 1 file, 22 tests passed, end to end, where the former output was the 404/503 chain.

### 2026-11-03 — S8-03b decided by the owner as hybrid, and its real blocker measured

- **Concurrent state reconciled first.** `git worktree list` shows one worktree (this one, on `main`); `HEAD == origin/main == e3f635bd`, whose subject is the other session's `pi-ai` mount-surface fix, and the only working-tree entries are untracked scratch files. Nothing was stashed, reset, or overwritten, and the other session's plan edits were never staged.
- **The owner answered S8-03b: hybrid.** Native modes and in-harness agents plus external CLI agents in one fleet, with `SpawnTeammateRequest` not forced onto CLI agents and native modes not dressed as processes. Recorded as a decision note at `.agents/notes/proposed/architecture/2026-11-03-hybrid-fleets-use-the-subagent-provider-seam.md` — deliberately `proposed/` with `Status: proposed`, because the decision is authorized while its code is not written, and `implemented/` with `Status: implemented` would claim work that does not exist.
- **No new abstraction, and the reason is in the source.** `docs/subsystems/subagent.md` §"The provider contract: `SubagentProvider`" plus `packages/subagent/subagent/src/index.ts:511` (`registerProvider`) already normalize capabilities, availability, lifecycle, cancellation and results, and the SPI already refuses an unsupported request with `UNSUPPORTED_CAPABILITY` instead of ignoring it. `packages/subagent/agent-team/src/index.ts:502-504` derives `continuable` from `prepareContinuable` presence. Provider names come from `config.providerName` (defaults such as `claude-code`), so the fleet layer must never match a name literally.

(2) A Team lane requires a continuable child: `roster.ts:242,247` ("one named, continuable direct child of the Team Lead"), `roster.ts:558` (`descriptor?.mode === 'continuable'` for recovery), and `tests/fleet-settings.spec.ts:27` asserting a CLI provider as `state: 'unsupported-version', continuable: false`. Mixed fleets therefore need a one-shot lane mode (S8-03c) before they run. Widening availability to `ready` first would only move the refusal to launch time, so it was not done.

It was rewritten to label each claim as read-from-source or not-yet-implemented, and the same honesty applies to S8-03a/04a/05a/06a, which remain unwritten.
- **This turn wrote no production code.** Phase 0 is still gated by `consumers`; the fleet implementation continues from S8-03d (admission derivation, checking whether any shipped bundle sets `fleetProviders` first), then S8-03c, then the manager fields with their enforcing tests.

### 2026-11-03 — first product defect fixed: the worker's builtin table was missing `node:string_decoder`

- **The pack failure was the sweep working, not failing.** `apps/web/tests/preview-boot.e2e.ts` refused to build the image because `packages/craft/craft-local/src/transport.ts` decodes stdout with `new StringDecoder('utf8')` and `packages/experimental/webworker-runtime/src/node/builtins.ts` had no entry for that specifier. The fix registers the builtin the way the existing `buffer` shim does: `builtin_modules/implemented/string_decoder.ts` backed by the maintained `string_decoder` npm package, declared in the package's dependencies, aliased for the prefixed specifier in `module-proxies.ts` (the bare name resolves to the backing package), and asserted in `builtins-table.spec.ts` for both bare/prefixed instance identity and a multi-byte sequence held until its continuation arrives.
- **Two rejections were based on fact, not preference.** `readable-stream@4` — already a dependency — does not export `StringDecoder`; a probe of the installed package showed its entry exposing stream classes only, so taking it from there would have been a guess. A hand-rolled decoder was rejected against this repository's dependency policy, because partial-sequence buffering is precisely the state an ad-hoc decoder corrupts silently.
- **Evidence.** `pnpm exec vitest run packages/experimental/webworker-runtime/tests/node/builtins-table.spec.ts packages/experimental/webworker-runtime/tests/node/node-stubs.spec.ts` → pass; the new shim holds 100% statements/branches/functions/lines under the per-file thresholds (13 tests). After `pnpm run build`, the scenario's failure moved off `sweepImage` and now sits one stage later as HTTP 503 from a 404 on `/preview/vfs-image.tar.gz`, recorded as S0-14 rather than claimed fixed.
- **Consequence the table now carries:** a builtin must exist in `builtins.ts` and, where the bare specifier does not resolve to the backing package, in `module-proxies.ts` too — the two serve different consumers, and a missing second entry yields two module instances, turning `instanceof` into a silent false.

### 2026-11-02 — my own S0-07 diagnostic broke `repository`; browser failures classified

- **The regression was mine and CI caught it.** Run 41482941545 on `f25b4939` kept `ci (22)` ✓ and `windows-shell` ✓ but turned **`repository` ✗**: 77 of 83 `snapshots/session/headless.snapshot.ts` tests failed on `expected 'gnk: …' to be ''`. Cause: S0-07's lenient-layer report defaulted to stderr, copied from `renderConfigDump`, which is a *dump* surface where notices are expected. `headless.snapshot.ts:751` asserts an empty stderr as the success signal, and the recorded `--patch` fixtures legitimately override `llm-greeneek` in a composition that mounts the snapshot provider instead — a correct lenient skip rendered as 77 failures. Fixed by taking the sink out of the boot path entirely: `checkPatchComposition(binName, layers)` refuses strict layers, tolerates lenient ones, and visibility stays on the two channels that already carry it (loader logger, `--dump-config`). A new spec pins that the path writes nothing to stderr, and the corrected contract is in `apps/cli/reference/README.md`.
- **Proof the fix lands where intended:** `pnpm run test:snapshot -- -t "bash-tool-turn"` now raises no stderr assertion at all; its only local difference is the `bash` tool-result body, because this host runs `pwsh` and the lane is POSIX-authoritative.
- **The 26 CI / 51 local browser failures classified.** Reading every failure against its assertion rather than its file name gives five groups. **Nondeterministic recording** (fixed by S0-11): the `persisted replay` diffs over `createdAt`/`dt`. **Platform-specific** (recorded, not "fixed"): `cordis-tool-round` and `ptc-round` system-prompt pins carry the Windows exit-code sentence; `navigation-panes`' bash row and `background-job-list`' `unknown tool "bash"` are the pwsh host. **Test infrastructure** (Windows-only): `hmr-live.e2e.ts` fails `spawn pnpm ENOENT`, `preview-boot.e2e.ts` on an unresolved module request in the packed worker, `pwa-manifest.e2e.ts` on manifest property count. **Legitimate snapshot drift awaiting the next canonical refresh**: the `Collapse sidebar` button in `lifecycle-chrome` (landed in `f25b4939`). **Candidate product defects, still open and not assumed to be drift**: `plugin-config` renders `120000` where the scenario's value should appear (three assertions), `onboarding-greeneek-config` paints takeover chrome on a configured reload (`['chrome','inert','chrome',…]` vs `[]`), `github-ready-review` hits a `strict mode violation` with two identical review rows, `reference-composer` orders `reference-order-target-session` ahead of `reference.txt`, and `chat-scroll-contract`/`replay-round-trip` flip booleans. These are worked next, in that order, because a green suite built on unexamined re-records is not this release's definition of done.
- **S0-11 closed at the source, not in the diff.** `zeroRecordClocks` is now the single rule and the recorder calls it on every record including the header, whose "byte-identical" promise was exactly what preserved the noise. A refresh of `steering` moves one line; scrub-of-scrub is asserted idempotent. `session-snapshot` holds 100% coverage and 274 tests.

### 2026-11-02 — S0-07 implemented: shipped patch layers must resolve

- **The box's premise needed correcting before the fix.** The loader is not silent: `vendor/include/src/index.ts:112` warns `patch: entry %C not found`. Two facts made that worth calling a silent drop — the warning is routed through `ctx.root.logger?.('loader')`, so a tree without a configured logger loses it entirely, and `apps/cli/tests/profiles/acp/tests/fixtures/image-offload.cordis.yml` reached the lane as a `--patch` overlay, where the diagnostic is easy to miss behind a failing assertion.
- **Leniency is load-bearing, so the rule is per-layer.** `packages/boot/app-boot/src/index.ts:322-334` states that one shared overlay must not be required to match every tree; refusing everywhere would break `$GNK_HOME/cordis.patch.yml` and `config/examples/`. `checkPatchComposition` therefore refuses bundle and profile layers and *reports* home/overlay skips with their file name. Verification pass B: ghost row in the profile layer → exit 1 naming the remedy; the same row as `--patch` → exit 0 plus the labeled line; clean profile still dumps.
- **A dump that boots differently would mislead.** The first live probe showed `--dump-config` bypassing `composeProfile` and still only warning, so the same rule now runs there, sharing `composeEntries` and the per-prefix attribution `renderConfigDump` already used rather than inventing a second composition model.
- **Risk was the shipped compositions themselves, and that is now a test.** `apps/cli/tests/shipped-patch-composition.spec.ts` reads every `packages/bundle` patch in each `PROFILE_TEMPLATES` order and asserts none trips the guard — all five templates pass, so no existing profile's boot is refused by the new rule. `profile.ts` holds 100% coverage on all four metrics; 169 tests pass across `packages/boot` and the two apps/cli specs; `test:docs` 12 gates.

### 2026-11-02 — run 41363578257 read: `ci (22)` fails on a teardown race, not a test

- **All 18,269 tests passed on the runner and the job still failed** with `Errors 1 error`: `ReferenceError: window is not defined`, thrown from `Timeout._onTimeout` in `@tanstack/virtual-core` → `Virtualizer.maybeNotify` → `Object.onChange` in `@tanstack/react-virtual` → React's `dispatchReducerAction`, attributed by vitest to `packages/client/ui-trajectory/tests/table.client.spec.tsx`. The virtualizer debounces its scrolling-reset notification onto a timer in the observed window; `cleanup()` destroys the tree with that timer pending, and vitest tears the jsdom environment down before it fires. Fixed in `afterEach` by waiting past the library's `isScrollingResetDelay` default after unmount. `pnpm exec vitest run packages/client/ui-trajectory` → 9 files, 134 tests passed, 15.44s.
- **The first CI run of the new re-record workflow (`41363598505`) failed inside its refresh step**, which is the useful outcome: it separates fixture drift from real breakage. The same scenarios that fail in replay also time out in refresh on basic locators — `locator.click` on `getByRole('button', { name: 'Collapse sidebar', exact: true })`, `locator.waitFor` on `[role="treeitem"]`, and two `locator.ariaSnapshot` timeouts. A refresh cannot fix a missing element, so those are defects to diagnose against the running app, not goldens to re-record.
- **Next action:** reproduce the `Collapse sidebar` and session-tree selectors in the live app at `http://127.0.0.1:8099`, fix what is actually broken, then re-record only the drift-only scenarios through the workflow.

### 2026-11-01 — Browser suite root-caused to a missing binary; Agent Teams layer duplication scheduled

- **The 80-file `Browser snapshot suite` failure is not product behavior.** Read from the runner log: `Error: browserType.launch: Executable doesn't exist at /home/runner/.cache/ms-playwright/chromium_headless_shell-1228/…`, with Playwright's own hint `pnpm exec playwright install`. `pnpm install` fetches the driver, never the browser, and **no workflow in this repository installs it** — `test:web:built` ran for the first time in the `consumers` job this release cycle, so every scenario in every file failed at launch. The cascade also explains the secondary symptoms: with no page, no prompt is driven, so `assertReplaySession` finds no live root session matching each fixture. Fixed by adding a `pnpm exec playwright install --with-deps chromium` step before the lane and pinning its position in `scripts/ci-workflow.spec.ts` (23 tests pass).
- **Two genuine defects surfaced under it, both reproduced on this host and scheduled rather than papered over.** S0-08: the private Agent Teams layers still `insert` `agent-team` and `ui-agent-team`, which `base` and `web-app` now own, so a profile that follows those packages' own READMEs refuses to load — reproduced through `apps/web/tests/agent-team-panel.e2e.ts`. The fix spans two bundles, their two `profile.spec.ts` files, the overlay that mirrors them, two READMEs, and three generated catalogs, so it is its own slice. Unrelated to the browser cause and still open: `apps/web/tests/minimal-preset.snapshot.ts` mounts `agentPresets.mount(agentCtx, 'minimal')` and the roster answers `preset "minimal" not found (available: standard, ptc, pixels-designer, cordis, instructor, army, maestro)` — no `minimal` preset exists anywhere in the tree, so the scenario's fixture preset was removed without retiring the scenario.
- **Local evidence for the browser lane.** `CI=true GNK_SNAPSHOT=replay pnpm run test:web:built` on this host: 13 failing files, of which `approval-composer` and the shell-name cases are Windows `bash`/`pwsh` drift (`Error: unknown tool "bash"` where the fixture recorded a Linux run) — the ubuntu runner is authoritative for those. `minimal-preset` and `agent-team-panel` fail identically on both hosts, which is why they are recorded as defects rather than platform noise.
- **Commits.** `ee3f8ce7` (CTC parity test + CTC-03 ticks), `993bbac8` (focus race, preset roster), and this row's workflow pin. All pushed to `main`.
- **Next action:** read the `consumers` job on the new SHA — with Chromium installed, the browser lane should report only the real defects; then land S0-08 and the `minimal` preset retirement.


### 2026-11-01 — run 41346043550 read: one focus race in `ci (22)`, one stale roster, browser suite cause named

- **`consumers` → `Browser snapshot suite` cause read from the runner log**: `assertReplaySession` (`apps/web/tests/scaffold.ts:886`) fails with "Web replay fixture …/snapshots/web/<scenario>/session.jsonl must match one live root session: expected [] to have a length of 1 but got +0" across many scenarios. ### 2026-11-09 — Phase 0 close-out evidence for S0-09, S0-15, S0-22, S0-25 (local only; no boxes ticked)

- **S0-25 (Windows expectation mismatches) — FIXED + verified locally.** Root cause: two test expectations encoded POSIX-path semantics on a Windows host. `scripts/build-exe-for-python-sdk.spec.ts` expected the unquoted `C:\Program Files\nodejs\node.exe` where the builder quotes space-containing paths; `packages/util/home-paths/tests/legacy-compat.spec.ts` expected `/legacy-home`/`/gnk-home` verbatim where the host resolves them to `D:\legacy-home`/`D:\gnk-home`. Fix: a `quoted()` helper in the exe spec (`JSON.stringify` when the part contains a space, matching the builder's `[dry-run]` output) and `resolve()` in the legacy-compat spec for platform-native resolution; production code untouched — the builder's quoting and the resolver's `resolve()` are the intended behavior, the tests were wrong. Evidence: reproduce before the fix → 4 failed; after the fix `pnpm exec vitest run scripts/build-exe-for-python-sdk.spec.ts packages/util/home-paths/tests/legacy-compat.spec.ts` → **2 files / 9 tests passed**; `pnpm exec tsc -b packages/util/home-paths/tsconfig.json` → exit 0; oxlint on both files → exit 0. The S0-25 row's (a) 26 load-timeouts and (b) `EPERM` teardown are not addressed by this fix and remain for the runner/POSIX lanes.
- **S0-22 (dead `llm-greeneek` overlays) — FIXED + verified locally.** Removed `- id: llm-greeneek` disable/config blocks in 12 files under `apps/cli/tests/profiles/headless/` (goal, retry, semantic-checkpoint, subagent-diagnostic/inheritance/settlement, team, workspace-context-resume, credentials, cli.patch, pi-ai-defaults, real-model full config block); the base bundle no longer mounts the adapter, so these blocks only printed a labelled skip line. Preserved legit insert rows: `apps/cli/tests/profiles/headless/tests/fixtures/greeneek-defaults.patch.yml:12`, `apps/cli/tests/profiles/acp/tests/fixtures/control-surface/cordis.yml:3`, `apps/web/tests/scenario-greeneek-adapter.overlay.yml:7`, `apps/web/tests/shipped-greeneek-adapter.overlay.yml:8`. Evidence: grep `- id: llm-greeneek` under `apps/` → only the four legit inserts plus one `llm-greeneek-keyless` credential id; YAML parses clean (expected `!!js` warnings only); `pnpm exec vitest run --config vitest.e2e.config.ts apps/cli/tests/agent-team-headless.e2e.ts` → **1 passed**.
- **S0-09 (note supersession pass) — ARCHIVED + verified locally.** Classified per `gnk-archive-agent-notes`: `2026-08-10-minimal-preset-owns-rl-composition.md` is fully superseded by `5d9e76fe` ("remove Minimal mode") — the composition now survives only as the lane-owned narrow test fixture and the still-shipped `sdk-minimal` bundle — so it moved to `.agents/notes/archived/bug-fix/` with the sole permitted change (inserted `Archived: 2026-11-09` below `Status: implemented`). The three notes still stating a shipped `minimal` preset were fact-updated in place and kept active (partial supersession): `2026-08-11-minimal-profiles-bare-two-tool-runtime.md`, `2026-08-11-pwsh-persistent-pty.md`, `2026-07-29-persistent-bash-str-replace-editor.md`, with inbound links retargeted to the archived path. Single-file archive: the bilingual workflow is retired (`notes/README.md` — no `.zh.md` counterpart is required), no sidecar exists, and the frozen manifest is a pre-retirement seal — `verify-archived-agent-notes` is not a script in this tree, so no hash is appended. ### 2026-11-11 — Session header utilities, pinned Sessions, and the Host open-in picker (owner reference: DeepSeek harness session header)

- **What landed.** A vertical slice that the previous session left half-wired is now complete and tested: `packages/util/native-command/src/external-app.ts` (external application catalogue, per-call availability, shell-free launch, file-manager reveal), the `session.externalApps` / `session.openPath` remotes on `SessionController`, a registry-global `pinnedSessionIds` set in the Workspace domain (a defaulted field; the unit format version does not move, because the single-unit JSON backend rejects a stamp it does not accept) with the `workspace.pinSession` command and the `pinned` follow increment, and the browser surfaces: the Session header cluster (split "open in" button, pin, archive, overflow menu, rightbar toggle) plus the sidebar row menu and the Workspace row "Open in…" submenu. Agent Note: `../implemented/feature/2026-11-11-session-header-utilities-and-pinned-sessions.md`.
- **Defects found and fixed in the inherited work.** The inherited `SessionController.externalApps` / `openPath` called themselves (infinite recursion) and shadowed their own private seams; the inherited edit had dropped the `@Remote('rename')` decorator from the Session rename remote; the inherited `session-controller` types used `NodeJS.Platform`, which no browser program can name (replaced by the `HostPlatform` union in `packages/api/session-controller/src/types.ts`); the inherited client shortcut matcher could never match a `mod` specification outside macOS (`packages/client/ui-primitives/src/shortcuts.ts` now separates the command key from the physical Control key); the three inherited brand icons carried hardcoded hex palettes (now monochrome, per the icon contract); the inherited header stylesheet used 1px solid neutral borders (now the 0.5px hairline the elevation contract requires).
- **Live desktop boot found one more defect.** The first inherited schema bump (`workspace` domain version 3) made the app refuse to start for existing users: the single-unit JSON backend rejects a stamp it does not accept, and `compatibleVersions` is honoured only by the per-record layout. The pin set is a defaulted field, not a format change, so the domain keeps version 2 and existing media stays readable. Reproduced on the real desktop app (`GNK entry failed: … unit 'workspace': stored version 2 != expected 3`), fixed in `packages/workspace/workspace/src/spec.ts`, and the app now boots.
- **Evidence.** `pnpm exec tsc -b tsconfig.host.json` → exit 0; `pnpm exec tsc -b tsconfig.client.json` → exit 0; `pnpm run test:gui` → **319 files / 4428 tests passed, 1 skipped**; `pnpm run test:docs` (14 doc-quick gates) → **14 passed, 0 failed**; `pnpm run gen-cordis-catalog` → regenerated with the five new type links (`SessionOpenPathRequest`, `SessionOpenPathValue`, `SessionExternalApp`, `SessionExternalAppsValue`, `WorkspacePinSessionRequest`, `WorkspacePinSessionValue`), and `packages/typert/generator/tests/cordis-catalog.spec.ts` → **3 passed**; oxlint over `packages/client/ui-workspace`, `packages/client/ui-primitives`, `packages/util/native-command` → exit 0. New suites: `packages/util/native-command/tests/external-app.spec.ts` (32), `packages/api/session-controller/tests/session-open-path.host.spec.ts` (10), `packages/client/ui-workspace/tests/session-header-utilities.client.spec.tsx` (50), `packages/client/ui-primitives/tests/shortcuts.client.spec.ts` (26). Per-file coverage is 100% on `external-app.ts`, `shortcuts.ts`, `openInMenu.tsx`, `SessionHeaderUtilities.tsx`, `WorkspaceBrowser.tsx`, and `Rows.tsx`. A full `pnpm test` run under CPU contention showed failures in files that pass in isolation; the two that reproduce in isolation (`packages/core/agent-loop/tests/resume.spec.ts > a pending idle inject() survives persist + resume without a synthetic turn` and `packages/shell/tool-pwsh-persistent/tests/loader-composition.spec.ts > preserves cwd and environment across calls`) are the two failures already recorded as the baseline in this ledger.
- **Live desktop verification (2026-11-11) found three defects that only the running app exposes.** (1) `packages/client/ui-workspace/src/client/index.ts` declared `remote` but not `remote.session` in its `inject` list, so every open-in read threw `cannot get property "remote.session" without inject` and the catalogue stayed empty; the list now names `remote.session`. (2) `apps/desktop` resolved the CLI through pnpm's injected workspace copy — a snapshot from install time — so a development launch served pre-change code; `resolveGnkEntry` now prefers the workspace build (`<repo>/apps/cli/lib/bin.js`) when a checkout sits beside the app and falls back to the staged CLI in a package. (3) `packages/api/remotes` inlines every package's generated remote contribution, so a new `@Remote` method needs that bundle rebuilt before the browser can call it. The header cluster was also slimmed to one place per verb (the overflow menu keeps only rename, fork, copy id, and delete; the visible pin and archive buttons and the open-in split own the rest).
- **The project's git facts (the reference menu's branch and change rows) are now real.** `packages/util/native-command/src/git-facts.ts` reports the checked-out branch, the base branch, and the change size through read-only git commands; `SessionController.gitFacts` exposes them; the Workspace row menu names them when the project is a repository. A directory outside a repository shows no git rows. **Commit & push, a branch picker, and "Add project script" are deliberately not built**: committing and pushing write to the user's repository, and a project script needs a concept the product does not have yet.
- **Evidence.** `pnpm exec tsc -b tsconfig.host.json` → exit 0; `pnpm exec tsc -b tsconfig.client.json` → exit 0; `pnpm run test:gui` → **319 files / 4428 tests passed, 1 skipped**; `pnpm run test:docs` (14 doc-quick gates) → **14 passed, 0 failed**; `pnpm run gen-cordis-catalog` → regenerated with the new type links, and `packages/typert/generator/tests/cordis-catalog.spec.ts` → **3 passed**; `pnpm --filter @greeneek/desktop run test` → **109 passed**; oxlint over the touched packages → exit 0. New suites: `packages/util/native-command/tests/external-app.spec.ts` (32), `packages/util/native-command/tests/git-facts.spec.ts` (9), `packages/api/session-controller/tests/session-open-path.host.spec.ts` (12), `packages/client/ui-workspace/tests/session-header-utilities.client.spec.tsx` (45), `packages/client/ui-primitives/tests/shortcuts.client.spec.ts` (26). Per-file coverage is 100% on `external-app.ts`, `git-facts.ts`, `shortcuts.ts`, `openInMenu.tsx`, `SessionHeaderUtilities.tsx`, `WorkspaceBrowser.tsx`, and `Rows.tsx`. The desktop app was driven with Playwright against its own service URL: the header cluster, the workspace "Open in…" submenu (File Explorer, Show in folder, Cursor, VS Code, GitHub Desktop — Android Studio is not installed on this host, so it is correctly absent), and the git rows (the probe project is not a git repository, so it shows none) were verified in the running app, with an empty console.
- **Still open.** The git-facing write rows of the owner reference (branch switching, commit & push) and a project-script concept are not built, for the reasons stated above. The Harness/provider selection surface (Fleet Settings Harness → Connection → Model → Effort → Context, S8-22/S8-23/S8-25) is in the tree and green, and remains the owner-facing "hand the job to another provider" path.

### 2026-11-11 — the desktop-v0.3.9 release body is committed and pushed

- **S7-01 done.** `apps/desktop/package.json` is `0.3.9`; `pnpm run verify-desktop-version` → `0.3.9 is current with desktop-v0.3.7` (the gate compares against the newest tag, so the new tag satisfies it).
- **The release body is now history instead of a dirty tree.** The accumulated v0.3.9 work (harness fleet surfaces, the session header utilities, the pin set, the open-in and git-facts capabilities, the release scripts, the documentation) was committed as `a2a6a607 feat(v0.3.9): the release body`, the desktop release preparation as `cef4a86d chore(desktop): stamp 0.3.9 and run the workspace build in development`, the upstream attribution allowlist as `55a8e9f0`, and the duplication repair as `7ffcf670`; all pushed to `main`.
- **Four defects the release gates caught, all fixed before the tag.** (1) The brand-residue gate refused the upstream attribution the adopted skill pack and the ported files carry — the gate's allowlist now lists those files with their reason (`scripts/rebrand/mapping.mjs`). (2) The local-scratch gate refused `.dependency-cruiser.json`, the dependency-rules gate's own configuration — it is now in the root-dotfile allowlist (`scripts/verify-no-local-scratch.ts`). (3) The duplication gate reported twenty clones across the release scripts, their specs, and two client packages — the shared readers, the temporary-workspace harness, the workflow contract, and the section measurement now live in `scripts/lib/`, the two client packages use the shared close icon, and the one remaining mirror (the tool-call derivation, which the client package rules forbid from becoming an import) carries the gate's ignore markers with that reason. `pnpm run duplication` → **0 clones**. (4) The `repository` job failed on `skill-load` because two snapshots embed the Cordis skill body verbatim and the skill had since been reworded to say its `agent-presets` directory supplies the shipped set rather than naming the four presets — both snapshots now carry the current sentence, and a word-level diff of the embedded text against the skill source leaves only JSON escaping.
- **Evidence.** `pnpm exec tsc -b tsconfig.host.json` → exit 0; `pnpm exec tsc -b tsconfig.client.json` → exit 0; `pnpm run lint` → exit 0; `pnpm run test:gui` → **319 files / 4435 tests passed, 1 skipped**; `pnpm run verify-generated-artifacts` → **all 6 generated artifacts are current**; `pnpm run doc-sync` → **31 of 32 gates pass**; the Cordis inspect catalog is the one remaining failure (packages whose client-visible type leaves have no client face yet — a generator face-model gap, not a CI gate).
- **Next action.** Wait for the `CI` workflow to be green on the release SHA, then run the S7-03 dry run against a throwaway `desktop-v0.3.9-rc.1` tag, then create the annotated `desktop-v0.3.9` tag on that green SHA (S7-05), verify the published release (S7-06), and publish the release notes (S7-07), whose text is prepared in `.artifacts/desktop-v0.3.9-notes.md`: the CTC rename and its reversibility, the PTC preset removal and its migration path, the npm baseline's unavailability on the registry, and every blocked lane by name.
- No plan checkbox ticked, no commit/push/tag from this entry.
